# Wiki audit, October 2026

Every page of the GitHub wiki (github.com/myklob/ideastockexchange/wiki, 13 pages, none edited since 2025-08-09) read against the code. Each specific promise a page makes is one row: its status, where the code does it (or refuses to), and what the code actually does. "Refused by design" means the code deliberately does the opposite and says why in a comment, test or doc. "Replaced by another mechanism" means the purpose is served a different way, named in the row.

**How far this was checked.** One auditor read each page and the code it concerns. A second pass, a skeptic per batch of four rows told to overturn anything it could not confirm, ran on part of the rows before the account hit its usage limit; it corrected 20 rows, and those corrections are applied below and marked. The rest are one auditor's reading, with file and line citations to check against. The planned sweep for mechanisms the wiki never mentions did not run.

**Two engines.** Most rows name two implementations, because the repository has two that disagree: `tools/static-site/` (the published site) and `src/core/scoring/` (the Next.js app). Where they differ, both are given.

| Status | Rows |
|---|---|
| Implemented | 114 |
| Partly implemented | 95 |
| Not implemented | 52 |
| Replaced by another mechanism | 50 |
| Refused by design | 40 |

## The owner's answers (October 2026)

The owner answered the five questions that shape most of the rewrite (Home questions 1, 2, 3, 5 and 7 below) and
asked not to be asked again at this level of detail. CLAUDE.md records them as standing direction.

- **Does volume count?** For belief strength, start from the number of truly independent reasons to agree and
  disagree, rank how valid they are against each other, and let the weight of pro against con decide. For choices
  of action, use costs and benefits across every stakeholder with a legitimate interest, counting both the quantity
  and the quality of interests met, under different assumptions about which interests are valid. Try different
  inputs and see what works. Built: `tools/static-site/tally.py`, printed beside the truth score on every belief page.
- **Do votes ever move a score?** Votes may decide what is promoted and given attention, never what is valid.
  Track what is popular apart from what stands up to analysis, and show where the two part ways.
- **Fallacy detection.** Nothing is final. Software may flag a possible fallacy; people then argue for and against
  the flag.
- **Linkage with no argument.** Start trusting and lower it when a reason is shown unrelated or invalid; a well
  argued link must rise. Keep contributors engaged; any sensible starting guess will do.
- **Evidence formula.** Prefer no upper limit, so some arguments can weigh 15 or 200 times others; ultimately use
  whatever promotes good ideas and demotes bad ones in practice.

## Questions for the owner

Design disagreements between a page and the code, which only the owner can settle. Each says what the wiki says, what the code does, and the ways to resolve it.

### Home

1. **Does volume count? The wiki's ReasonRank weighs arguments 'by quantity and quality'; the two engines disagree.** tools/static-site/score_reference.py:161-166 makes an unargued row worth exactly 0 ((2 x 0.5 - 1) = 0) and test_engines.py:43 pins that an unsourced graph reads 0.50 forever; src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:578-585 scores Pro/(Pro + Con) over summed rawImpacts, so listing an unargued pro row raises the score. CLAUDE.md names this as open decision 1. *Options:* (a) Adopt the signed rule and rewrite the wiki's 'quantity' into 'argued weight'; (b) keep A/(A + D) and remove the static site's zero-contribution rule and its test; (c) keep both and have the wiki say which engine each page's number comes from.
2. **Do votes and user feedback ever move a score?** Home and the ReasonRank page promise 'user feedback (votes, credibility scores)' refining rankings and the sample code multiplies scores by mean feedback. The static site states 'A vote never moves a score' (README.md:258-270, test_render.py:2373). The Next.js engine lets linkage votes (scoring-engine.ts:341-349, weighted average in [-1, 1]) and 60%-consensus fallacy confirmations (consensus.ts:31-34; claims.ts:97-127) change scores. *Options:* (a) Votes are display-only everywhere: strip linkage votes and consensus-driven penalties from the TS engine and rewrite the wiki; (b) votes count only through argued pages (a vote files a reason, never a weight) and the wiki says so; (c) keep the TS vote paths and document them as the exception, with the credibility multiplier [0.3, 1.4] as the safeguard.
3. **Is automated fallacy detection a promise to keep, or does the page adopt the 'accusation is an argument' model?** Home: 'Logical Fallacy Score measures the degree and impact of fallacies'; Truth-Score page: ML/regex detectors and a five-band confidence interval. integrity.py:3-17 and method.py:186 refuse pattern-matched penalties by design; src/lib/fallacy/ implements a template + consensus + validity ladder (0.95/0.75/0.45/0.25) instead, and the regex detector only drafts counter-arguments. *Options:* (a) Replace the Logical Fallacy Score section with the structured-claims model and the ladder constants, marking ML detection as proposed; (b) keep ML detection as the goal and have the structural checks and the ladder described as the current approximation; (c) remove the fallacy half of Truth Score from the overview entirely and point to /algorithms/fallacy-detection.
4. **Should computed similarity move a score (uniqueness = 1 - max similarity; equivalency blend), or only flag?** Wiki: TF-IDF uniqueness multiplied into ReasonRank, ES = w_ces x CES + w_ues x UES with vca_multiplier 0.8. Static site: DEFUNIQ = 1, w_ces = 0, similarity flags and 'changes no score and cannot' (similarity.py:39-41, method.py:130). Next.js: uniquenessFromSimilarities = 1 - max similarity is multiplied into every impactScore (propagate-belief-scores.ts:19-24; duplication-scoring.ts:355), blend 0.4/0.6/0.0. *Options:* (a) Flag-only everywhere, and the wiki's formulas are labelled proposed pending a validity comparison argument; (b) computed discount everywhere, with the static site adopting the TS blend; (c) keep the split and name it: published numbers are flag-only, the app's are discounted.
5. **What is the linkage scale and the default when nobody has argued a link?** Wiki ECLS: [0, 1], 0.0 when there are no reasons to agree. Static site: linkage page truth by the argued-truth rule, DEFLINK = 1.0 when no page (score_reference.py:34). Next.js: (A - D)/(A + D) in [-1, 1], 0.0 with no arguments (scoring-engine.ts:173-193). A default of 1 means an unexamined link transmits fully; 0 means it transmits nothing. *Options:* (a) Default 1 with the row then zeroed by the unargued-claim rule (the static site's position: the claim's 0.5 does the work, not the link); (b) default 0 so an unargued link contributes nothing, matching the wiki and TS; (c) a signed [-1, 1] scale so a link can actively contradict, as TS has.
6. **Does 'stability over time' survive, or is the score redefined as structural confidence?** Home and Confidence-Stability-Scores.md define stability by repeated evaluations, vote variance, visitors and dwell time. confidence.py:15-20 gives all seven behavioural signals weight 0 by design ('one snapshot and no user'); all-scores.ts:509-543 computes argCount/10 x (0.4 + 0.6 x dominance) with no time term; a score-event ledger exists (/api/beliefs/[id]/history) but feeds no number. *Options:* (a) Rename the section to Confidence and publish the structural weights (0.30/0.20/0.20/0.15/0.05/0.05/0.05) and bands, listing behavioural signals as proposed with weight 0; (b) keep stability-over-time as the goal and derive it from the score-event ledger and changes.py, which already record movement; (c) describe both numbers (static confidence, TS stability) and say which page shows which.
7. **Which EVS formula is the wiki's: the unbounded sum, the bounded prior, or the log2 variant?** Wiki: EVS = ESIW x ECRS x ERQ x (ERP/100), summed per belief. Static site computes exactly that for display only and scores p0 = 0.5 + 0.5 x ESIW x (2 x ERP/100 - 1) with w = k x 2 x ERQ/(ERQ + 1), bounded [0.05, 0.95] (evidence.py:140-158, method.py:96). Next.js: ESIW x log2(ERQ + 1) x ECRS x ERP over four tiers T1 1.0 / T2 0.75 / T3 0.5 / T4 0.25 (scoring-engine.ts:110-137). Also unresolved: the rank-2 band split (RCT 0.85 / meta 0.80 or the reverse) and the added 'record' tier at 0.90. *Options:* (a) Wiki keeps its sum as the displayed Overall EVS and adds the prior as the scoring rule, with the 18-tier table; (b) wiki adopts the TS four-tier log2 form and the static site conforms; (c) keep the wiki's sum as the scoring rule and change both engines (this reopens the padding problem the static README argues against).
8. **What is the Belief Impact Score? Define it or retire the name.** Home promises a BIS combining argument strength with CBA. The code has net expected value = sum(magnitude x truth) with low/high bands (render_site.py:251-262,723-727), calculateExpectedValue (scoring-engine.ts:734-736), Decision Leverage, and a 'beliefImpactWeight' that is log10(reach) on books. Three different things could claim the name. *Options:* (a) BIS = net expected value of a belief's costs and benefits, as the static site computes; (b) BIS = the books' reach weight, renamed so it does not collide; (c) drop BIS from the overview and describe the CBA readout under its own name.
9. **Is a 'resolution likelihood' score wanted, and from which inputs?** Home promises proposals scored on resolution likelihood with CBA trade-offs and compromise requirements. The static site has ease = max(compromise truth) x (1 - mean obstacle truth) (render_site.py:245-247) and says nothing yet tests which disagreements a small likelihood change would flip (2247-2249); the TS pipeline has compromise candidates with requiredShift <= 0.15 (conflict-resolution.ts:255-291) and never reaches the published site. *Options:* (a) Publish ease-of-resolution as the score and port compromise candidates to the static site; (b) define resolution likelihood as the TS compromise-candidate test and leave 'ease' as a readout; (c) drop the promise and describe the four conflict-resolution outputs that exist.
10. **Which engine does the wiki describe?** The page reads as one system; the repository has src/core/scoring/ (Next.js, counts volume, votes move linkage, fallacy ladder, computed uniqueness), tools/static-site/ (published, signed rule, votes never score, no fallacy scoring, flag-only similarity, evidence prior, sensitivity, ReasonRank walk, conformance contract) and src/lib/ise-pages/ (unwired page/edge model). CLAUDE.md says 'Adding a sixth is the wrong move' and that leaving both indefinitely is the outcome that costs. *Options:* (a) The wiki describes the published static-site engine and its conformance contract, and marks TS-only behaviours as the app's; (b) the wiki describes the TS engine and the static site is brought to it; (c) a per-mechanism table on Home saying which engine runs which rule, until one is retired.

### ReasonRank:-Google's-PageRank-for-Arguments

11. **Which mechanism should carry the name ReasonRank on this page: the signed row rule, the dependence walk, or the TypeScript damped blend?** The wiki describes one algorithm that both scores arguments and propagates like PageRank. The code has three under the name and they answer different questions. tools/static-site/reasonrank.py:1-45 says the PageRank analogue 'is not a score of a claim at all' but 'how much depends on it', and pins that the walk ignores truth and confidence (test_engines.py:249-296); score_reference.py:161-174 is the scoring rule; src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:13, 486 blends 0.15 x own truth + 0.85 x sub-argument net and calls that ReasonRank. The method page already says 'Two different things live under that name in the wiki'. *Options:* (a) Make this page the dependence walk (reasonrank.py) and move the scoring rule to Truth-Score / ReasonRank.md; (b) keep the page as the scoring recursion and present the walk as a section titled 'what the PageRank part actually computes'; (c) describe all three with their real formulas and label the TS blend as the protocol/CBA engine's variant.
12. **Should ReasonRank count the quantity of reasons, as the page says, or only argued quality, as the static site enforces?** The page says 'weigh arguments by quantity and quality'. score_reference.py:12-14, 163-165 makes an unargued row contribute exactly 0 and test_argument_alone_can_never_leave_the_neutral_point pins it; scoring-engine.ts:538-566 scores ProRank/(ProRank+ConRank) so listing an unargued reason raises the score. CLAUDE.md records this as open decision 1 ('The two engines disagree about volume'). *Options:* (a) State the static site's rule (volume never counts) and mark the TS engine as diverging; (b) state the TS rule and change the static site, which breaks the pinned test and the conformance fixture; (c) keep both, labelled, until the engines are consolidated.
13. **Does user feedback (votes, credibility) belong in ReasonRank at all?** The page promises votes and credibility 'refine rankings over time'. The static site refuses it as a standing rule: intake.py:17 'A vote never moves a score', confidence.py BEHAVIOURAL weight 0, render_site.py:2520 'Nobody's votes or views are counted here'. The TS protocol engine lets reputation-weighted linkage votes set one edge's linkage when no debate exists (scoring-engine.ts:215-228, 341-349). *Options:* (a) Strike the promise and state the rule: votes are shown beside the analysis and never move a score; (b) narrow it to what exists: votes can set a linkage multiplier in the protocol engine only, with reputation weights, behind an argued debate; (c) keep it as a proposed future mechanism, marked proposed, with the owner's reason for wanting it.
14. **Should the sentiment enhancement and TF-IDF uniqueness stay on the page as proposals, or be withdrawn?** Sentiment: no engine multiplies by it; method.py:186 refuses scores moved by text matching, CLAUDE.md refuses model-asserted numbers, and spaCy's doc.sentiment is 0.0 by default so the step is inert. Uniqueness: the sample's 1 - max TF-IDF weight does not measure duplication; similarity.py computes IDF-weighted Jaccard + 4-gram cosine and gives it weight 0 (line 261-269), while duplication-scoring.ts (1 - max similarity, threshold 0.85) does move the product's scores via propagate-belief-scores.ts. *Options:* (a) Withdraw the sentiment step and replace the uniqueness sample with the real similarity.py and duplication-scoring.ts rules, saying which one moves a score; (b) keep both samples verbatim under a 'proposed, not implemented' heading with the refusal reasons beside them; (c) decide whether the computed similarity should move static-site scores (raise w_ces above 0), which is itself an engine change.
15. **Which version of this page is canonical: the live wiki (Aug 2025) or the docs/wiki copy (PBworks 'Operating System for Truth', March 2026)?** docs/wiki/ReasonRank-Google's-PageRank-for-Arguments.md is a different document with different formulas (V x L x I x U; Base + sum Pro - sum Con; tiers 1.0/0.8/0.1/0.0; U duplicate 0.05) and tools/static-site/method.py:194 already cites it as 'wiki: ReasonRank' for a rule the site does not run. The live page has none of those numbers. *Options:* (a) Rewrite the live page and overwrite docs/wiki with it so the method page's citations resolve; (b) merge both into one page that keeps every table from each, each labelled with its status; (c) keep the PBworks text as a separate 'Operating System for Truth' page and cross-link.
16. **Should the convergence rule be stated as 'until residual < 1e-12, capped at 2000' or as the page's 'default 100 iterations'?** reasonrank.py:50-51, 120 stops at TOLERANCE = 1e-12 with ITERATIONS = 2000 as 'a cap the walk never reaches' and publishes the residual; the TS tree recursion has no iterations; docs/ALGORITHMS.md:394 still says 100. The wiki sample has no restart term so 100 iterations of it would decay to zero. *Options:* (a) State the static site's rule and retire the 100; (b) keep 100 as the ALGORITHMS.md matrix form's default, marked not run; (c) fix the sample to a true PageRank step and keep 100 as its illustrative count.

### ReasonRank

17. **Should the wiki keep promising that user votes feed the Belief Score (the rank_arguments sample), or adopt the published site's rule that a vote is shown and never scored?** ReasonRank.md lines 33-53 score an argument as (pro_votes - con_votes)/total x evidence_strength. The static site states the opposite three times as a design rule: intake.py:17 "A vote never moves a score", render_site.py:1595 VOTE_NOTE, and method.py's limits section "No vote enters any number here: every score comes from the structure of the argument and from what pages cite". The Next.js engine does let votes in at one point only: aggregateLinkageVotes (scoring-engine.ts:341-352) is priority 2 in resolveLinkageScore when no LinkageDebate exists. *Options:* (1) Rewrite the page to the no-vote rule and mark the sample as the superseded 2025 proposal; (2) keep votes as a factor and change intake.py / method.py / VOTE_NOTE and the Next.js linkage priority chain to match, which is a rule change with a conformance diff; (3) keep the sample but relabel it as ranking what people think (the vote-vs-truth gap card on the home page), separate from the score.
18. **Does the Belief Score include a logical fallacy penalty, or is a fallacy only ever a scored counter-argument?** ReasonRank.md line 22 lists "Logical fallacy penalties" as a Belief Score input, and scoring-engine.ts:431-436 does implement baseTruth = truthScore x (1 - sum\|impact\|/100). But the published site refuses pattern-matched fallacies by name (render_site.py:1021-1026), fallacy-detector.ts:1-9 declares "no score is ever docked because a detector fired", and the bridge confirmedClaimsToDetectedFallacies (claims.ts:116) is called by nothing but its test, so the penalty path is dead code on the live belief page; only the confirmed counter-argument (strength 0.8 major / 0.5 minor in the linkage sub-debate) reaches a number. The validity ladder 0.95/0.75/0.45/0.25 is published on /algorithms/fallacy-detection but applied nowhere. *Options:* (1) Wire confirmedClaimsToDetectedFallacies into fetch-belief / propagation so the ladder is real, and say so on the wiki; (2) delete the ladder and the penalty branch, and document that a fallacy is an argument that damages linkage, validity or evidence quality through the sub-debate only; (3) keep both and state on the wiki that the ladder is a proposal not yet in the scoring path.
19. **Which engine's Belief Score is the wiki describing: the static site's signed total POS - NEG, or the Next.js ratio ProRank/(ProRank + ConRank)?** score_reference.py:21,210 defines belief score = POS - NEG (open-ended, a listed-but-unargued claim adds 0 because (2 x 0.5 - 1) = 0). scoring-engine.ts:581 defines truthScore = ProRank/(ProRank + ConRank) where rawImpact = RR x linkage x importance x uniqueness and a leaf's RR is its stored truthScore, so listing an unargued reason raises the score. CLAUDE.md records this as open decision 1: "the two engines disagree about volume" and "the disagreement is unresolved, not accidental". A wiki page cannot be accurate about both. *Options:* (1) Make the wiki describe the static site (engine of record for published pages) and note the Next.js ratio as the app's variant; (2) consolidate the Next.js engine onto the signed (2 x Truth - 1) rule and the evidence prior, then describe one engine; (3) describe both explicitly, with the volume disagreement named as unresolved.
20. **Should the wiki's Equivalency Score be a Belief Score factor at all, given the code uses uniqueness as the multiplier and equivalence only for merging?** ReasonRank.md line 21 lists equivalency scores as an input. score_reference.py:172-174 multiplies by uniq_id (DEFUNIQ = 1) and never by equiv_id; similarity.py says w_ces is 0 until a VCA page exists; render_site.py:975 shows Equiv only on the Similar Beliefs table as a merge candidate signal. In the Next.js engine uniquenessScore (default 1.0) is the factor and calculateBeliefEquivalencyScore (all-scores.ts:700) is a grouping aid with bands 0.90/0.70/0.45/0.20. *Options:* (1) Rewrite the list item as Uniqueness scores (DEFUNIQ = 1, argued on a uniqueness page) and move equivalency to the merging section; (2) keep equivalency as a factor and add it to _contrib, which moves every published number and needs a conformance rewrite.
21. **Does the wiki stand by its 'current best estimate of a belief's validity' purpose, or adopt the method page's 'a truth score is not a probability' disclaimer?** ReasonRank.md line 25 promises a best estimate of validity. method.py's limits section says "A truth score is not a probability. It is a share of argued weight around a starting point... Nothing here has been checked against outcomes." scoring-engine.ts:39-41 claims the PageRank probability interpretation. The three disagree on what the number means. *Options:* (1) Adopt the disclaimer on the wiki; (2) keep the estimate wording and add the calibration work (checking against outcomes) as a roadmap item; (3) distinguish the two engines' interpretations explicitly.

### Truth-Score

22. **Should automated fallacy detection ever move a score, or only draft an accusation for people to argue?** The wiki (steps 5-7) says ML detects fallacies and the results aggregate into a score. The static site refuses this by design and has a build test for the refusal (tools/static-site/integrity.py:3-11; method.py:186 'Fallacy detection is not done'; test_render.py:652). The app's detector exists but is barred from scoring by its own type shape (src/lib/agent-ingest/fallacy-detector.ts:8-9, test 'never returns numeric fields'), and runs only on agent submissions (ingest.ts:234). Three different answers across the wiki, the published site and the app. *Options:* (a) Adopt the 'an accusation is an argument' rule in the wiki and retire the ML-scoring promise, keeping ML/regex strictly as a draft generator; (b) keep the wiki's promise and change integrity.py, method.py and the build test so a detector can deduct, with a page arguing each deduction; (c) document the two surfaces as deliberately different (static site: never; app: drafts only) and say so on the page.
23. **Does a vote move a score?** The static site's standing rule is that a vote never moves a score (CLAUDE.md; votes shown as counts after the score badge; content/votes.csv). In the app, a 60% weighted vote on a fallacy claim (src/lib/consensus.ts:31-33) publishes a counter-argument at strength 0.8 or 0.5 into the linkage aggregate and propagates (src/app/api/fallacy-claims/[id]/votes/route.ts:196-214), so the vote is what moves the number. The wiki's step 6 asks for scoring by 'supporting/opposing sub-arguments', which is neither. *Options:* (a) Keep the vote as the confirmation gate in the app and state on the page that it is the one exception to the no-vote rule; (b) replace the vote with a sub-argument tree on the accusation (the accusation's own truth score, static-site style, decides whether the counter-argument publishes); (c) make the static site adopt the vote gate.
24. **Which Truth Score formula is canonical?** Three exist and none is the wiki's. App: overall = (LV + V) / 2, LV = clamp(0.01, 0.99, pro share of truth x (1 - fallacyPenalty)), with the belief page passing truthScore = \|impactScore\|/100 and no penalty (src/core/scoring/all-scores.ts:299-351; src/features/belief-analysis/data/fetch-belief.ts:381-388). Static site: truth = (POS + w x p0) / (POS + NEG + w), capped by the weakest load-bearing component, evidence as a prior, no fallacy term (tools/static-site/score_reference.py:152-159, 207-209; evidence.py:146-148). docs/ALGORITHMS.md:316-333: TS = (LV x EQ x VL) +/- CW on -1..+1 with LVS = 1 - sum(severity x weight)/Max, never implemented. CLAUDE.md lists the app-versus-static disagreement as open decision 1. *Options:* (a) Declare the static site's rule canonical and rewrite the page around it, listing the app's (LV + V)/2 as the interim product formula; (b) declare the app's rule canonical and reconcile the static site; (c) present both as the open decision with the differences tabulated, and delete the ALGORITHMS.md formula.
25. **Should a confirmed fallacy propagate to semantically equivalent arguments?** Wiki step 4 asks for auto-flagging similar arguments via semantic equivalency, and the /algorithms/fallacy-detection explainer (src/app/algorithms/fallacy-detection/page.tsx:139-140) says 'a fallacy confirmed once stays confirmed for the whole cluster'. The schema keys every FallacyClaim to one argumentId (prisma/schema.prisma:650) and no code copies claims or confirmations across equivalence candidates. The static site's similarity.py flags pairs and changes nothing. *Options:* (a) Implement propagation (file or confirm a claim on every argument in a confirmed equivalence group); (b) correct the explainer and the wiki to say claims are per-argument and equivalence only prevents fragmenting the debate; (c) implement it as auto-filed DRAFT claims on equivalents that still need their own vote.
26. **Which fallacies are in scope, and what are their weights?** The wiki names four; docs/ALGORITHMS.md:433-447 names ten with numeric severity weights (1.0 down to 0.3); the catalog has 14 with only minor/major, no red herring, no appeal to emotion, no contradiction, no circular reasoning (src/lib/fallacy/catalog.ts:31-200). The ladder constants 0.95/0.75/0.45/0.25, the strengths 0.8/0.5 and the credibility clamp [0.3, 1.4] are code constants with no page arguing them, which the static site's rule ('every number is a link to the page that argues it') would not permit. *Options:* (a) Make the catalog the single list, add any missing types the owner wants, and publish the ladder and credibility constants on the page as the rule; (b) keep the ALGORITHMS.md weight table and make the catalog carry a numeric weight per type; (c) state that circular reasoning and contradiction are structural checks, red herring is a linkage question, and the catalog covers everything else.

### Quantifying-Reason:-The-Power-of-Truth-Scores

27. **Should any automatic detector, regex or ML, ever move a score, or is a fallacy always and only an argument someone must win?** The page (Algorithm Outline 5, Path Forward) asks for ML detection that feeds the score. The published static site refuses this on principle and pins the refusal: tools/static-site/integrity.py:1-20, method.py:186 ('Fallacy detection is not done'), render_site.py:1017-1025, test_render.py:649-653. The Next.js app runs a six-rule regex detector (src/lib/agent-ingest/fallacy-detector.ts) whose output is a draft counter-argument that changes nothing until humans confirm it, and whose module header makes 'returns no numeric fields' the invariant. The only engine that ever scored a pattern match is retired (_archive/backend/utils/fallacyDetector.js). *Options:* (a) Keep the refusal everywhere and rewrite the page so detection drafts arguments and never scores, marking ML as abandoned by design; (b) let detectors auto-file full FallacyClaims that still need the 60% vote, so ML is an accuser with a track record like anyone else; (c) build the scoring ML detector the page promises and accept that the static site's test and method page must change.
28. **Which factor does a confirmed fallacy damage, and through which code path?** The catalog says a confirmed claim damages the factor its type names: relevance → linkage, logical-validity → the validity component, evidence-quality → evidence quality (src/lib/fallacy/catalog.ts:14-17; explainer page.tsx:74-91; ArgumentTreesSection.tsx:306-315). In the code, every confirmed claim of every type takes the same path: a published LinkageArgument of strength 0.8/0.5 lowering linkage = A/(A+D) (votes route 186-214; propagate-belief-scores.ts:481-510). The validity ladder 0.95/0.75/0.45/0.25 and its bridge confirmedClaimsToDetectedFallacies (claims.ts:100-127) are tested in tests/unit/lib/fallacy/claims.test.ts:74-107 but called by nothing in src, so scoreArgument's fallacyPenalty is always 0 for DB-backed beliefs. *Options:* (a) Wire the bridge so logical-validity claims reduce baseTruth and evidence-quality claims reduce the evidence row, as the catalog promises; (b) declare that all fallacies are linkage objections, delete the ladder and its test, and reword the catalog and explainer; (c) keep both and say on the page that the ladder is proposed.
29. **What is 'the' Truth Score composition the page should describe?** all-scores.ts:338-351 averages a logical-validity share and a verification share ((LV + VTS)/2); scoring-engine.ts:556-574, which the belief page runs, uses ProRank/(ProRank+ConRank) + 0.2 × evidence net with no fallacy term; the static site uses (POS + w × p0)/(POS + NEG + w) with an evidence prior and no fallacy term; docs/ALGORITHMS.md:320-333 gives TS = (LV × EQ × VL) ± CW on −1..+1. The page's 'two metrics integrated' matches the first in shape only. CLAUDE.md already lists the engine disagreement as open decision 1; this page cannot be rewritten truthfully without picking which formula it documents. *Options:* (a) Document scoring-engine.ts as the product's truth score and the static site's rule as the published site's, naming both; (b) make all-scores.ts's two-channel average the canonical belief-page number and migrate; (c) retire the 'two metrics' framing and describe truth as the argued balance plus evidence prior.
30. **Does a community vote move a score?** The page's whole mechanism is vote-driven. In the Next.js app a 60% weighted FallacyClaimVote publishes a counter-argument and propagates scores (votes route 210-214), and aggregateLinkageVotes (scoring-engine.ts:341-353) lets linkage votes set linkage. CLAUDE.md's standing rule for the published site is 'A vote never moves a score' (votes.csv is displayed only; confidence.py BEHAVIOURAL weight 0). The two products answer the question oppositely. *Options:* (a) Accept the split: votes resolve accusations in the app, never in the static site, and say so; (b) bring the app to the static rule by making confirmation publish an argument whose weight comes from its own sub-debate rather than from the vote; (c) bring the static site to the app's rule and let recorded votes confirm claims.
31. **Which direction and scale should the published fallacy number have, if one is published at all?** This page: high = relies on flawed reasoning. Truth-Score.md's sample: positive = sound, negative = fallacious, labels at −2/0/2. Code: logicalValidityMultiplier high (0.95) = clean, consensus agreeShare high = fallacy upheld. No per-argument roll-up exists; only per-claim shares and a never-called ladder. *Options:* (a) Publish no roll-up and show per-claim consensus shares only, as today; (b) publish the validity multiplier (high = clean) and rewrite the 'Low score / High score' paragraph to match; (c) publish a fallacy-likelihood (high = fallacious) and invert the ladder's presentation.
32. **Should red herring (and the wiki's four-item list) be the published list, or the catalog's fourteen?** The page tracks ad hominem, appeal to authority, red herring and false cause. The catalog has fourteen slugs with target factors, severities and evidence requirements, and no red herring; the old JS detector and docs/ALGORITHMS.md:437-446 list ten with different weights (e.g. Red Herring 0.3). Adding red herring is an editorial call about whether it is a relevance or a validity fallacy and whether it needs exhibits. *Options:* (a) Add a 'red-herring' catalog entry (relevance, minor, no evidence requirement) and keep the four as the page's examples of the fourteen; (b) keep the catalog as is and drop red herring from the page; (c) adopt the ALGORITHMS.md ten and their weights, which would also revive the weighted-deduction model the static site refuses.
33. **Merge the two drafts or keep both pages?** Quantifying-Reason and Truth-Score.md share the intro, the four fallacies, the outline and the path forward almost verbatim; each has sections the other lacks (listed under stale). Keeping both means every correction above lands twice, and they already disagree on the score's direction and on which Google Sites page is the Logical Fallacy Score. *Options:* (a) Fold this page's Role / Alternate Explanation sections into Truth-Score.md and redirect this title; (b) keep Truth-Score.md as the Truth Score composition page and make this one the fallacy-mechanism page, each linking the other; (c) keep both and accept the duplication.

### Evidence-Verification-Score-(EVS)

34. **Is EVS a score that ranks beliefs, or a displayed diagnostic that no score reads?** The wiki (sections 1 and 3) says EVS is 'essential for ranking beliefs based on their evidentiary strength' and that the belief's Overall EVS is the sum of its evidence's EVS. The published engine computes exactly that sum (tools/static-site/render_site.py:215-217) and prints it under every Evidence Ledger 'for comparison and read by no score' (render_site.py:776-778), because an unbounded sum 'grows with every finding listed, and a score that grows with listing is the padding this site exists to stop' (tools/static-site/method.py:96). What the engine actually uses is the prior p0/w on the finding's own page (tools/static-site/evidence.py:140-151). The TypeScript product uses a third thing, the supporting share of EVS (src/core/scoring/all-scores.ts:843-850). *Options:* (a) Ratify the engine: rewrite the page so EVS names the starting point p0 and weight w, and keep the wiki's product as a displayed 'unbounded sum, read by nothing'. (b) Keep the wiki's definition as the score and change the engine, accepting that listing findings then raises a score (the padding the engine was built to stop, and test_argument_alone_can_never_leave_the_neutral_point would need a reason to change). (c) Keep both under two names (EVS the sum, Starting point the prior) and say which one the page's title refers to.
35. **Which source-type table is canonical: the wiki's sixteen rows (plus the engine's `record` and no-source 0.0) or the product's five T-tiers?** tools/static-site/evidence.py:79-98 carries the wiki's table weight for weight with an 18th row and conformance.py ships it as the contract. src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:108-118 uses T0 0.05, T1 1.0, T2 0.75, T3 0.5, T4 0.25 with 0.5 for unknown; prisma/schema.prisma:732 defaults new evidence to 'T3'; seeds, both API routes, fetch-belief.ts:369 and grounding.ts:66 all read the T-tiers. docs/BELIEF_PAGE_RULES.md:92 says the four tiers are retired and the React ledger renders them as plain text (EvidenceSection.tsx:46-54), yet they still drive every TypeScript number. The two tables also disagree on order: the wiki puts anecdote (0.55) above news (0.30) and surveys (0.25); the T-tiers put journalism/surveys (0.5) above opinion/anecdote (0.25). *Options:* (a) Migrate the product onto the eighteen keys (an `etype` column replacing evidenceType, weights from evidence.py, a data migration for the 26 seeded T-rows) and delete EVIDENCE_TYPE_WEIGHTS. (b) Declare the T-tiers a coarse product-side rollup of the eighteen and publish the mapping (which rows are T1, T2, T3, T4) so both tables can be printed from one source. (c) Keep two tables and say so on the page, which is the current state and the one that costs.
36. **How should replication count enter: linearly (wiki), bounded 2n/(n+1) capped at 2k (static site), or log2(n+1) (TypeScript)?** The wiki's formula multiplies by ERQ, so ten replications weigh ten times one. tools/static-site/evidence.py:101-109 bounds it at REP_CAP = 2.0 so 'no pile of replications can put a claim beyond argument', and test_engines.py:88-104 pins both the bound and that six sourced objections can pull a 50-times-replicated statistic under 0.5. src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:132 uses log2(ERQ+1), unbounded but concave (10 -> 3.46). Three implementations, three answers; the ERQ=1 case is the only one where they agree. *Options:* (a) Adopt the bounded form everywhere and change calculateEVS and its test (tests/unit/core/scoring/scoring-engine.test.ts:518-527). (b) Adopt log2 in the static engine, which means a conformance diff (`python3 conformance.py --write`) and removing the 2k cap guarantee. (c) Keep the wiki's linear ERQ for the displayed EVS sum only, as the static site already does, and state that the weight that moves a score is the bounded one.
37. **Is relevance (ECRS) a fourth typed factor on the evidence row, or the linkage page?** The wiki lists ECRS as a separate component with its own value (0.8, 0.9, 0.7 in the sample). tools/static-site/evidence.py:51-55 refuses a fourth factor: relevance is the linkage page, evs() takes the row's linkage as ECRS, and the prior excludes relevance so 'the same finding cited on four pages has one truth'. The TypeScript product stores conclusionRelevance (default 0.5) and linkageScore (default 0.5) as two fields and multiplies relevance in twice: calculateEVS includes conclusionRelevance, then computeEvidenceImpactScore (scoring-engine.ts:147-149) and calculateTruthScoreBreakdown (all-scores.ts:321-323) multiply by linkageScore again. *Options:* (a) Follow the static engine: ECRS is the linkage score, drop conclusionRelevance from the TypeScript formula and schema, fix the double count. (b) Keep two distinct quantities and define on the page what distinguishes 'relevance' from 'linkage' so the product's two fields mean different things. (c) Keep conclusionRelevance only inside the displayed EVS and remove it from impact.
38. **Should a finding's source type be argued on its own page, and can votes ever bear on it?** The wiki says each category choice is 'debated in pro/con form with voting'. The engine types it (tools/static-site/ise_tables.py:292; method.py:232 says 'Here it is typed, and a wrong call is not visible as a disagreement'), and the project rule is that a vote never moves a score (intake.py:90, render_site.py:1595). Linkage, importance and uniqueness each already have an argued page whose truth is the multiplier; source type is the one factor that does not. The product's answer is a provenance job confirming an agent's tierClaim (evidence-exposure.ts:36-41), which is review, not debate. *Options:* (a) Give source type an argued page like linkage ('This finding is a published statistic'), with the tier weight taken from the winning classification, and drop the word 'voting' from the page. (b) Keep it typed and have the page say so, as the method page already does. (c) Record classification disputes as a visible flag (as tierClaim/tierVerified does in the product) without changing the weight.
39. **Ratify or reject the engine's two departures from the wiki's table: the `record` tier at 0.90 and the RCT 0.85 / meta-analysis 0.80 split order.** tools/static-site/evidence.py:61-70 adds 'record' (statute, judicial holding, agency rule, sworn filing) because the wiki has no row for a primary official record and 'calling Ex parte Garland a historical trend would be wrong', and splits the wiki's single band '0.85–0.80' in listing order while noting the opposite reading is possible. docs/BELIEF_PAGE_RULES.md:97-99 and the conformance corpus (page 6 uses record) already depend on both. Neither is in the wiki. *Options:* (a) Add `record` to the wiki table as rank 1 (tied with statistics) and state RCT 0.85, meta-analysis 0.80 explicitly. (b) Reject the addition: drop `record` (those rows fall to unclassified, 0.0) and reclassify the corpus rows that use it. (c) Reverse the split (meta 0.85, RCT 0.80), which is a conformance diff.
40. **What should a finding with no replication data or no source type default to?** The wiki gives no defaults. The static engine reads blank ERQ as 1 and blank ERP as 100 (evidence.py:131-135; conformance.py:27), so an unreplicated finding is treated as fully consistent, and a blank or unrecognised etype as ESIW 0.0 (evidence.py:99), so it starts at exactly 0.50. The product reads blank etype as 'T3' (0.5), sourceIndependenceWeight default 0.5, replicationPercentage default 1.0, and an unknown tier as 0.5 (scoring-engine.ts:117; schema.prisma:732, :746, :749). A 0.0 versus 0.5 default for an unclassified source is the difference between 'no pull' and 'half pull'. *Options:* (a) Adopt the static engine's defaults (0.0 / 1 / 100) everywhere and state them on the page. (b) Adopt the product's (0.5 tier / 1 / 1.0) and accept that an unclassified finding then moves a score. (c) Make etype mandatory at intake so the default never applies.
41. **Does the V in 'Evidence Verification Score' now mean the verification lifecycle (Verified / Unverified / Disputed / Falsified) that the product runs and the static site lacks?** The wiki page never mentions verification status, retraction or standing. The product multiplies evidence by VERIFICATION_SCORES (1.0 / 0.5 / 0.5 / 0.0, src/core/reasonrank/types.ts:179-184; fetch-belief.ts:329-343, :376), reports retraction exposure (evidence-exposure.ts) and the rules doc says 'Standing is software only' (docs/BELIEF_PAGE_RULES.md:363). The static engine has no lifecycle and every row counts in full. A reader of the wiki cannot tell which of these 'verification' refers to. *Options:* (a) Add a Standing section to the page describing the lifecycle weights and the exposure readout as product-only. (b) Add etype/erq/erp-style columns for standing to the static site's pages sheet so both engines carry it, which changes published numbers. (c) Rename the concept on the wiki so 'verification' is not overloaded (e.g. Evidence Starting Point for the prior, Standing for the lifecycle).

### Evidence-to-Conclusion-Relevance-Score

42. **What should an undebated linkage read: 0.0 (wiki), 1 (published site, ise-pages, founding workbook, SQL), 0.5 (Next.js calculateLinkageFromArguments and Evidence rows), or 0.1 (Prisma Argument.linkageScore default)?** The wiki's range text and sample code make no-reasons read 0.0, which would zero every row until someone argued its relevance. tools/static-site/score_reference.py:34 sets DEFLINK = 1 and build_pages.py:53 / method.py:44 argue the opposite in print: 'A reason placed under a conclusion is presumed relevant until a linkage page says otherwise; the burden is on the challenger.' src/lib/conclusion-score.ts:81-91 and sql/conclusion_score_process.sql:120-125 also default to 1. scoring-engine.ts:272 returns 0.5 ('maximum uncertainty'), pinned by tests/unit/core/scoring/scoring-engine.test.ts:540-542; scoreLinkageDebate (scoring-engine.ts:170-176) returns 0.0 'not assumed relevant' but nothing calls it. prisma/schema.prisma:402 defaults Argument.linkageScore to 0.1 and :758 Evidence to 0.5. README.md:275-282 notes DEFLINK is the multiplier 'most rows in this corpus actually read'. Four live defaults, none the wiki's. *Options:* (a) Adopt the published site's rule everywhere: presumed relevant at 1, burden on the challenger, and rewrite the wiki's range text to say so; (b) adopt the wiki's 0.0 or the Next.js 0.5 and accept that every unargued row in the corpus goes to zero or halves, with a conformance diff (python3 conformance.py --write) and a Prisma default change; (c) keep the defaults distinct per engine and have the wiki document each, naming which engine a reader is looking at.
43. **Is linkage the raw agree share A/(A+D) the wiki states, the share shrunk toward 0.50 by weight k that the published site computes, or the signed (A-D)/(A+D) on -1..+1 that three engines and the explainer use?** Wiki: ECLS = Σ agree / Σ all, 0..1. Published site (score_reference.py:152-155, 186-188): (POS + w x p0)/(POS + NEG + w) with p0 = 0.5, w = k = 1 for a linkage page citing nothing, so one unopposed reason reads 0.75, not 1.0; build_subpages.py:28 explains the signed wiki form as 2 x this - 1 'once enough is argued'. scoring-engine.ts:173-189 scoreLinkageDebate, conclusion-score.ts:81-91, sql/conclusion_score_process.sql:113-125 and the explainer src/app/algorithms/linkage-scores/page.tsx:356-371 use (A-D)/(A+D) on -1..+1, while the Next.js linkage page and API compute A/(A+D) (scoring-engine.ts:269-287) and print the signed formula beside it (page.tsx:92-100, route.ts:7-10). *Options:* (a) Declare the published site's shrunk share canonical, document k and the 0.50 start on the wiki, and fix the Next.js page/API labels to A/(A+D) or switch them to the same shrunk rule; (b) declare the signed -1..+1 form canonical (it lets a row that contradicts its parent carry negative linkage) and change score_reference.py, ise-pages and conformance accordingly; (c) keep 0..1 as the stored multiplier and present 2 x Link - 1 as a display-only wiki scale, as build_subpages.py:39 already does in its readout.
44. **May votes, credibility or reputation ever move a linkage score, as the wiki's step 4 and the explainer's 'weighted by each voter's established reputation' promise?** The published site's standing rule is that a vote never moves a score (tools/static-site/README.md:258-270; render_site.py:1595 VOTE_NOTE; intake.py:90); 'Bears on this: yes / no' votes are recorded in content/votes.csv with `on` set and shown with the words 'votes, not a score'. method.py:208 lists behavioural signals at weight zero. The Next.js code still carries aggregateLinkageVotes with reputation weights (scoring-engine.ts:333-349) and resolveLinkageScore's priority chain (lines 215-229), neither called from any page, and there is no LinkageVote table in prisma/schema.prisma; LinkageArgument.strength is a typed Float the schema itself calls legacy (schema.prisma:491-494). *Options:* (a) Affirm the no-vote rule, rewrite the wiki's step 4 to 'each reason's strength is its own page's argued truth', and delete aggregateLinkageVotes/LinkageVote types and the explainer sentence; (b) reinstate reputation-weighted votes as a linkage input in the Next.js app only, with the wiki saying the two sites differ; (c) keep votes as a displayed signal and let the vote-vs-analysis gap ranking (render_site.py:1727-1729) be the only place they appear, which is the current state.
45. **Should the wiki's two-factor contribution (Truth x ECLS) become the engines' Truth x Link x Imp x Uniq with sign and confidence, and should it count an unargued listed reason at all?** Wiki step 6 multiplies truth by linkage and step 7 sums. score_reference.py:160-174 contributes sign x (2 x Truth - 1) x Conf x Link x Imp x Uniq so an unargued reason (Truth 0.5) contributes exactly 0 and a refuted one counts against its own side; test_engines.py pins that argument alone cannot leave 0.50. scoring-engine.ts:489-495 and :774-785 multiply reasonRank x linkage x importance x uniqueness with \|linkage\| and A/(A+D) aggregation, so listing an unargued reason does raise the score. conclusion-score.ts:166-168 adds one structural point per listed reason before linkage applies at all. CLAUDE.md names this as open decision 1. *Options:* (a) Document the published five-factor signed rule as the formula and mark Truth x ECLS as the founding simplification it generalises; (b) pick scoring-engine.ts's A/(A+D)-with-volume rule and change the static site and conformance; (c) keep both and have the wiki state the disagreement and which site each governs.
46. **Which linkage question wording is canonical: 'would necessarily strengthen' (wiki/ALGORITHMS.md, Next.js page header) or 'would significantly strengthen' (the formula that builds every published linkage page)?** tools/static-site/build_subpages.py:38, render_site.py:159 and src/lib/ise-pages/questions.ts:55 all generate 'it would significantly strengthen the conclusion that'; build_pages.py:2-4 (a comment), docs/ALGORITHMS.md:196 and src/app/arguments/[id]/linkage/page.tsx:7-8 say 'necessarily'. The published pages' claim text is what readers argue about, so the word sets the bar a reason has to clear. *Options:* (a) Standardise on 'significantly' and fix the comment, ALGORITHMS.md and the Next.js header; (b) standardise on 'necessarily' and regenerate every linkage page's claim (changes.py will show the diff; claim text is not scored so numbers hold); (c) keep 'necessarily' as the full-strength question and 'significantly' as the degree, as the Next.js page header already frames it, and say so on the wiki.

### Equivalency-Score:

47. **Should the computed half (CES) ever carry weight in a published equivalence score, and if so by what mechanism?** The wiki says w_ces is set by a Validity Comparison Argument and the example defaults it to 0.2. The static site hard-codes `w_ces = 0.0` (similarity.py:274-282), has no VCA page kind (ise_tables.py:57), and publishes the reason: an input nobody has argued counts nothing (method.py:130). The TS engine does the reverse and gives computed layers 100% of the weight (duplication-scoring.ts:67-71) with the community layer at 0.0. Both cannot be the rule. *Options:* (a) Add a 'vca' page kind so an argued page can set w_ces, keep 0 as the unargued default, and rewrite the wiki around that; (b) drop VCA from the wiki and state that computed similarity only proposes pairs and never scores, which is what the site does today; (c) adopt the TS fixed-weight blend and give up the 'argued input' rule for this one number.
48. **Do votes belong anywhere in the user-generated equivalency score?** The wiki's UES is built from up/down votes on reasons and the sample averages 'community ratings'. Every engine refuses this: the static site shows votes and never scores them (render_site.py:1588-1595, vote.yml:2, intake NO_MOVE), and the Next.js GroupingVote changes only a merge `status` at 60% weighted consensus with quorum 3 (consensus.ts:31-33), never a similarity or truth number, with voter-supplied weights rejected (votes route line 94). *Options:* (a) Keep the standing rule and rewrite UES as the equivalence page's argued truth score, with votes displayed beside it; (b) let a 60%-consensus 'grouped' verdict (the Next.js model) count as the merge decision while still not entering the score, and document that as the only vote-driven step; (c) reinstate vote-driven UES, which reverses a rule stated in CLAUDE.md, the README, the method page and the issue template.
49. **What does an unargued equivalence read: 0.50, 0.0, or blank?** The wiki's sample returns 0 when there are no user scores. The static site's Similar Beliefs column reads UNARG = 0.50 for a row with no equivalence page (render_site.py:976), the Prisma `SimilarBelief.equivalencyScore` defaults 0.0 and renders blank under Rule 6 (SimilarBeliefsSection.tsx:17-20), and the equivalence page itself starts at 0.50 by the argued-truth rule. *Options:* (a) 0.50 everywhere, as the argued-truth starting point, with the Next.js default changed to match; (b) blank until a page exists (Rule 6), with 0.50 shown only once a page has rows; (c) 0.0 as the wiki sample has it, which the static engine would treat as 'argued false'.
50. **Which of the three equivalency formulas is canonical, and what happens to the other two?** Static site: lexical 0.5/0.5 word-gram measure with FLAG 0.45 / MERGE 0.70 for proposing, and the argued page with verdict bands 0.5 / 0.9 for deciding (similarity.py:46-50, render_site.py:1266-1268). TS product engine: synonym Jaccard, 0.85 short-circuit, 0.4/0.6 blend, bands 0.90/0.70/0.45/0.20 (all-scores.ts:699-724), plus a separate agent-ingest measure 0.7/0.3 with 0.5/0.8/0.95 (agent-ingest/similarity.ts). Dormant: `EquivalenceAnalysis` scorecard `0.40 x synonym + 0.40 x overlap(0.25/0.35/0.20/0.20) + 0.20 x argumentBalance + network - penalty`, bands 90/75/50 (equivalence/[slug]/score/route.ts:7-20), which the template page already labels 'a SOFTWARE DESIGN... The built site does not run it'. *Options:* (a) Name the static site's pair (computed proposes, argued page decides) canonical and mark the TS and scorecard formulas as proposed or retired in the wiki; (b) reconcile the TS engine onto the static measure and thresholds so the belief page and the static site agree; (c) keep all three and label each with where it runs, which is the state the page must at least describe honestly.
51. **Should an equivalence above the merge line propagate evidence and scores between the two pages, or only choose a wording?** The wiki promises 'evidence supporting one claim automatically supports others' and the Next.js explainer promises a 'score cascade' on merge (belief-equivalency/page.tsx:294-302). The static site does neither: `brief()` only lets the shorter wording stand in at >= 0.9 (render_site.py:138-143), scores rest on page and edge alone, and merging pages is an editorial act. *Options:* (a) Keep propagation out and rewrite the promise as 'a merge candidate is flagged for a human to merge'; (b) add a rule that rows of an equivalent page (>= 0.9) are read into the other page's tables, which changes published scores and needs a conformance diff; (c) propagate only the wording and the cross-link, which is (a) stated positively.
52. **Is a semantic (embedding or LLM) layer ever acceptable in a published number?** The wiki's CES is defined as semantic similarity plus a trained model. CLAUDE.md records that 'a number a model asserted is the one thing the published engine refuses to carry' and the static site runs with no model and no network; src/core/ai holds an embedding engine (all-MiniLM-L6-v2, threshold 0.75) that nothing imports and whose only consumer does not parse. The TS blend reserves a 0.6-weight 'semantic' slot that no caller ever fills. *Options:* (a) Rule it out and say so on the page, keeping lexical similarity as a reading queue; (b) allow it only as a proposer (flagging pairs) and never in a score, which needs no rule change; (c) allow it in scores once a page argues that the model tracks meaning well enough, which is the VCA question again.

### Confidence-Stability-Scores

53. **Which of the two implemented quantities is the Confidence Stability Score, and should the other be renamed or removed?** The static site's confidence (tools/static-site/confidence.py:30-48, 119-121) is a seven-component structural measure that multiplies every row's contribution (score_reference.py:172-174) and is pinned by conformance.py item 2. The Next.js engine's `calculateConfidenceStabilityScore` (src/core/scoring/all-scores.ts:509-540) is `min(1, args/10) x (0.4 + 0.6 x \|pro-con\|/(pro+con))`, is stored in `Belief.stabilityScore` (prisma/schema.prisma:193, default 0.5), is shown on the scorecard, multiplies nothing, and has no vitest pin. The Next.js page also prints a blank per-row 'Confidence' column (types.ts:240-243, ArgumentTreesSection.tsx:205-207) and a scorecard sentence claiming the static site's `sign x (2T-1) x Confidence x ...` rule (ScorecardSection.tsx:150-165) that no TS function implements. Two definitions under one name is the kind of drift CLAUDE.md says costs. *Options:* (a) Port confidence.py to TypeScript, fill the blank Confidence column from it, make it gate impact as the scorecard sentence already claims, and retire or rename the TS stabilityScore as a separate 'dominance' readout; (b) keep both but rename the TS one (it measures argument count and one-sidedness, not work done) and delete the unfulfilled Confidence column and sentence from the live page; (c) declare the TS formula canonical and change the static site, which would move every published number and the conformance contract.
54. **Do the behavioural signals stay in the definition as proposed inputs, or does the page adopt the code's rule that they carry weight zero by design?** The wiki makes votes and their ratios, duplicate submissions, evaluation responses, visitors, Google PageRank, time spent and edit frequency the primary inputs (Factors 2-8 and the Python sample). The code refuses all of them: confidence.py:15-20 and 39-40 give them weight 0 and report 'no data'; intake.py:90 and render_site.py:1590-1595 say 'a vote never moves a score'; test_render.py:2272 (TestVotesAreShownAndNeverCounted) and tests/unit/core/engagement-firewall.test.ts make it a build failure to score them. confidence.py:19-20 does leave the door open: 'Wire them up and they take weight from the structural components, which is the migration the wiki describes.' *Options:* (a) Rewrite the page around the structural rule and list the behavioural signals as 'recorded or recordable, weight zero by design', with the firewall and the votes test as the stated reason; (b) keep them as a proposed future phase with explicit weights to be assigned, which means the firewall test and the votes-never-scored rule become the gate a future change must consciously remove; (c) wire some of them in now, which changes published scores and requires deleting the firewall test and the TestVotesAreShownAndNeverCounted invariant.
55. **Does 'stability over time' remain part of the definition, and if so, from which record is it computed?** The wiki defines BSS as consistency 'over time' and step 4 as the 'standard deviation of argument scores over time'. Nothing computes a dispersion over time. The static site has one snapshot plus changes.py (truth_before/after and conf_before/after versus HEAD~1 only, returning None when git is unavailable); the TS side has the BeliefScoreEvent ledger (prisma/schema.prisma:2017-2034; scoreBefore/After, stabilityBefore/After per propagation, written by src/lib/propagate-belief-scores.ts:71-90) and an /api/beliefs/[id]/history route that returns the events raw. The only stdDev in the code (scoring-engine.ts:711-728) is across competing likelihood estimates at one moment. *Options:* (a) Drop 'over time' from the definition and describe confidence as a snapshot of work done, with changes.py as the errata sheet; (b) add an eighth component computed from the git history (static) or BeliefScoreEvent (TS), which needs a weight taken from the existing seven and a conformance diff; (c) report movement over time only (as changes.py already does) and say so on the page.
56. **Should beliefs with broad prior consensus (theft, pollution, murder) read as stable without cited evidence or two-sided argument?** The wiki says they 'show high stability because consensus has already been reached'. In the static site an unargued, uncited page has truth 0.50, grounding 0 and confidence near 0 (confidence.py:95-105; evidence.py:47-49), and test_argument_alone_can_never_leave_the_neutral_point pins that argument without evidence never moves a score; the 'norm' tier (ESIW 0.40) exists for cultural norms but must be declared on the page. In the TS engine zero arguments gives argFactor 0 and status 'fragile'. Both engines deliberately refuse to let consensus count on its own. *Options:* (a) Accept the code's rule and rewrite the sentence: a consensus belief reads as established only once someone files the norm or statistic that establishes it; (b) add a declared-consensus prior (an etype or a typed column) that sets p0 and grounding, which is a rule change with a conformance diff; (c) leave the sentence as the owner's aspiration and mark it 'not how the engine behaves'.
57. **What is the default importance of an argument whose importance nobody has argued: 0.5 or 1.0?** Home.md:30 links 'Argument Importance Score' to this page. The static site and its conformance contract use `DEFIMP = 0.5` (score_reference.py:34; conformance.py:17), so an unargued importance halves a row. The TS engine falls back to `arg.importanceScore ?? 1.0` (scoring-engine.ts:439), the CBA engine to `?? 1.0` (cba-scoring.ts:93), and the explainer says 'defaulting to 1.0 (full weight)' (importance-score/page.tsx:219), while `deriveImportanceFromBeliefScore(0)` gives 0.5 for a neutral importance sub-belief (scoring-engine.ts:807-809). The same unargued argument therefore counts twice as much on the Next.js page as on the static site. *Options:* (a) 0.5 everywhere, matching UNARG and the static contract (changes Next.js belief scores downward where importance is unset); (b) 1.0 everywhere (changes the static site, the conformance corpus and the 'listing a claim is worth nothing until argued' story); (c) keep both and state the divergence on the importance explainer and this page.
58. **Should the two band tables be harmonised, and under which labels?** Static: `BANDS = {'established': 0.75, 'developing': 0.45, 'early': 0.15}` else 'unstarted' (confidence.py:48). TS: robust >= 0.75, established >= 0.50, developing >= 0.25, else fragile (all-scores.ts:527-531). A score of 0.50 is 'established' on the Next.js page and 'developing' on the static site; the static site has no 'robust' or 'fragile', the TS side no 'early' or 'unstarted'. Both are reader-facing. *Options:* (a) Adopt the static bands in TS (one set of cut-offs, four labels); (b) adopt the TS bands in the static site (a conformance-visible change to method.html and every page's meta line); (c) keep both and have the page print both tables with their engines named.
59. **Does the Home page's 'Impact Algorithm' entry (Argument Importance Score, Belief Impact Score) belong on this page, or should it be relinked?** Home.md:30 sends readers here for Argument Importance and Belief Impact; the page contains neither. Importance is explained at /algorithms/importance-score and implemented in score_reference.py:190-193 (importance page = max Validity x Bears) and scoring-engine.ts:807; 'how much a belief depends on an argument' is sensitivity.py and decision-leverage.ts; 'real-world consequences' is the cost-benefit expected value (render_site.py:249-266, cba-scoring.ts). No code uses the acronym BIS for a belief score (docs/wiki/Media.md uses BIS for Book Importance Score). *Options:* (a) Relink Home's 'Impact Algorithm' to the importance and cost-benefit pages and keep this page about confidence alone; (b) add an importance and impact section to this page describing the three implementations and their defaults; (c) split a new 'Impact Algorithm' wiki page out of the Home bullets.
60. **Is the Python sample kept as the owner's historical proposal, or corrected to match its own prose?** The owner wrote it and the rewrite rules say every example survives. As written it is inverted (a larger standard deviation yields a larger 'stability'), divides by zero with no con sub-arguments, and prints 0.0 on its own data; and it rests on vote ratios the code refuses. Replacing it with the real formula changes what the owner said; leaving it unlabelled misleads. *Options:* (a) Keep it verbatim under a 'Proposed in 2025, not implemented' heading with the three defects noted beside it; (b) keep it and add a second, runnable sample that calls confidence.Confidence over TableCorpus so the two sit side by side; (c) correct the sample's sign and guard and still label it proposed.

### Media

61. **Should audience size (sales, views, ratings, citation counts) ever multiply a media score, and if so, in which role?** The wiki's sorting formula multiplies five popularity factors into the Impact Score. The static site refuses: Reach is 'not recorded ... nothing is multiplied by it' (render_site.py:1159), is not even a column (ise_tables.py:108-110), and Influence is argued from reasons that start at 0.50 (score_reference.py:199-201; lists page 2543-2545: 'a huge audience that already agreed changes little'). The Next app multiplies one typed reach Int into Epistemic Impact = truth x reach (media-truth.ts:142-146) and ranks /media by it (page.tsx:30-36), but never lets it touch a truth or quality score. The template's authoring rule 1 allows exactly one typed number, Reach with its source (templates/media-analysis-template.html:57). *Options:* (a) Keep the static rule: influence is argued, reach is a typed sourced number shown beside it, and nothing multiplies popularity into a score; mark the wiki formula as a retired proposal. (b) Adopt the Next rule across both engines: reach x signed truth as a ranking called Epistemic Impact, never as a score, which means adding a reach column to pages.csv. (c) Implement the wiki's normalised factors, which means defining the normalisations, the data sources and a page arguing each factor, since a number nobody argued is the one thing the published engine refuses to carry.
62. **Does a work's row count toward the belief score, or is it shown only?** The wiki ranks media as supports and opposers of a belief and the Next MediaResource carries an impactScore (default 0.5) that the belief page ranks by (MediaResourcesSection.tsx:59-71), plus a belief-scorecard 'Media truth' aggregate (ScorecardSection.tsx:93-94; all-scores.ts:605-629). The static site scores each work's row as (2 x Quality - 1) x Bears x Influence x Importance and declares 'These rows are shown, not counted: the belief score above is arguments, evidence and predictions only, because a book is a container for reasons rather than a reason' (render_site.py:956; method.py:81). *Options:* (a) Shown, not counted, in both engines, and drop or relabel the Next belief-scorecard 'Media truth' row. (b) Counted as evidence rows when a work is a primary source (a study), shown only when it is a container (a book or film), which needs a rule for the split. (c) Counted everywhere, which reintroduces padding by citation.
63. **Where is a work's truth argued: on its own page (the wiki's BTS = (Logical Soundness + Verifiability)/2) or only read from the belief pages it carries?** Both engines deliberately refuse to argue truth on the work's page: 'Is what it backs true? That is argued on the belief pages that cite it, and this page only reads their scores' (render_site.py:1357-1358); media-truth.ts:4-5 'each claim is evaluated at its own belief page, across ALL sources that make it'; template rule 6 'Craft and truth are scored on different tables and never mixed'. The template still names a 'Media Logical Validity Score' with no route (line 190). *Options:* (a) Keep the design, retire BTS, and strike the Media Logical Validity Score mention from the template. (b) Add a third argued table on the work page for the work's own reasoning (does its argument follow from its evidence), kept apart from both Quality and the carried truth, with a page explaining why it is not double-counting 'Well reasoned' under Quality. (c) Restore the wiki's BTS as a per-work truth score, which contradicts the one-claim-one-page rule.
64. **Should uniqueness apply to works (the wiki's Unique Score and BUS), and at what level?** Neither engine applies uniqueness to a media row: render_site.py:950 has no Uniq factor and the Next schema has none. Uniqueness pages exist only for argument rows (score_reference.py:172-174, DEFUNIQ = 1). The claim-ledger design arguably deduplicates at the claim level, since two works carrying the same claim link to the same belief page, but nothing says so. *Options:* (a) Declare deduplication happens at the claim level and say so on the page; no uniqueness on works. (b) Add a Uniq factor to the static media row (reading the row's uniqueness page or DEFUNIQ) so a belief page citing three works making one point does not display three equal rows. (c) Add a novelty argument table on the work page, the wiki's BUS, as a sixth yardstick on media.html.
65. **Do votes on media relevance and accuracy ever carry weight, and are some voters weighted more?** The wiki wants votes on relevance and accuracy, weighted toward people who have read, bought or reviewed the work. The static site records votes (Agree/Disagree on the heading, 'Bears on this: yes/no' on a row) and pins that 'A vote never moves a score' (intake.py:90; render_site.py:1595; README 258; test_intake.py:210; test_render.py:2269); there are no accounts to weight. The Next app has no media votes. *Options:* (a) Keep votes inert and shown; strike the credibility-weighting feature as a retired proposal. (b) Weight votes for display only (a 'read it' tick on the GitHub form), still never moving a score. (c) Build accounts and weighted votes that move scores, which reverses the standing rule and needs a page arguing it.
66. **Which Media Truth Score is canonical on the belief page: the stored genre-derived 0-1 truthScore or the signed claim-based score?** all-scores.ts:605-629 aggregateMediaScores averages MediaResource.truthScore (default 0.5, 'Derived from genreType and reliabilityTier', schema 1377-1380) weighted by genreScore onto the belief scorecard as 'Media truth' (ScorecardSection.tsx:93), while /media and the explainer use mediaTruthScore from media-truth.ts (signed -1..+1, claim-weighted, genre prior fallback) and the explainer says 'when this page and the code disagree, the code wins' (media-truth-score/page.tsx:23-24). A work can read 0.70 on the belief page and -0.40 on its own page. *Options:* (a) Make the belief scorecard read resolveMediaTruth per work and average by centrality, retiring the stored truthScore. (b) Rename the scorecard row 'Media genre prior' and keep both. (c) Drop the row, consistent with 'shown, not counted'.
67. **Sum or average: should any media number be unbounded (the wiki's BAS = sum over books)?** The wiki's BAS sums per-book products; the engines average (work_accuracy = sum(t x b)/sum(b), render_site.py:3001-3006; mediaTruthScore = sum(truth x linkage)/sum(linkage), media-truth.ts:120-130). The site keeps one unbounded number, EVS, and shows it while nothing reads it, explicitly because 'a score that grows with listing is the padding this site exists to stop' (method.py:90). *Options:* (a) Averages only; mark BAS as superseded. (b) Add an unbounded 'how much is carried' total per belief (sum of carried truth x centrality over its works), shown and read by nothing, as EVS is. (c) Restore a summed BAS as the ranking on media.html.
68. **Which kinds of work does the static site admit, and are laws, songs, images and websites among them?** The wiki lists Books, Movies, Songs, Laws, Websites, Videos, Images, Podcasts. The static site admits Book, Study, Article, Report, Film, Podcast, Video plus 'Other works' (ise_tables.py:287; render_site.py:2950-2951) and files laws under Legal Framework. The Next app has book, article, podcast, movie, song, poem, image, scientific_paper (schema 1371) and infers a genre from type (song and movie -> opinion, all-scores.ts:593-594). *Options:* (a) Add Song, Image and Website to the static TYPES_MEDIA and KINDS_OF_WORK, keep laws in Legal Framework. (b) Leave the static list as it is and let unusual works fall under 'Other works', saying so on the page. (c) Unify both engines on one type list and one inferred-genre table.
69. **What happens to src/features/books, the dormant Book Accuracy Score lineage?** book-scoring.ts implements log10(sales + citations + socialShares), quality weights 0.2/0.3/0.2/0.15/0.15, validity = AI 0.3 + Crowd 0.5 + Expert 0.2, and totalImpact = LV x quality x overlap x log(reach); book-service.ts is @ts-nocheck because 'Book models depend on a schema that is not active'; prisma/schema.prisma has no Book or Author; /books/[id], /api/books and /api/books/[id]/analysis import it and would throw; /books/[id] links to a /books index that does not exist. CLAUDE.md says adding a sixth engine is the wrong move and leaving losers indefinitely is the outcome that costs. *Options:* (a) Delete the feature and its three routes, and record on the wiki page that the popularity-weighted book score was tried and retired. (b) Restore it with a schema as a deliberate alternative engine, with a conformance diff. (c) Leave it, which keeps code that reads as working and is not.
70. **What should a work's linkage read before anyone argues it: 1.0 (static DEFLINK) or 0.5 (Next default)?** score_reference.py:34 sets DEFLINK = 1, so an unargued work fully bears on the belief it is filed under (the row still scores 0 because Quality sits at 0.5); schema 1402 and 1459 default linkageScore to 0.5, so an unargued Next claim counts at half centrality. The two engines disagree on the same labelled constant. *Options:* (a) Pick 1.0 everywhere: filing under a belief is itself the claim that it bears. (b) Pick 0.5 everywhere: unargued means unknown. (c) Keep both and document the disagreement on the page as unresolved.

### Automated-Conflict-Resolution-Platform

71. **Should contributor identity be hidden (as the wiki promises under Anonymization) or shown-and-never-scored (as the code does)?** Wiki line 57: "Anonymization: hide author identity during analysis." Code: tools/static-site/intake.py:21-24 and 218-227 write `added_by` = GitHub login and `added` = date into every contributed row "so the exports carry them and the page shows them under the row"; render_site.py:337-350 prints "Added by @login on date" linking to github.com/login; votes are keyed by login (content/votes.csv); the Next app prints ~quoteAuthor (ArgumentTreesSection.tsx:142-170). What both agree on is that identity is never a scoring input (render_site.py:985; prisma/schema.prisma:343-347). *Options:* (a) Keep the code's rule and rewrite the step as "Identity is shown for provenance and never scored"; (b) stop rendering added_by on pages while keeping it in exports and votes.csv; (c) drop added_by from intake entirely, which loses the audit trail intake.py was written to provide.
72. **Is any form of tone or fallacy detection wanted, given integrity.py's stated refusal?** Wiki lines 54-55 promise sentiment analysis to flag emotional attacks and entity recognition to catch ad hominem. tools/static-site/integrity.py:3-12 and render_site.py:1019-1024 say pattern matching is "deliberately not done" because it would fire on the corpus's central legitimate argument and "a score moved by a word pattern has no page to appeal to"; docs/BELIEF_PAGE_RULES.md:511 repeats it. The Next app has a community-confirmed fallacy mechanism (FallacyClaim in prisma, ConfirmedFallacyNotes) where a confirmed claim damages a named factor, which is argument-by-page rather than detection. *Options:* (a) Keep the refusal and rewrite the page to say attacks are handled by the confirmed-fallacy claim, a page like any other; (b) add a non-scoring advisory flag to Structural Checks (a note, never a deduction), which integrity.py's design would allow; (c) implement scoring deductions, which breaks the every-number-is-a-page rule and needs a conformance diff.
73. **Which interest-scoring engine is canonical: the static site's Validity x Drives (0-1 products, defaults 0.5) or the Next app's linkageAccuracy x validityScore / 100 with a Resolution Floor of 70 and a harmonic-mean pairing?** tools/static-site/render_site.py:884-909 and score_reference.py: Validity and Drives are each a page's truth (0-1), Drives defaults to UNARG 0.5, Score = Validity x Drives, primary pair = max product per side. src/core/scoring/conflict-resolution.ts:31-37, 96, 114-117: validity and linkage are typed 0-100, validity defaults to 50 when null, driveScore = linkage x validity / 100, shared interests need both validities >= 70 and similarity >= 0.5, pairedValidity = 2sv·ov/(sv+ov); src/features/belief-analysis/lib/interests.ts:16 pins RESOLUTION_FLOOR_VALIDITY = 70. The wiki's own methodology page (docs/wiki/Likely_interest_...md) uses 0-100 with Maslow bands 85+ / 60-85. The two engines cannot both be "the" IVS. *Options:* (a) Declare the static site's argued 0-1 product canonical and mark the Next app's typed 0-100 fields as a different, provisional surface; (b) port the Resolution Floor and the compromise-candidate rule into the static site as computed outputs; (c) keep both and say so on the page, which CLAUDE.md warns is the outcome that costs.
74. **Objective criteria: are the four labels typed and unread (static site) or four 0-1 scores averaged into a total (Next app), and is the fourth factor Importance or Independence?** tools/static-site/render_site.py:810, 819 and ise_tables.py:88: Validity / Reliability / Linkage / Importance are author-typed labels, "nothing reads them", Score is the criterion page's truth. src/core/scoring/all-scores.ts:419-451 and prisma/schema.prisma:792-822: validityScore, reliabilityScore, independenceScore, linkageScore (each default 0.5) averaged into totalScore (default 0), labelled excellent/good/moderate/weak/invalid at 0.80/0.60/0.40/>0/0, aggregated by sum(total x linkage)/sum(linkage). Different factor set, different arithmetic, different defaults. *Options:* (a) Static rule wins: a criterion's only score is its page's truth, labels are advice; (b) Next rule wins: argue each of four factors on its own page and multiply or average them, which the static site's "every multiplier is a page" design could host; (c) accept two surfaces and label the Next one provisional.
75. **Does the owner still want an attribution confidence INTERVAL (IACI), or is a point score (Drives / linkageAccuracy) the intended design?** Wiki line 125 promises intervals. Nothing computes one: static site Drives is a page truth (render_site.py:896-900), Next app linkageAccuracy is a typed 0-100 (prisma:923-926) plus a typed 0-1 confidence on ranking rows (types.ts:532-533). The static site's sensitivity.py already computes which single input would change an answer, which is the nearest existing uncertainty readout. *Options:* (a) Drop the interval and rename the step to Drives; (b) derive an interval from the driver page's confidence (confidence.py) and the row's sensitivity sweep, as a displayed range, not a score; (c) keep IACI as a proposed mechanism, marked as such.
76. **Should the Maslow classification of interests exist in the static site at all?** Wiki line 123 and docs/wiki/Likely_interest_...md:76-82 make Maslow the validity prior. Only the Next app carries it, as a typed free-text maslowPrior ("Safety 70-85") that reasons then move (InterestValiditySection.tsx:231); tools/static-site has no Maslow field, and its validity starts at the neutral 0.50 like every other page (evidence.py prior) unless the interest page declares evidence. *Options:* (a) Add a typed Maslow band column to interest pages as a label only (like the criterion labels); (b) make the Maslow band an evidence-style prior that sets p0 on an interest page, which is a rule change needing a conformance diff; (c) remove the claim and keep validity purely argued.
77. **Should the Next app's compromise-candidate rule (likelihood shift <= 0.15 flips a category net) be ported to the static site, which currently says it has no such test?** tools/static-site/render_site.py:2247-2249: "A compromise here is typed by its author; nothing on this site yet tests which disagreements a small change in likelihood would flip." src/core/scoring/conflict-resolution.ts:37, 230-275 implements exactly that test (ACHIEVABLE_SHIFT = 0.15, requiredShift = \|net\| / \|magnitude\|) over the CBA rows, read-only, feeding no score. The wiki's "Invent options for mutual gain" is the home for it. *Options:* (a) Port it to the static site as a computed Common Ground output; (b) leave it Next-only and say so on the page; (c) drop it from the Next app as well since nothing publishes it.

### Electronic-Democracy
