78. **Should the cost-benefit section have a controlled list of dimensions, and if so which: the wiki's seven, the TypeScript engine's four, or the static site's free-text units?** The wiki (lines 8-15) promises Financial, Time, Freedom, Security, Autonomy, Risk, Health. src/core/types/cba.ts:15-22 hard-codes Financial, HumanLife, Freedom, Time and every per-category breakdown iterates that array (cba-scoring.ts:344). docs/automated-cba/SKILL.md:30-44 adds Health, Environmental, Social Cohesion, Innovation as expansions. tools/static-site/ise_tables.py:322 defines `category` as 'cost and benefit rows: the units', free text, and the 218 live rows use roughly 200 different unit strings; render_site.py:293-299 withholds the net whenever more than one unit is priced. Security, Autonomy and Risk are categories nowhere. *Options:* (a) Adopt the seven as a controlled vocabulary in both engines and map the free-text units onto them, accepting that most live rows become 'other'; (b) keep the static site's rule that a row's unit is whatever it is measured in and drop the dimension list from the page, describing the four TypeScript categories as that engine's choice; (c) keep both and say which engine a reader is looking at.
79. **Do costs and benefits count toward a belief's truth score, as the wiki's 'reasons can include costs and benefits' implies, or stay a separate priced section?** Wiki line 27 lists costs and benefits among reasons. The static site scores a belief from arguments, evidence and predictions only (score_reference.py:181-210) and prices costs and benefits in their own section as magnitude x the claim page's truth (render_site.py:259-263), never feeding the belief score. The TypeScript CBA is a separate object (CostBenefitAnalysis) with its own likelihood trees. *Options:* (a) Keep the separation and rewrite the page to say a cost is a claim about the world that is priced, not a reason that scores; (b) let a priced cost or benefit row also contribute to the belief's argument score, which changes every published number.
80. **Is a media work a reason (wiki) or a container for reasons (static site)?** Wiki line 30 lists Media References among reasons and section 'Using Media as Evidence' treats a work's 'pull' as evidence. render_site.py:956 says media rows are 'shown, not counted: the belief score above is arguments, evidence and predictions only, because a book is a container for reasons rather than a reason'. The product's media-truth.ts reads belief scores into a work's score, not the reverse. *Options:* (a) Keep the one-way flow and rewrite the section as 'how a work is scored from the beliefs it carries'; (b) let a media row's score, (2 x Quality - 1) x Bears x Influence x Importance, enter the belief score.
81. **Should study rigor be typed as factors (sample size, randomization, blinding, controls, setting) or stay as a source-type tier plus replication?** Wiki line 29 names five rigor factors. evidence.py:79-99 ranks by 18 source types with fixed weights (rct 0.85, meta 0.80, observational 0.75 ...) plus ERQ replications and ERP agreement; none of the five factors is a field. The TypeScript CBA still uses T1-T4 (cba.ts:32-37) which BELIEF_PAGE_RULES.md:95 calls retired. *Options:* (a) Keep the tier model and rewrite the line to name ESIW, ERQ, ERP; (b) add typed rigor fields that adjust a study's starting point, with a conformance diff; (c) retire T1-T4 from the CBA types to match the rules doc.
82. **Is influence argued or measured?** Wiki line 52: influence is 'sales, citations, cultural reach'. The static site argues it (Influence Arguments table, same truth rule, render_site.py:1396, 2544) and records Reach as a separate typed number that no work has (line 1159); the product stores reach as an Int defaulting to 0 and multiplies it into Epistemic Impact (media-truth.ts:140-146, schema.prisma:1408), so every work with untyped reach has impact 0 on /media. *Options:* (a) Argued influence, with reach shown and never multiplied (static site); (b) typed reach multiplied in (product); (c) both, labelled, with reach typed for the works on the static site so the two agree.
83. **Does the community decide anything, or only argue?** The page's title and 'Pragmatic Democracy' section promise communities that identify optimal policies. The code records votes and refuses to let them move a score (README.md:258, intake.py:90, test_intake.py:210, 610), and confidence.py carries behavioural signals at weight 0. *Options:* (a) Keep 'a vote never moves a score' and rewrite the democracy sections to say the vote is shown beside the analysis as what people think; (b) let votes feed confidence (the migration confidence.py:17-23 describes), which is a rule change with a conformance diff.
84. **Should any engine print a verdict?** The wiki's algorithm 'identifies policies that maximize safety, happiness, and societal well-being'. CLAUDE.md: 'There is no verdict module any more'; the static site withholds even the net when units are mixed. cba-scoring.ts:455-461 prints net_positive / net_negative / uncertain with a 10 percent threshold. *Options:* (a) Remove the verdict from the TypeScript CBA to match the static site; (b) keep it and document that the two engines differ; (c) restore a verdict on the static site.
85. **In the TypeScript CBA, is likelihood derived from arguments or chosen by a person?** SKILL.md:52 says likelihood_score is 'Computed (never manually assigned)' and gives normalized = (raw/max_possible + 1)/2. cba-scoring.ts:115-126 implements pro/(pro+con) instead, and recalculateCBA (lines 398-437) does not call it: it scores each typed estimate's tree with scoreLikelihoodEstimate and takes activeProbability = the typed probability of the estimate whose tree scores best (scoring-engine.ts:671-705). The recursive three-factor function with 0.5^(d-1) attenuation (lines 68-126) is imported by nothing but tests. *Options:* (a) Wire calculateLikelihoodFromTree into recalculateCBA so likelihood is derived; (b) keep the winning-estimate rule and correct the skill doc; (c) retire the unused three-factor path.
86. **Fallacy detection: penalize (product) or refuse (static site)?** render_site.py:1018-1027: matching wording against named fallacies is 'deliberately not done ... a score moved by a word pattern has no page to appeal to'. scoring-engine.ts:431-436 multiplies baseTruth by (1 - sum of \|fallacy impact\|/100) and /algorithms/fallacy-detection explains it. *Options:* (a) Remove the penalty and make fallacies arguments on the claim's own page; (b) keep the penalty in the product and say the static site differs; (c) adopt the penalty on the static site with a conformance diff.

### Evidence‐based-policy

87. **Which text is the Evidence-based policy page: the 2025-08-09 five-point summary on the GitHub wiki, or the TRS-style 'Evidence Scoring Engine' text committed to docs/wiki/ under the same filename?** The two are unrelated documents. The GitHub wiki copy promises a structured database, three-factor scoring, automatic updates, discussion and transparency; the docs/wiki copy promises 'Evidence Impact = Quality Score x Linkage Score', nested Measurement/Methodology/Inference claims, UUIDs, PostgreSQL or Neo4j, an NLP parser for unstated assumptions, ReasonRank as user credibility and real-time recursive updates. The code contradicts the latter on ReasonRank (reasonrank.py:1-15, a page's share of a damped walk, never a user score), on model-asserted numbers (CLAUDE.md: 'the one thing the published engine refuses to carry'), and on storage (export_db.py: SQL and SQLite exports, no server; integer ids). Whichever is chosen, the sidebar link [[Evidence‐based policy]] resolves to one of them. *Options:* (a) Keep the five-point summary as the page and fold the TRS text's survivable parts (Quality x Linkage as the row formula's Truth x Link; forms that categorise evidence; load-bearing assumptions) into it with their real status; (b) keep the TRS text and rewrite each of its contradicted claims against the code; (c) keep both under different names, with the TRS one marked as a proposal.
88. **Should 'prevents outdated data from influencing decision-making' be a scoring rule or stay as 'argue it false on its own page'?** The page promises it; the code has no recency, decay or lifecycle factor and method.py:202 lists the wiki's lifecycle multipliers (active 1.0, weakened 0.7, conditional 0.8, outdated 0.3, refuted 0.1) under rules the site does not run, replaced by 'A row argued false scores below zero through 2 x Truth - 1'. The data to do otherwise is partly there and read by nothing: intake writes a `checked` date into each submitted row (ise_tables.py:94), interest pages carry `latest`, and a finding's erp can be lowered when replications fail. *Options:* (a) Keep the engine as is and have the page say outdated evidence is retired by argument or by lowering erp, with the date fields as display only; (b) add a dated-evidence rule (which would be a rule change: conformance.py --write, a reviewed diff, and a test_engines pin); (c) make staleness a structural check that names old findings and moves no score, like the other integrity checks.
89. **Should uniqueness be computed, or remain a presumed 1 that a lexical detector only flags?** The page promises 'avoiding double-counting similar arguments'. The code's Uniq factor defaults to DEFUNIQ = 1 and only 2 uniqueness pages exist in 4,263 pages, so no row in the corpus is discounted; similarity.py (FLAG 0.45, MERGE 0.70, IDF-weighted Jaccard + char-4-gram cosine) flags pairs and method.py:130 says 'It changes no score and cannot' because 'a number that silently discounts an argument and has no page behind it cannot be audited'. ALGORITHMS.md 10 and the wiki's Equivalency Score specify the computed form (1 - highest similarity). *Options:* (a) Keep uniqueness argued-only and have the page say the defence is a reading queue plus argued pages; (b) wire the computed similarity in as Uniq with a labelled weight (a rule change with conformance consequences, and the method page's auditability objection to answer); (c) keep argued-only but make a flagged, unargued pair cap the pair's combined weight, as a middle rule.
90. **Should assumptions carry a score (the wiki's Necessity Score) or remain a typed load-bearing flag plus unscored lists?** The page's first contribution is a database that 'quantifies the strength' of links between conclusions and assumptions. In the code, the only quantified effect of an assumption is the cap min(argued, weakest component with lb: Y) (score_reference.py:208-209), the lb flag is typed by the author and cannot be set from the contribution form (intake.py has no lb handling), and the 233 `assumption` rows (Required to accept / Required to reject) are read by no formula. render_site.py:876-877 prints that the Necessity Score is not computed. The same cap is what holds every belief in the corpus at 0.50 (CLAUDE.md open decision 6). *Options:* (a) Keep the typed flag and say so on the page; (b) add a necessity page per component, argued like a linkage page, whose truth decides whether and how far the component caps; (c) score the assumption lists as ordinary claims with their own pages so 'challenge an assumption' has a scored target.
91. **Which engine does the page describe when it says a score is determined by 'strength of supporting/opposing arguments': the static site's signed rule (an unargued reason is worth 0) or src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts (A/(A+D), where listing a reason raises the score)?** CLAUDE.md records the disagreement as unresolved (open decision 1). tools/static-site/score_reference.py:160-166 and test_engines.py:43 pin that volume counts for nothing and that argument alone never leaves 0.50; the Next.js belief page's engine does not share that rule. The wiki page is read as describing one product. *Options:* (a) Describe the static site's rule and say the belief-page engine differs pending consolidation; (b) describe both; (c) decide the consolidation first and write the page to the winner.
92. **Should readers be able to classify evidence (etype, erq, erp) and mark load-bearing premises through the site's forms, or are those author-only acts?** The page says the public can 'share evidence' and 'challenge assumptions'. Today the contribution form carries Source, URL and Date checked and intake.py stamps provenance, but the three fields that set a finding's starting point (evidence.py:146-148) and the lb flag are page/row columns with no form field and no intake handling, so a finding a reader submits starts at p0 = 0.50 and moves nothing until an author edits the tables. method.py:232 already names the classification as 'a judgement call' that moves a claim from 0.65 to 0.95. *Options:* (a) Author-only, and the page says a submitted finding is classified by the maintainer before it counts; (b) add etype/erq/erp and lb to contribute.yml and intake.py, accepting reader-typed tiers; (c) make the tier itself an argued page per the EVS wiki's pro/con form, which the method page says the wiki asks for.

## Page by page

### Home

Home.md is the wiki's landing page: eight linked sections (ReasonRank, Truth Score, Linkage Scores, Impact Algorithm, Redundancy Reduction, Automated Conflict Resolution, Belief Stability Confidence Score, The Vision) each promising one scoring mechanism, plus two links that point at pages that do not exist. It was last edited 2025-08-09 (wiki commit 70fe239); the repository copy at docs/wiki/Home.md is a different, older page describing a React/Express/MongoDB stack the project does not have, and _archive/wiki-updates/Home.md is an ASCII-quote copy of the live page. Against the code, every section's headline idea exists somewhere, but under different names, formulas and defaults, in two engines that disagree with each other (tools/static-site/, the published site, and src/core/scoring/, the Next.js app): the published engine deliberately refuses three of the page's specific promises (votes moving scores, automated fallacy scoring, computed uniqueness/equivalency moving a score) and documents why in integrity.py, method.py and the tests; the Next.js engine does a version of each. Nothing on the page is named the way the code names it (no "Belief Impact Score", no "Logical Fallacy Score", no "resolution likelihood", no "Belief Stability Confidence Score"), and the linked explainer pages carry 2023-era sample code (dask, spaCy, sklearn, TextBlob, BeautifulSoup) that nothing in the repository uses.

| Promise on the page | Status | Where | What the code does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Intro: 'an open, evidence-based platform to rank arguments, measure belief strength, and automate conflict resolution' | Partly implemented | tools/static-site/score_reference.py:34,160-212; tools/static-site/render_site.py:506-508,2466-2497,2642; src/core/scoring/conflict-resolution.ts:1-25,255-291 | Ranking and belief strength are implemented in both engines (static site: row = sign x (2 x Truth - 1) x Conf x Link x Imp x Uniq, page truth = (POS + w x p0)/(POS + NEG + w) capped by the weakest load-bearing component; Next.js: rawImpact = RR x linkage x importance x uniqueness, truth = Pro/(Pro + Con)). Conflict resolution is a read-only readout over scored rows (shared interests, primary conflict pair, value conflicts, compromise candidates), not an automated resolver; the static site's topic page says 'nothing on this site yet tests which disagreements a small change in likelihood would flip' (render_site.py:2247-2249). |
| ReasonRank: 'Google's PageRank can be adapted to assess the strength of pro/con arguments' | Implemented | src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:10-19,58,430-496,520-574; tools/static-site/reasonrank.py:1-45,49-51,95,103-125,172; tools/static-site/test_engines.py:234-313; docs/ALGORITHMS.md:374-422; tools/static-site/method.py:198 | Two different things carry the name. (1) scoring-engine.ts is a damped recursion over the argument tree: for a leaf RR = baseTruth; otherwise subArgScore = 0.5 + 0.5 x (proSubRank - conSubRank)/numSubArgs and RR = (1 - d) x baseTruth + d x subArgScore with d = DAMPING_FACTOR = 0.85 (line 58); rawImpact = RR x linkage x importance x uniqueness (line 496); belief truth = ProRank/(ProRank + ConRank) (lines 578-585). (2) tools/static-site/reasonrank.py is an actual PageRank-style random walk: starts evenly at the beliefs, steps along rows in proportion to capacity = Link x Imp x Uniq (line 95), DAMPING = 0.85 (line 49), restart at the beliefs with probability 1 - d, dangling mass returned to the beliefs, iterated to TOLERANCE 1e-12 with an ITERATIONS cap of 2000 (lines 50-51; the wiki says 100 iterations). It deliberately excludes the claim's truth and its confidence (docstring lines 20-25; tests test_rank_ignores_the_truth_of_the_claims_it_ranks and test_rank_ignores_confidence_entirely), so it ranks how much of the corpus depends on a page, not whether it is true, and feeds work = rank x (1 - confidence) (line 172). The static site's truth score is the separate recursive rule in score_reference.py. docs/ALGORITHMS.md:374-422 gives a third form, R(p) = (1-d)/N + d x sum R(q)/L(q) restarting evenly over every argument, which method.py:197 lists as not what runs. **Corrected on verification:** Status stands but two citations are wrong. scoring-engine.ts:578-585 is the confidence-interval code (argBalance/baseCI/argFactor), not the belief truth; ProRank/(ProRank+ConRank) is at 557-566, and the actual truthScore (line 574) adds an evidence contribution of up to +/-0.2 and clamps to [0.01,0.99], which the detail omits. method.py:197 is the Topic overlap row; the 'ReasonRank restarting evenly across every argument' not-run row is line 198 (fallacy blurb is 186 as cited elsewhere). Everything else verified: DAMPING_FACTOR 0.85 at :58, subArgScore = 0.5 + normalizedNet*0.5 and RR = (1-d)*baseTruth + d*subArgScore at :480-485, rawImpact at :496; reasonrank.py cap = Link x Imp x Uniq at :95, DAMPING 0.85 / ITERATIONS 2000 / TOLERANCE 1e-12 at :49-51, restart (1-d)*seed and dangling mass returned to seeds at :106-117, work = rank x (1 - confidence) at :172, docstring :20-25 excludes truth and confidence; tests at test_engines.py:249 and :283; ALGORITHMS.md:382 gives R(p) = (1-d)/N + d x sum R(q)/L(q) with iterations = 100 at :395. |
| ReasonRank: 'values conclusions based on the strength of supporting arguments, factoring in logic, evidence, and relevance' | Partly implemented | tools/static-site/score_reference.py:160-179,194-212; tools/static-site/evidence.py:140-151; tools/static-site/integrity.py:3-17; src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:432-436,496 | Evidence and relevance are factors in both engines (evidence sets a page's starting point p0 and weight w in the static site; linkage multiplies every row). 'Logic' as a factor exists only in the Next.js engine, as baseTruth = truthScore x (1 - sum\|impact\|/100) from confirmed fallacy claims (scoring-engine.ts:432-436). The static site has no logical-validity factor at all: integrity.py:3-17 says fallacy pattern matching is deliberately not implemented and its structural checks move no score; a fallacy is argued as a reason to disagree on the linkage page (method.py:186). |
| ReasonRank linked page: 'Weigh arguments by quantity and quality of their supporting or opposing reasons' | Partly implemented | src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:538-566 (counts quantity: proRank/conRank are sums of rawImpact, truth = proRank/totalRank); tools/static-site/score_reference.py:161-166,177-180 (refuses quantity by design); tools/static-site/test_engines.py:43; tools/static-site/README.md:19-21,34-40; CLAUDE.md:126-145 | The published engine refuses quantity: a row contributes sign x (2 x Truth - 1) x ..., so a listed-but-unargued claim (Truth 0.5) contributes exactly 0, and test_argument_alone_can_never_leave_the_neutral_point pins that every page of an unsourced argument graph reads 0.50 forever. The Next.js engine does count volume (truth = Pro/(Pro + Con) over summed rawImpacts, so an extra unargued pro row raises the score). CLAUDE.md records this as an unresolved disagreement, not an accident. **Corrected on verification:** The design refusal is real and quoted: score_reference.py:163-164 'A claim nobody has argued sits at 0.5 and contributes exactly 0, so listing a claim is worth nothing until it is argued'; README.md:34-40 and test_engines.py:43 pin the 0.50 fixed point. But that is one engine. The product engine the belief page runs, src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:538-566, sums rawImpact per side and scores proRank/(proRank+conRank), so an extra unargued pro leaf (RR = baseTruth) raises the score: quantity and quality are both weighed there, which the auditor's own detail admits and CLAUDE.md:142-145 records as an unresolved disagreement. A claim implemented by the product engine and refused by the static site is partial, not contradicted_by_design. The cited scoring-engine.ts:578-585 is also wrong (confidence interval), the sum/ratio is at 538-566. |
| ReasonRank linked page: 'Incorporate user feedback (votes, credibility scores) to refine rankings over time'; `feedback_data` multiplies scores in the sample code | Partly implemented | src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:210-228 (resolveLinkageScore priority 2) and 341-349 (aggregateLinkageVotes); src/app/api/protocol/[id]/linkage/route.ts:26,95-122; src/app/api/fallacy-claims/[id]/votes/route.ts:6-9,58,99-101,127; src/lib/consensus.ts:31-34; src/lib/fallacy/calibration.ts:22-28; refusal only in tools/static-site/README.md:258-270, test_render.py:2373-2376, method.py:231 | Static site: 'A vote never moves a score.' Votes are recorded in content/votes.csv, shown on the heading line as 'votes, not a score', and test_no_vote_moves_any_score asserts truth and belief are identical with and without votes.csv. Next.js engine does let two kinds of vote in: aggregateLinkageVotes is a weight-averaged vote on linkage in [-1, 1] (scoring-engine.ts:341-349, priority 2 after a linkage debate), and fallacy claims are confirmed by weighted consensus at CONSENSUS_THRESHOLD 0.6 with CONSENSUS_QUORUM 3, each voter weighted by a credibility multiplier in [0.3, 1.4] from accuracy and side balance (calibration.ts). Neither engine implements the wiki's mean-of-feedback multiplier. **Corrected on verification:** Every citation checks out as quoted: README 'A vote never moves a score', test_no_vote_moves_any_score, method.py 'never scored', aggregateLinkageVotes weighted average clamped to [-1,1], CONSENSUS_THRESHOLD 0.6 / QUORUM 3, CREDIBILITY_FLOOR 0.3 / CEILING 1.4. But 'contradicted_by_design' only describes tools/static-site. The engine CLAUDE.md says the product runs (src/core/scoring) does incorporate user votes into scores: resolveLinkageScore (scoring-engine.ts:215-228) returns the weighted community vote as the linkage score whenever no linkage debate exists, and that linkage multiplies into rawImpact (scoring-engine.ts:442); the protocol linkage route stores votes and calls aggregateLinkageVotes (route.ts:122). Fallacy-claim votes are weighted by a credibility multiplier computed from the voter's track record (votes/route.ts:127 callerCredibilityFor; client-supplied weight rejected at 99-101) and the confirmed claim then moves the target's truth (claims.ts:115-127 bridge into scoreArgument). That is votes plus credibility scores refining rankings, so the general claim is partially implemented in the live engine; only the specific mean-of-`feedback_data` multiplier is absent (grep 'feedback' in src/core, src/lib, tools/static-site finds nothing scoring-related). One further exaggeration in the detail: the linkage vote 'weight based on user reputation' is only a comment; the protocol route takes weight as a client-supplied z.number 0-10 defaulting to 1.0 (route.ts:26), not a computed reputation. |
| ReasonRank linked page: uniqueness computed as 1 - max TF-IDF similarity and multiplied into the propagation | Partly implemented | src/core/scoring/duplication-scoring.ts:355-359 (uniquenessFromSimilarities = 1 - max), 267-269 (mechanicalSimilarity = Jaccard over normalized claims); src/lib/propagate-belief-scores.ts:15-18,146-178 (siblingUniquenessFor multiplies into impactScore), wired from src/app/api/v1/ingest/route.ts:5, src/app/api/beliefs/[id]/arguments/route.ts:29, src/app/api/arguments/[id]/linkage/route.ts:28; static-site refusal at tools/static-site/score_reference.py:34,174, similarity.py:28-41,88-90, method.py:130,200 | Static site: Uniq reads a uniqueness page's truth or DEFUNIQ = 1 (presumed distinct); similarity.py computes ces = 0.5 x IDF-weighted Jaccard of content words + 0.5 x IDF-weighted cosine over character 4-grams (not TF-IDF, no model), flags pairs at FLAG 0.45 and MERGE 0.70, and 'changes no score and cannot' (method.py:130). Next.js engine does compute it: uniquenessFromSimilarities = 1 - max similarity over earlier same-side siblings (duplication-scoring.ts:355), blended 0.4 mechanical / 0.6 semantic / 0.0 community (lines 67-71), and propagate-belief-scores.ts multiplies it into impactScore. **Corrected on verification:** Static-site citations confirmed verbatim: DEFUNIQ=1 in CONSTS (score_reference.py:34), the row formula reads pg(uniq_id, DEFUNIQ) (line 174), ces = 0.5*jaccard + 0.5*cosine over 4-grams with IDF weighting (similarity.py:88-90, 26-28), FLAG 0.45 / MERGE 0.70 (lines 46,50), method.py:130 'It changes no score and cannot' and :200 'Presumed distinct until a uniqueness page is argued'. But the auditor's own detail concedes the product engine computes exactly the wiki's formula (uniqueness = 1 - max similarity over earlier same-side siblings) and multiplies it into impactScore, and propagate-belief-scores is imported by live mutation routes (ingest, arguments, linkage). An engine the product runs implementing the formula cannot be 'contradicted_by_design'; only the static site refuses it. The gap from the wiki is the similarity measure: TF-IDF appears nowhere live (only _archive/backend/utils/redundancyDetector.js), and the live propagation uses plain Jaccard via mechanicalSimilarity (propagate-belief-scores.ts:173), not the 0.4/0.6 layer blend the auditor cites from DEFAULT_LAYER_WEIGHTS (duplication-scoring.ts:67-71), which no file outside duplication-scoring.ts references. Hence partial. |
| ReasonRank linked page: sentiment enhancement `enhanced_scores *= (1 + doc.sentiment)` via spaCy en_core_web_lg, dask chunked matrices, `num_iterations` default 100, damping 0.85 | Not implemented | none found for sentiment/dask/spaCy; damping at tools/static-site/reasonrank.py:49 and src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:58; iterations at tools/static-site/reasonrank.py:50-51 | No sentiment factor anywhere (grep for 'sentiment' in scoring code finds nothing; the only NLP in the repo is src/core/ai/, unwired, and its similarity is embeddings, not sentiment). Damping 0.85 matches both engines. Iterations: the static walk stops at TOLERANCE 1e-12 under a 2000 cap and reports the residual; the TS recursion is a single bottom-up pass with no iteration count. |
| Truth Score: 'Logical Fallacy Score measures the degree and impact of fallacies in an argument' | Replaced by another mechanism | src/lib/fallacy/claims.ts:97-108,115-127,141-143; src/lib/fallacy/catalog.ts:1-30; src/lib/consensus.ts:31-34; src/lib/agent-ingest/fallacy-detector.ts:1-17; src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:432-436; src/app/algorithms/fallacy-detection/page.tsx; tools/static-site/integrity.py:3-17,35-45; tools/static-site/method.py:186,196 | No score called 'Logical Fallacy Score' exists. Next.js replaces it with structured fallacy claims: a six-field accusation template (fallacyType from a catalog, quotedText, explanation of 40+ chars, missingElements, evidenceLinks, consequences), confirmed only by weighted consensus (60%, quorum 3); once confirmed, a logical-validity ladder VALIDITY_NO_FALLACY 0.95 / MINOR 0.75 / MAJOR 0.45 / MULTIPLE 0.25 is bridged into scoreArgument as truth x (1 - sum\|impact\|/100) (claims.ts:115-127), and the confirmed claim also enters the target's linkage sub-debate as a counter-argument of strength 0.8 (major) or 0.5 (minor). The regex detector (fallacy-detector.ts) only drafts counter-arguments and 'returns NO numeric fields'. The static site refuses any fallacy scoring (integrity.py:3-17: a pattern matcher 'would fire on the central legitimate argument on every page') and runs nine structural checks instead (circular support, assumes its own conclusion, same page listed twice, rows that may be the same point twice, rests only on authority, evidence filed above the premise, single-figure costs, nothing falsifiable, one-sided), none of which moves a score. The wiki's five-band confidence interval (score < -2 'Very likely fallacious' ... >= 2 'Very likely sound') and the keyword-regex ImprovedFallacyDetector are not implemented. |
| Truth Score: 'Evidence Verification Score evaluates how well the claim is supported by independent, rigorous evidence' | Replaced by another mechanism | tools/static-site/evidence.py:27-29,79-101,105-109,140-158; tools/static-site/README.md:22-23; tools/static-site/method.py:94-96,194; src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:108-137; tools/static-site/test_engines.py:82,128,638-666 | Static site: the wiki's EVS formula ESIW x ECRS x ERQ x (ERP/100) is computed by evs() and printed in every Evidence Ledger 'for comparison; no score reads it' (method.py:96). What scores is a prior: p0 = 0.5 + 0.5 x ESIW x (2 x ERP/100 - 1), w = k x 2 x ERQ/(ERQ + 1) with k = 1 and REP_CAP 2.0, so p0 is confined to [0.05, 0.95] and replication can at most double the weight. ESIW is the wiki's 16-row table with its weights (0.90 down to 0.10) in its order, the rank-2 band '0.85-0.80' split RCT 0.85 / meta-analysis 0.80 in listing order, plus one added tier 'record' (primary official record) at 0.90 with no wiki rank, and NOSOURCE = 0.0 for an unclassified page (evidence.py:61-70,79-99); ECRS is not duplicated because it is the linkage page (lines 51-55). Next.js: calculateEVS = ESIW x log2(ERQ + 1) x ECRS x ERP with ERP as a 0-1 fraction (scoring-engine.ts:124-137), and ESIW comes from four tiers T1 1.0 / T2 0.75 / T3 0.5 / T4 0.25, default 0.5 (lines 110-118), not the 16-row table. Differences from the wiki, item by item: wiki ERQ linear vs TS log2(ERQ+1) vs static bounded 2n/(n+1); wiki ERP/100 vs TS ERP as fraction; wiki 16 tiers vs TS 4 tiers vs static 18 (16 + record + none); wiki sums EVS unbounded, static caps the start at [0.05, 0.95]. **Corrected on verification:** Status is right and the static-site half is exactly as described: evidence.py:146-148 computes p0 = 0.5 + 0.5*ESIW*(2*ERP/100 - 1) and w = k*2n/(n+1) with REP_CAP = 2.0 (line 101), NOSOURCE = 0.0 (line 99), the 16 ranked rows 0.90..0.10 plus 'record' at 0.90/None (lines 79-98), evs() at 154-158 reproduces ESIW*ECRS*ERQ*(ERP/100), the docstring at 51-55 says ECRS is the linkage page, method.py:95-96 says 'shown and read by no score', README.md:22-23 prints the same formulas, and test_engines.py:646 pins len(ESIW)==18. calculateEVS at scoring-engine.ts:124-136 is ESIW*log2(ERQ+1)*ECRS*ERP as stated. Overturned on one citation detail: the Next.js tier table is not 'four tiers'. EVIDENCE_TYPE_WEIGHTS (scoring-engine.ts:108-114) has five entries: T0 0.05 (retracted/fraudulent) at line 109, then T1 1.0, T2 0.75, T3 0.5, T4 0.25, default 0.5 at line 117. The 'wiki 16 vs TS 4 vs static 18' comparison should read TS 5, and the cited range should start at 108. |

The wiki's ECLS = sum agree / sum all in [0, 1], 0.0 when no reasons, differs on three counts: scale ([0,1] wiki vs [-1,1] TS vs [0,1] static), the no-argument default (wiki 0.0, TS 0.0, static 1.0), and the static site's shrinkage toward p0 (method.py:204). |

Decision Leverage additionally reports the transmission weight of each edge, \|linkage\| x importance x uniqueness x 0.5^depth. |
| Linkage: 'Uses an adapted PageRank model to reflect collective judgment on relevance' | Partly implemented | tools/static-site/score_reference.py:197-198; src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:173-193,341-349; tools/static-site/README.md:261-265 | Static site: a linkage page is scored by the same argued-truth rule as any page (kind 'linkage' in SPECIAL), not by a walk; the 'Bears on this: yes/no' vote is shown and never scored. Next.js: calculateImportanceScore clamps a 0-1 input and labels decisive >= 0.85, significant >= 0.65, moderate >= 0.40, minor >= 0.15, else negligible; deriveImportanceFromBeliefScore = (overallScore + 100)/200. 'How much a belief depends on an argument' is answered more literally by Decision Leverage: weight = \|linkage\| x importance x uniqueness x 0.5^depth, openRange = weighted gaps (evidence 0.40, linkage 0.25, scoring 0.15, examination 0.20), leverage = weight x openRange x 100, load-bearing at weight >= 0.35; and by the static site's sensitivity.py, which bisects each input to find the value that flips the conclusion across 0.5. |

- Link 'Redundancy Reduction' -> https://github.com/myklob/ideastockexchange/wiki/Better-Ways-of-Saying-the-Same-thing: no such page exists and none ever did in the wiki's git history; nearest is Equivalency-Score:.md.
- Link 'Automated Conflict Resolution' -> .../wiki/Automated-Conflict-Resolution: page existed 2023-04-01 to 2023-11-10 and is gone; the live page is Automated-Conflict-Resolution-Platform.md.
- Link 'Impact Algorithm' -> .../wiki/Confidence-Stability-Scores: wrong target; that page never mentions importance or belief impact, and the same URL is reused by the 'Belief Stability Confidence Score' section.
- Name 'Belief Impact Score (BIS)': no such score in code; the only beliefImpact* identifier is beliefImpactWeight = log10(reach) on book pages (src/features/books/services/book-scoring.ts:40), and Media.md uses BIS for Book Importance Score.
- Name 'Logical Fallacy Score': no such score in code; the wiki page Logical-Fallacy-Score.md was deleted after 2023-05-10, so the Truth-Score and Quantifying-Reason pages' links for it point at sites.google.com/d/1f62q1ZSjo3Kcb7Pj5OHCNTEfaacTWuKD/... (private Google Sites drafts) and the ReasonRank pages' link for it points at a chatgpt.com conversation ending in '#'.
- Name 'Belief Stability Confidence Score' / 'Evidence Stability Score (ESS)' / 'Belief Stability Score (BSS)': code names are Confidence (tools/static-site/confidence.py), stabilityScore and calculateConfidenceStabilityScore (src/core/scoring/all-scores.ts); no ESS or BSS.
- Name 'resolution likelihood': code has 'ease of resolution' (render_site.py:245-247) and compromise candidates by requiredShift (conflict-resolution.ts:255-291).
- Section title 'Linkage Scores' links to a page titled 'Evidence to Conclusion Linkage Score (ECLS)' while the EVS page calls the same factor 'Evidence-to-Conclusion Relevance Score (ECRS)'; the code calls it the linkage page / linkageScore and the file is named Evidence-to-Conclusion-Relevance-Score.md.
- See-Also blocks on ReasonRank:-Google's-PageRank-for-Arguments.md and ReasonRank.md use nested links of the form [[text](https://chatgpt.com/g/g-zEqCtLTMb-idea-stock-exchange/c/...)](Page) that render as a chatgpt.com link wrapped in a wiki link.
- Truth-Score.md 'See Also' links both entries to sites.google.com edit URLs that require the owner's Google account.
- Code fences closed with four backticks (````) instead of three in ReasonRank:-Google's-PageRank-for-Arguments.md:100, ReasonRank.md:54, Truth-Score.md:78, Evidence-Verification-Score-(EVS).md:72 and 113, Evidence-to-Conclusion-Relevance-Score.md:31 and 113, Equivalency-Score:.md:46 and 71, Media.md (several), Confidence-Stability-Scores.md:95.
- Formulas placed inside empty fences with blank lines (EVS.md:68-72, ECLS.md:26-31, Equivalency-Score:.md:42-46, Media.md 'Impact Score' and 'BAS' blocks) so they render as code blocks containing a blank line, and Equivalency-Score:.md:44 escapes underscores as w\_ces * CES(A,B) + w\_ues * UES(A,B).
- Sample code depends on libraries the project does not use: dask.array, spacy (en_core_web_lg, en_core_web_md, en_core_web_sm), sklearn TfidfVectorizer/CountVectorizer/LatentDirichletAllocation, textblob, requests + bs4 BeautifulSoup. The repository's Python toolchain requires only openpyxl (tools/static-site/requirements.txt) and its similarity.py says 'this has no model and no network'.
- ReasonRank:-Google's-PageRank-for-Arguments.md: `num_iterations` default 100 vs the code's convergence to 1e-12 under a 2000 cap; `feedback_data`, `integrate_feedback`, `apply_domain_specific_enhancements` (spaCy sentiment) have no counterpart.
- Evidence-Verification-Score-(EVS).md: the rank-2 band '0.85-0.80' covers two categories; the code splits it RCT 0.85 / meta-analysis 0.80 in listing order and adds an unranked 'record' tier at 0.90 (evidence.py:61-70). The page's sample defines only three category keys with names (statistics_and_data, ..._randomized_controlled_trials, ..._meta_analysis) that do not match the code's keys (statistics, rct, meta).
- Quantifying-Reason:-The-Power-of-Truth-Scores.md:60 links 'GitHub Wiki - Logical Fallacy Score' to https://github.com/myklob/ideastockexchange/wiki#logical-fallacy-score, an anchor that does not exist on Home.
- docs/wiki/Home.md inside the repository is a different page (React 18 + Vite frontend, Node/Express/JWT backend, MongoDB, `backend/routes/auth.js`, 'Conclusion Score CS = sum((RtA - RtD) x ES x LC x VC x LR x UD x AI)', 'Credibility = 50 + verifiedCount x 10 - disputedCount x 10', '10 types of logical fallacies', '4 similarity algorithms') describing a stack the repository does not contain; the live wiki Home is the one audited here, and _archive/wiki-updates/Home.md is a straight-quote copy of it.
- Home.md itself has no date, no pointer to the published site (myklob.github.io/ideastockexchange/beliefs/), its method page (method.html), the /algorithms index in the Next.js app, or the conformance contract (data/conformance_corpus.json, data/conformance_expected.json), which are where the running rules are written down.
- Em dashes throughout; the project's own template check (tools/check_templates.py) treats em dashes as a stale-template fingerprint, so a rewritten page copied into templates/ or docs/ would fail it.

**What an updated page must say:**

- Date the page and say what it covers: the overview of the scoring system as of the rewrite, with a line that the previous version (2025-08-09) predates the published site (myklob.github.io/ideastockexchange/beliefs/), its method page (method.html), the /algorithms index and the conformance contract (data/conformance_corpus.json, data/conformance_expected.json; 25 pages, 26 edges, 5 constants, 18 tiers).
- State the two engines once, up front, by path: tools/static-site/ (the published site, scorer of record score_reference.py) and src/core/scoring/ (the Next.js app), and that src/lib/ise-pages/ is an unwired proposal. Every mechanism below is labelled with which engine runs it.
- ReasonRank section: keep the PageRank framing and the owner's variable list (M_pro/M_con, linkage matrices, uniqueness, initial scores, num_iterations, d, feedback_data) and the Python sample, labelled 'original 2023 sketch; not what runs'. Add the two things that do run: (1) the recursive truth rule, row = sign x (2 x Truth - 1) x Confidence x Link x Imp x Uniq, page truth = (POS + w x p0)/(POS + NEG + w) then min with the weakest load-bearing component, belief score = POS - NEG, constants K 1, UNARG 0.5, DEFLINK 1, DEFIMP 0.5, DEFUNIQ 1 (score_reference.py:34; src/lib/ise-pages/types.ts:117-123); (2) the ReasonRank walk, capacity = Link x Imp x Uniq, DAMPING 0.85, restart at the beliefs, converged to 1e-12 (2000-iteration cap), truth and confidence deliberately excluded, work = rank x (1 - confidence) (reasonrank.py:49-51,95,172). Give the TS form beside it: RR = (1 - 0.85) x baseTruth + 0.85 x (0.5 + 0.5 x net/numSubArgs), rawImpact = RR x linkage x importance x uniqueness, truth = Pro/(Pro + Con), evidence adds up to +/-0.2 (scoring-engine.ts:486,496,578-585). Mark 'quantity' as the open disagreement (CLAUDE.md decision 1) and feedback_data as contradicted by the published engine (test_render.py:2373).
- Truth Score section: keep the Logical Fallacy + Evidence Verification pairing as the owner's framing, then say what each became. Fallacy: the published site runs no fallacy score (integrity.py:3-17) and runs nine named structural checks instead (list them with their severities from integrity.py:35-45); the app runs structured fallacy claims: six required fields, catalog of types with targetFactor relevance / logical-validity / evidence-quality, 60% weighted consensus with quorum 3, validity ladder 0.95 / 0.75 / 0.45 / 0.25 bridged as truth x (1 - sum\|impact\|/100), counter-argument strength 0.8 major / 0.5 minor, caller credibility in [0.3, 1.4] (claims.ts, catalog.ts, consensus.ts, calibration.ts). Keep the owner's five-band confidence interval, the fallacy list, and both code samples labelled proposed/not implemented. Keep the 'ad hominem fires on the central argument' reason verbatim from integrity.py so a future reader knows why.
- EVS section: keep the owner's 16-row ESIW table exactly, with its weights, and the formula EVS = ESIW x ECRS x ERQ x (ERP/100) and the Python sample. ### ReasonRank:-Google's-PageRank-for-Arguments

The page proposes ReasonRank as a PageRank adaptation for a pro/con forum: a 100-iteration matrix propagation with damping 0.85 over separate pro and con adjacency matrices, multiplied by TF-IDF uniqueness and by user feedback (votes, credibility), then multiplied by spaCy sentiment. It was last edited 2025-08-09 and has not followed the code. The repository now carries three things under the name: the signed row rule in tools/static-site/score_reference.py (sign x (2 x Truth - 1) x Conf x Link x Imp x Uniq), a true PageRank walk in tools/static-site/reasonrank.py that measures how much of the corpus depends on a page and deliberately leaves truth, confidence and sign out, and a 0.85-damped tree recursion in src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts that blends an argument's own truth with its sub-arguments. The 0.85 constant is the only item on the page the code shares verbatim; the matrices, the 100 iterations, the feedback term and the sentiment term are either superseded, unimplemented, or refused on stated principle (a vote never moves a score; a model-asserted number is not carried; fallacy pattern-matching is not done). The repo's own docs/wiki/ copy of this page is a different document (the PBworks "Operating System for Truth" version, committed 2026-03-27), so two incompatible versions of the page exist.

| Promise on the page | Status | Where | What the code does |

The walk ranks dependence, not persuasiveness; persuasiveness/relevance/quality are the row rule's Truth, Link, Imp factors. scoring-engine.ts:13 states a third reading: RR(A) = (1-d) x baseTruth + d x f(proSubRank - conSubRank). (static site only). That is a different mechanism for the same consideration, not a contradiction of it: partial. |

The ranking the wiki is about is not refined by votes in any engine. The TS protocol engine does read votes, but only on linkage and only as priority 2 behind a linkage debate: aggregateLinkageVotes is a reputation-weighted mean (LinkageVote.weight 'Based on user reputation in Logic'), clamped to [-1, 1]; FallacyClaimVote.weight is a caller-credibility multiplier. No vote anywhere moves a truth score or a ReasonRank rank. **Corrected on verification:** All static-site citations confirmed verbatim (NO_MOVE at intake.py:90, VOTE_NOTE at render_site.py:1595, BEHAVIOURAL list at confidence.py:38-39, 'Nobody's votes or views are counted here' at 2520, votes.csv has two data rows). But the sentence 'No vote anywhere moves a truth score or a ReasonRank rank' is false for the product, which is what makes contradicted_by_design wrong as the overall status. (1) protocol linkage route: a LinkageVote (weight 'Based on user reputation in Logic', schlicht.ts:72-78) is aggregated into argument.linkageScore (route:121-125), which scoreArgument multiplies into rawImpact (scoring-engine.ts:493) and scoreProtocolBelief sums into proRank/conRank for the belief's truth score (539-540): a vote moves a truth score. (2) fallacy-claims votes: weight is callerCredibilityFor(userId) from calibration.ts (a credibility score; user-supplied weight is rejected at route:99-101); at 60% weighted agreement the counter-argument is published and route:213 calls propagateFromLinkageChange, persisting new impact and parent belief scores in the DB. (3) GroupingVote resolves equivalence pairs at 60% weighted agreement. (4) decision-leverage.ts:45/237: the /leverage ranking is explicitly refined by the number of linkage votes (1/sqrt(1+votes)). So the product incorporates votes and credibility scores to refine scores and rankings; only the static site refuses. Partial, with the static-site refusal correctly documented. |

Sentiment exists nowhere as a scoring input. **Corrected on verification:** Overturned from contradicted_by_design to partial. The static-site refusal is real and quotable: intake.py:90 NO_MOVE = 'A vote never moves a score; the page shows it next to what the analysis says, as what people think.' and ReasonRank(corpus, damping) at reasonrank.py:75 takes no feedback. But the product engine does take reputation-weighted votes as a ReasonRank input: scoring-engine.ts:214-230 resolveLinkageScore falls through to aggregateLinkageVotes(arg.linkageVotes) when no linkage debate exists; :341-349 computes the weight-averaged vote score, where LinkageVote.weight is documented at schlicht.ts:75 as 'Based on user reputation in Logic' (i.e. credibility); :493 multiplies that into rawImpact, which is summed into the parent's reasonRank at :466-486. decision-leverage.ts:45 also uses the linkage vote count (1/sqrt(1 + votes)). So votes and credibility-weighting are implemented in src/core/scoring/ for the linkage factor; the static site refuses them by design; sentiment appears nowhere as a scoring input (grep confirmed). That mix is partial, not a uniform design refusal. |
| Sample code: reason_rank() runs propagate_scores for num_iterations and returns apply_domain_specific_enhancements(scores). | Replaced by another mechanism | tools/static-site/reasonrank.py:103-125; src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:430-512 | The static-site _run() is a convergent power iteration with a restart term and dangling-mass handling, producing a distribution that sums to 1 (test_the_walk_is_a_distribution, test_engines.py:234-237) and is non-negative. The wiki's loop has no restart term and no normalisation: updated = M . (scores x uniq x feedback) x d, so with d = 0.85 and the example's 2x2 matrices (row sums 0.3) every score decays toward 0 after 100 iterations. No post-processing step exists in any engine. |
| Sample code: compute_uniqueness_scores(argument_texts) = 1 - max TF-IDF value per argument, over pro + con texts together. | Replaced by another mechanism | tools/static-site/similarity.py:22-41, 46-51, 63-115, 144-151, 261-269; tools/static-site/test_similarity.py; src/core/scoring/duplication-scoring.ts:199-279, 355; tools/static-site/requirements.txt | Three differences. (1) What is measured: the wiki's expression takes the largest TF-IDF term weight inside each document (toarray().max(axis=1)), which is a property of one text, not a similarity between two, so it does not measure duplication; the code measures pairwise similarity. (2) How: static site uses 0.5 x IDF-weighted Jaccard of content words (STOP list line 52-57) + 0.5 x IDF-weighted cosine over character 4-grams (NGRAM = 4), idf = log((N+1)/(df+1)) floored at 0, IDF_FLOOR = 0.05, JMIN = 0.05, PAIRCAP = 1,500,000; product uses token Jaccard with synonym and negation normalisation. (3) Effect: the static site's computed number carries weight w_ces = 0 until a validity-comparison page says otherwise (similarity.py:39-41, 261-269), so it flags pairs (FLAG 0.45, MERGE 0.70) and never moves a score; the product's duplication-scoring does move the score via uniqueness = 1 - max similarity. sklearn is not a dependency (requirements.txt is openpyxl only); it is imported only by the unwired src/core/ai/similarity.py and overlap_engine.py. |

| Sample code: propagate_scores multiplies M by (scores x uniqueness x feedback) and then by damping_factor, for pro and con separately, using dask arrays chunked at 1000. | Partly implemented | src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:58, 405-428, 457-486, 493-495, 532-541; tools/static-site/reasonrank.py:19-25, 84-125; tools/static-site/test_engines.py:249-296 | Static site: the step weight on a row is Link x Imp x Uniq (defaults 1.0, 0.5, 1.0 at line 95), deliberately excluding the truth of the claim and its confidence (docstring lines 20-25; pinned by test_rank_ignores_the_truth_of_the_claims_it_ranks and test_rank_ignores_confidence_entirely, test_engines.py:249-296); a row with capacity <= 0 is skipped; the share is divided evenly among the pages the row references. Pro and con are not propagated separately. No dask; pure dicts, 'One pass over the corpus; everything else is a lookup' (line 73). **Corrected on verification:** The static-site half of the detail is accurate (line 95 defaults 1.0/0.5/1.0, line 96 skips cap <= 0, line 97 splits evenly, docstring 21-25, tests at 249-296, line 73 quote). But the auditor cited scoring-engine.ts:454-486 and then described only the static site, and the statement 'Pro and con are not propagated separately' and 'the walk refuses to multiply the argument's score into what it transmits' are false for that cited product engine. scoring-engine.ts:463-468 sums rawImpact for pro subs and con subs separately; rawImpact at line 493 is reasonRank x linkage x importance x uniqueness, i.e. the argument's own score times uniqueness times the edge weights (the wiki's M . (scores x uniqueness)); line 470 takes pro minus con; line 486 multiplies the result by DAMPING_FACTOR = 0.85 (line 58) and adds (1-d) x baseTruth; scoreProtocolBelief (532-541) does the same pro/con split at belief level. That is the wiki's propagate step in substance, minus the feedback factor, minus dask (pure recursion on plain arrays), plus a 1/numSubArgs normalisation and a base-truth restart term the wiki lacks. The live product therefore partially implements the claim; only the static site supersedes it with a different rule. |

Equivalence-candidate votes (src/app/api/equivalence-candidates/[id]/votes/route.ts) record a grouped verdict but do not touch scores. So feedback does shift scores in the product (via fallacy votes), still not in the static site. |

**Stale on the page:**

- Four See Also links are wrapped in chatgpt.com conversation URLs (https://chatgpt.com/g/g-zEqCtLTMb-idea-stock-exchange/c/...) and rendered as nested [[text](url)](target) markdown, which GitHub renders oddly; the outer targets should be plain wiki links.
- See Also 'Logical Fallacy Score' points at '#' (lines 116); there is no such wiki page.
- See Also 'Equivalency Score' targets 'Equivalency-Score' but the wiki page file is 'Equivalency-Score:' (trailing colon), so the link may 404 on the wiki.
- The code fence closes with four backticks (line 100, '````') after opening with three (line 37), so the Python block may swallow the following 'Why It Matters' section on some renderers.
- The sample imports numpy, dask.array, spacy (en_core_web_lg) and sklearn TfidfVectorizer. None is a project dependency: tools/static-site/requirements.txt is 'openpyxl' only; sklearn appears only in the unwired src/core/ai/similarity.py and overlap_engine.py; spacy and dask appear nowhere in code.
- doc.sentiment in spaCy en_core_web_lg is 0.0 without a custom pipeline component, so apply_domain_specific_enhancements is a no-op as written even if the libraries were installed.
- propagate_scores has no (1-d) restart term and no normalisation, so with d = 0.85 the example's scores decay toward zero over 100 iterations; the sample does not converge to a PageRank distribution.
- compute_uniqueness_scores takes vectorizer.transform(texts).toarray().max(axis=1), the largest TF-IDF term weight inside each text, which is not a similarity between arguments; it cannot detect a duplicate.
- Variable names M_pro, M_con, M_linkage_pro, M_linkage_con, uniqueness_scores_pro, initial_scores_pro, num_iterations, feedback_data, integrate_feedback, apply_domain_specific_enhancements exist nowhere in the repository. The real names are reasonrank.py: DAMPING, ITERATIONS, TOLERANCE, REF_COLS, ReasonRank._channels/_run/work_queue/top/beliefs_reached; score_reference.py: CONSTS {K, UNARG, DEFLINK, DEFIMP, DEFUNIQ}, Model._contrib/_share/evaluate; scoring-engine.ts: DAMPING_FACTOR, scoreArgument, scoreProtocolBelief, resolveLinkageScore, aggregateLinkageVotes, computeArgumentImpactScore.
- 'credibility scores' as a general feedback input does not exist; the only credibility weights are LinkageVote.weight (src/core/types/schlicht.ts:75, 'Based on user reputation in Logic') and FallacyClaimVote.weight (prisma/schema.prisma:702-706), each confined to one sub-question.
- The repository's own copy, docs/wiki/ReasonRank-Google's-PageRank-for-Arguments.md (committed 2026-03-27, 'Media Skills'), is a different document: the PBworks 'ReasonRank: The Operating System for Truth' page with Argument Score = V x L x I x U, Belief Score = Base + sum(Pro) - sum(Con), tier values 1.0/0.8/0.1/0.0, duplicate U = 0.05, red-herring L = 0.1, and a different sample (calculate_reason_rank). It also contains syntax-highlighter garbage ('Plain textANTLR4BashCC#CSS...' at lines 50 and 126) and every link wrapped in https://www.google.com/search?q=. method.py:194 cites 'wiki: ReasonRank' for the evidence start values 1.0/0.8/0.1, which come from that copy, not from the live page being audited. Two incompatible versions of the page exist.
- docs/ALGORITHMS.md section 7 (lines 380-414) carries a third formula, R(p) = (1-d)/N + d x sum R(q)/L(q) with iterations = 100 restarting evenly over every argument, which method.py:198 lists as a rule the site does not run ('The walk restarts at the beliefs only and steps in proportion to Link x Imp x Uniq'). docs/wiki/Algorithms.md:78-100 has the same under the name argumentrank.
- The product explainer /algorithms/reason-rank (src/app/algorithms/reason-rank/page.tsx) gives no formula, no damping constant and no walk, and its metadata lists Objective Criteria and Confidence Stability among the ReasonRank inputs, neither of which the per-edge formula uses; /how-it-works:218-227 says 'the twelve score dimensions' while all-scores.ts:737 says 'All 11 ReasonRank scores'.
- The page's 'How It Works' bullet 'logical soundness' and the Home.md blurb 'factoring in logic, evidence, and relevance' both presuppose a logical-validity factor the static site has deliberately declined to compute (method.py:186, 197).

**What an updated page must say:**

- Keep the page's framing (PageRank measures links, ReasonRank measures reasoning) but say up front that the code carries the name on three mechanisms, each with its file: the signed row rule (tools/static-site/score_reference.py), the dependence walk (tools/static-site/reasonrank.py), and the damped tree blend (src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts). Quote tools/static-site/reasonrank.py:9-14 on why the walk is 'not a score of a claim at all'.
- Implemented, the row rule: contribution = sign x (2 x Truth - 1) x Confidence x Link x Imp x Uniq; page truth = (POS + w x p0)/(POS + NEG + w); belief score = POS - NEG; page truth capped by the weakest load-bearing component. Constants K = 1, UNARG = 0.5, DEFLINK = 1, DEFIMP = 0.5, DEFUNIQ = 1 (score_reference.py:34). An unargued claim contributes exactly 0, pinned by test_engines.py::test_argument_alone_can_never_leave_the_neutral_point.
- Implemented, the walk: start evenly at the beliefs (1/n each); from a page step to a page it reads with probability proportional to capacity(row) = Link x Imp x Uniq (defaults 1.0, 0.5, 1.0), split evenly among the pages the row references through id, link, imp, uniq, drives, addresses, equiv; with probability 1 - d restart at a belief; mass on a page with no rows returns to the beliefs; d = DAMPING = 0.85; stop when the L1 residual < 1e-12, cap 2000; output sums to 1 and no page outranks a seed belief. Truth and confidence are deliberately absent so a refuted claim does not leave the queue (test_rank_ignores_the_truth_of_the_claims_it_ranks, test_rank_ignores_confidence_entirely) while an argued-down linkage page does move the rank (test_rank_does_follow_a_multiplier_that_is_argued_down). Readouts: 'Relied on (ReasonRank)' and rank place per page, work(page) = rank x (1 - confidence), the 'What to argue next' queue (seeds excluded), 'Most relied on' list, beliefs_reached count, p/<key>.json fields reasonrank and reasonrank_place.
- Implemented, the TypeScript protocol/CBA engine: baseTruth = truthScore x (1 - sum \|fallacy impact\|/100); leaf reasonRank = baseTruth; otherwise subArgScore = clamp(0.5 + 0.5 x (proSubRank - conSubRank)/numSubArgs) and reasonRank = (1 - 0.85) x baseTruth + 0.85 x subArgScore; rawImpact = reasonRank x linkage x importance x uniqueness with importance and uniqueness defaulting to 1.0; belief TruthScore = ProRank/(ProRank + ConRank) (0.5 when empty) plus an evidence term of up to +/-0.2, clamped to [0.01, 0.99]. Linkage resolves debate > reputation-weighted votes clamped [-1, 1] > static field. Say that this engine runs for the Schlicht protocol dashboard, /api/protocol, and likelihood estimates (calculateReasonRankScore), and that the canonical belief page uses computeArgumentImpactScore = sign x childTruth x \|linkage\| x importance x uniqueness x 100 and calculateTruthScoreBreakdown (pro share of argued weight) instead.
- Mark as a third, mostly unwired kernel: src/core/reasonrank/engine.ts (five TDD invariants: propagation continuity, death-match clamping to 0, uniqueness decay, falsified evidence -> 0, edge relevance; claim prior (S + 0.5)/(S + A + 1); DEFAULT_CONFIG epsilon 0.001, maxDepth 100, baseScore 1.0). Only its VERIFICATION_SCORES (VERIFIED 1.0, DISPUTED 0.5, UNVERIFIED 0.5, FALSIFIED 0.0) reach the belief page, through the Evidence Ledger.
- Keep the Variables Needed list verbatim and annotate each item: M_pro/M_con -> superseded by per-row references (reasonrank.py REF_COLS) and tree recursion; M_linkage_* -> superseded by a linkage page per edge (link_id) / resolveLinkageScore; uniqueness_scores_* -> uniqueness page per edge (default 1) / 1 - max similarity; initial_scores_* -> 1/n at the beliefs for the walk, evidence prior p0 for truth; num_iterations 100 -> residual < 1e-12, cap 2000 (static) or none (TS); d 0.85 -> same constant, restart probability (static) or blend weight (TS); feedback_data -> refused: a vote never moves a score.
- Keep the Sample Code verbatim under a heading that says it is the 2025 sketch and is not run, with these corrections beside it: no (1-d) restart term so it decays to zero; uniqueness takes the max TF-IDF term weight per text rather than a pairwise similarity; doc.sentiment is 0.0 in en_core_web_lg; none of numpy, dask, spacy, sklearn is a project dependency. Then give the real duplicate detector: similarity.py CES = 0.5 x IDF-weighted Jaccard over content words + 0.5 x IDF-weighted cosine over character 4-grams, FLAG 0.45, MERGE 0.70, IDF_FLOOR 0.05, JMIN 0.05, PAIRCAP 1,500,000, blend ES = w_ces x CES + (1 - w_ces) x UES with w_ces = 0 unless a validity-comparison page argues otherwise; and the product's duplication-scoring.ts: token Jaccard with synonym/negation normalisation, MECHANICAL_EQUIVALENCE_THRESHOLD 0.85, layer weights 0.4/0.6/0.0, uniqueness = 1 - max similarity, applied at propagation time.
- Replace 'incorporate user feedback (votes, credibility scores)' with the standing rule and its mechanics: a vote is a GitHub issue (contribute.js, intake.py), recorded in content/votes.csv as (key, login, vote, issue, date, on), latest per person wins, shown on the heading line after the score badge and in the home card's vote-share-versus-truth gap, and never moves a score (intake.py NO_MOVE, render_site.py VOTE_NOTE, confidence.py BEHAVIOURAL weight 0). State the one exception by name: in the TS protocol engine a reputation-weighted mean of LinkageVote.score sets an edge's linkage only when no linkage debate exists.
- Replace 'logical soundness' with what runs: the static site computes no logical-validity factor and says why (method.py:186: a pattern match has no page anyone can argue with; a fallacy is argued as a reason to disagree on the row's linkage page); the product applies the confirmation ladder 0.95 / 0.75 / 0.45 / 0.25 only to community fallacy claims that reach CONFIRMED (src/lib/fallacy/claims.ts:94-108), fed into scoreArgument's multiplicative penalty.
- Mark 'sentiment' as withdrawn or proposed, with the reasons: method.py:186 and CLAUDE.md refuse scores moved by text matching or asserted by a model; the only model-asserted scores in the repo (src/core/ai/scorer.py importance = mean of four 1-10 factors x 10, default 50) are imported by nothing in the product.
- Under 'Why It Matters', keep all three bullets and label: objective ranking implemented (votes never count; unargued claims count 0); transparent scoring implemented (every multiplier is an arguable page, constants shown in grey, per-page readouts, /arguments/[id]/score provenance, method.html#reasonrank); dynamic updates partial (evidence and arguments propagate: propagate-belief-scores.ts, changes.py; feedback does not).
- Fix See Also: plain wiki links to ReasonRank, Evidence-Verification-Score-(EVS), Equivalency-Score: (match the actual file name or rename the file), and either drop 'Logical Fallacy Score' or point it at the Truth-Score page's fallacy section; add links to the live explainers /algorithms/reason-rank, /how-it-works, method.html#reasonrank, and to tools/static-site/reasonrank.py.
- Resolve the two-version problem explicitly: either overwrite docs/wiki/ReasonRank-Google's-PageRank-for-Arguments.md with the rewritten live page or fold the PBworks tables (V x L x I x U, Base + sum Pro - sum Con, tiers 1.0/0.8/0.1/0.0, U = 0.05 duplicate, L = 0.1 red herring, retraction cascade scenario) into this page as labelled proposals, and note that method.py:194 lists the tier values as a rule not run and method.py:198 lists even restarting across every argument (ALGORITHMS.md 7) as not run.
- Do not simplify the 0.85: say in one sentence that both engines use it, that the static site uses it as the chance of stepping on rather than restarting at a belief (so 15% of the walk's time restarts), and that the TS engine uses it as the share of an argument's score that comes from its sub-arguments versus its own truth.

### ReasonRank

The page is the short overview of ReasonRank: it names the Belief Score as the primary output, lists five sub-scores it "continuously updates" from (truth, linkage, equivalency, logical fallacy penalties, evidence verification), gives a 20-line Python sample that ranks arguments by (pro_votes - con_votes)/total x evidence_strength, promises transparency, and links four sibling pages through chatgpt.com. It was last edited 2025-08-09 (wiki commit 13d6a99) and is 72 lines. Against the code it is half right in spirit and wrong in almost every specific: both live engines (tools/static-site/score_reference.py and src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts) score from argument structure, never from votes; the published static site states by design that a vote never moves a score and that fallacy pattern-matching is deliberately not done; equivalency is not a multiplier in any engine (uniqueness is); and the Belief Score itself is a signed open-ended total (POS - NEG) in the published site and a 0-1 ratio (ProRank/(ProRank+ConRank)) in the Next.js engine, which disagree about whether an unargued listing counts. The page is a statement of intent whose mechanisms have been replaced wholesale, and nothing on it names a constant, formula or default the code actually uses.

| Promise on the page | Status | Where | What the code does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Intro: ReasonRank "evaluates arguments by breaking them into components and scoring each one using specialized algorithms" and ties "the strength of a conclusion directly to the strength of the evidence supporting it". | Implemented | tools/static-site/score_reference.py:10-24, 34, 152-155, 160-174, 181-212; tools/static-site/evidence.py:27-29, 140-150; tools/static-site/test_engines.py:43-52; src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:58, 108-118, 430-493, 532-593 (RR blend at 486, rawImpact at 493, ProRank/(ProRank+ConRank) at 563, evidence term at 570-574) | Both live engines are component-wise. Static site (engine of record for the published pages): a row contributes sign x (2 x Truth - 1) x Conf x Link x Imp x Uniq (score_reference.py:161, 172-174), where Truth, Link, Imp, Uniq are each the truth score of a separate argued page or a labelled constant (CONSTS = {'K': 1, 'UNARG': 0.5, 'DEFLINK': 1, 'DEFIMP': 0.5, 'DEFUNIQ': 1}, line 34). Page truth = (POS + w x p0)/(POS + NEG + w) (line 155) with p0, w from evidence.py (p0 = 0.5 + 0.5 x ESIW x (2 x ERP/100 - 1), w = k x 2 x ERQ/(ERQ+1), lines 146-148). The "tie to evidence" is literal: test_engines.py:43-52 pins that an unsourced argument graph reads 0.50 at every page forever, and only an evidence prior (etype/erq/erp on the page) can move any score. Next.js engine: scoreArgument (scoring-engine.ts:430) computes RR = (1 - 0.85) x baseTruth + 0.85 x (0.5 + net/count x 0.5) for non-leaf nodes (lines 495-505), rawImpact = RR x linkage x importance x uniqueness (line 511), and scoreProtocolBelief gives TruthScore = ProRank/(ProRank+ConRank) (line 581) plus an evidence term capped at +/-0.2 (lines 587-592). **Corrected on verification:** Static-site half confirmed exactly: CONSTS at line 34 as quoted; _contrib at 172-174 is sign x (2 x Truth - 1) x conf x link x imp x uniq; _share at 154-155 is (pro + w p0)/(pro + con + w); evidence.py:146-148 computes p0 = 0.5 + 0.5 x ESIW x (2 x ERP/100 - 1) and weight = k x replication(ERQ); test_engines.py:43-52 asserts truth == 0.5 and belief == 0.0 at every page of unsourced chains of 2, 5 and 40. Next.js half: formulas are as described (DAMPING_FACTOR = 0.85 at line 58; tier weights max 1.0 so the evidence term is bounded by +/-0.2), but every scoring-engine.ts line number in the citation is wrong by 8-18 lines: RR blend is at 486 not 495-505, rawImpact at 493 not 511, baseTruthScore = proRank/totalRank at 563 not 581, evidence contribution at 570-574 not 587-592. Also note the detail overstates the evidence tie for the Next.js engine: with zero evidence rows, scoreProtocolBelief still moves truth to proRank/(proRank+conRank) from arguments alone (CLAUDE.md records that scoring-engine.ts does not share the static site's rule). The claim stands on the published engine; status kept, citations corrected. |

Next.js: resolveLinkageScore priority chain (scoring-engine.ts:215): a LinkageDebate scored (A - D)/(A + D) in [-1, 1] with 0.0 when no arguments (line 173-194); else aggregated community votes (weighted mean, lines 341-352); else the static field. calculateLinkageFromArguments uses A/(A + D) in [0, 1] with 0.5 when empty (line 269-291); the wizard maps proof 1.0, strong 0.8, context 0.5, weak 0.2, not relevant 0.0 (lines 304-337). Equivalence pages exist as a separate page kind (SPECIAL includes 'equivalence', line 35) whose truth is shown on the Similar Beliefs table as "Equiv" (render_site.py:975) and used to pick a merge candidate at EQUIV_MERGE (line 140), never to weight a row. similarity.py computes the wiki's CES lexically (Jaccard + char-4-gram cosine, IDF weighted) and states w_ces is read from a VCA page and is 0 when none exists, so it changes no score. Next.js calculateBeliefEquivalencyScore (all-scores.ts:700) blends 0.4 x mechanical + 0.6 x semantic with MECHANICAL_THRESHOLD 0.85 and relationship bands 0.90/0.70/0.45/0.20, and is a grouping aid, not a factor. |

Next.js: the detector is declared "an argument, not a penalty... no score is ever docked because a detector fired" and returns no numeric fields (fallacy-detector.ts:1-9); a filed claim is a draft counter-argument in the linkage sub-debate and changes nothing (fallacy-claims route:12-15); only a claim confirmed by weighted consensus at CONSENSUS_THRESHOLD = 0.6 with CONSENSUS_QUORUM = 3 (consensus.ts:31-34) publishes a counter-argument of strength 0.8 (major) or 0.5 (minor) (claims.ts:137-139) and propagates through linkage. A validity ladder exists (VALIDITY_NO_FALLACY 0.95, MINOR 0.75, MAJOR 0.45, MULTIPLE 0.25, claims.ts:100-110) and scoreArgument does apply baseTruth = truthScore x (1 - sum\|impact\|/100) (scoring-engine.ts:431-436), but the bridge confirmedClaimsToDetectedFallacies is called by nothing outside its test; the belief page fetches confirmed claims for an inline note only (fetch-belief.ts:87-92). So the only fallacy effect that reaches a live number is the confirmed counter-argument in the linkage sub-debate, which is a scored argument, not a penalty. |

Next.js: EVIDENCE_TYPE_WEIGHTS T0 0.05, T1 1.0, T2 0.75, T3 0.5, T4 0.25 (scoring-engine.ts:108-114); calculateEVS = ESIW x log2(ERQ + 1) x ECRS x ERP (line 124-137), which differs from the wiki's linear ERQ; scoreProtocolBelief adds (supporting - weakening)/count x 0.2 to the argument ratio (lines 587-592). |
| Belief Score section: "Purpose: Reflect the current best estimate of a belief's validity given all available arguments and evidence." | Partly implemented | tools/static-site/method.py limits list ("A truth score is not a probability"); src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:532-545 | The static site's method page explicitly disclaims this: "A truth score is not a probability. It is a share of argued weight around a starting point. It has the shape of a probability and does not have the calibration of one. Nothing here has been checked against outcomes." The Next.js docstring claims the PageRank probability interpretation (a random walker lands on a pro-conclusion, scoring-engine.ts:39-41, 541-543). The wiki should not promise an estimate of validity where the engine of record says it is a share of argued weight. |
| Argument Ranking Algorithm: rank_arguments uses pro_votes and con_votes; score = (pro_votes/total - con_votes/total) x evidence_strength; sorted descending. | Refused by design | tools/static-site/intake.py:17-19, 90; tools/static-site/render_site.py:71-72, 1595; tools/static-site/method.py limits list ("Votes are counted and shown, and never scored"); src/app/how-it-works/page.tsx:211; tools/static-site/score_reference.py:160-174 | No engine ranks by votes. The static site records GitHub votes in content/votes.csv and states in three places that a vote never moves a score: intake.py:17 and NO_MOVE at line 90; render_site.py:71-72 ("shown, never scored") and VOTE_NOTE line 1595; method.py limits ("No vote enters any number here: every score comes from the structure of the argument and from what pages cite"). how-it-works/page.tsx:211 says "Not by counting upvotes, because a vote measures...". The real ranking key for a row is the signed contribution sign x (2 x Truth - 1) x Conf x Link x Imp x Uniq (score_reference.py:172-174), where Truth is the claim page's own argued truth, not a vote share. Item by item against the sample: (a) pro_score - con_score (a vote share in [-1, 1]) is replaced by (2 x Truth - 1) of the claim's page; (b) evidence_strength as a single multiplier in [0, 1] is replaced by four argued multipliers Conf, Link, Imp, Uniq with defaults 1.0 (conf), 1, 0.5, 1 and by the evidence prior p0 on the claim's page; (c) total_votes == 0 -> score 0 corresponds to the engine's rule that an unargued claim at UNARG = 0.5 contributes exactly 0, so that one property survives, by a different mechanism. The only vote-weighted numbers anywhere are the linkage vote aggregate in scoring-engine.ts:341-352 (priority 2 in resolveLinkageScore, used only when no LinkageDebate exists) and fallacy/equivalence consensus (consensus.ts), which decide publication, not score. |
| Argument Ranking Algorithm: "Strength score: assumed to be between 0 and 1". | Partly implemented | tools/static-site/score_reference.py:34, 172-174; src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:173-194, 304-337 | Every static-site multiplier is a truth score in [0, 1] or a constant in that range, so the bound holds there; but the Next.js scoreLinkageDebate and the wizard return linkage in [-1, 1] (a negative linkage flips the row), and computeArgumentImpactScore takes \|linkageScore\| so that only side controls direction (scoring-engine.ts:779-795). The wiki's single evidence_strength has no counterpart; the engines split it into Link, Imp, Uniq and Conf. |

The method page is honest that some inputs are typed rather than argued: etype/erq/erp classifications, cost magnitudes, value rankings, the four objective-criterion labels, the load-bearing flag and a belief's axis position (method.py limits list; render_site.py TYPED_INPUTS 1109-1123). The wiki EVS page says category choice is itself debated pro/con; the code types it and the method page names that as a limitation. |

The static site adds a sensitivity sweep (which single input would change the answer) and ReasonRank-as-dependence (reasonrank.py: capacity(row) = Link x Imp x Uniq, DAMPING = 0.85, walk seeded at the beliefs; work(page) = rank x (1 - confidence)); the Next.js app adds Decision Leverage per edge and an impact-provenance page per argument. |

Auditability is now the conformance suite (24 pages, five constants, eighteen tiers, python3 conformance.py). The Next.js app has its own /algorithms index (reason-rank, linkage-scores, importance-score, truth-scores, evidence-scores, unique-scores, fallacy-detection, decision-leverage, media-truth-score, etc.).
