| See Also: "Logical Fallacy Score" is a wiki page. | Not implemented | none found (the link target is "#"); nearest: src/app/algorithms/fallacy-detection/page.tsx; src/lib/fallacy/catalog.ts:1-30 | No wiki page by that name exists in the checkout, the link resolves to "#", and no module computes a Logical Fallacy Score. What exists is a fallacy catalog of named entries with targetFactor in {relevance, logical-validity, evidence-quality} and severity in {minor, major}, an accusation template with six required fields and MIN_EXPLANATION_LENGTH = 40, a 60% weighted consensus bar with quorum 3, a caller-credibility multiplier in [0.3, 1.4] (calibration.ts: accuracyFactor = 2 x accuracy after 3 resolved, balanceFactor = 0.4 + 0.9 x sideBalance after 5 flagged), and the validity ladder 0.95/0.75/0.45/0.25. The Truth-Score wiki page's 'ImprovedFallacyDetector' regex sample has a distant cousin in src/lib/agent-ingest/fallacy-detector.ts (six regex rules), but that module drafts counter-arguments and returns no numbers. |
| Implicit in the title and the sibling page: ReasonRank is a PageRank-style iterative propagation with a damping factor. | Implemented | tools/static-site/reasonrank.py:1-45, 49-51, 85-120; src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:58, 495-505 | Two different things carry the name. Static site reasonrank.py is a true PageRank walk: DAMPING = 0.85, ITERATIONS cap 2000, TOLERANCE 1e-12, seeded evenly at the beliefs, stepping along rows in proportion to capacity = Link x Imp x Uniq (truth and confidence deliberately excluded, lines 20-25), dangling mass returned to the seeds; it measures how much of the corpus depends on a page and changes no truth score ("Nothing in this file changes a truth score", line 8). Next.js scoring-engine.ts uses DAMPING_FACTOR = 0.85 as a blend, not an iteration: RR = 0.15 x baseTruth + 0.85 x subArgScore for nodes with children, RR = baseTruth for leaves, no fixed-point loop (lines 495-505, pinned by tests/unit/core/scoring/scoring-engine.test.ts:141-215). The sibling wiki page's num_iterations default 100 matches neither (2000 cap / none). |

**Stale on the page:**

- Line 54: the Python code block closes with four backticks (````) instead of three, so the rest of the page may render inside the fence on some Markdown renderers.
- Lines 68-71: all four See Also links are double-wrapped [[text](chatgpt.com URL)](target); the outer links point at chatgpt.com/g/g-zEqCtLTMb-idea-stock-exchange/c/..., which is a private custom-GPT conversation, not the wiki. Replace with plain [[Evidence Verification Score (EVS)]] style wiki links.
- Line 69: the "Logical Fallacy Score" link target is "#" and the chatgpt.com URL is a conversation id (6897d671-...); no such wiki page exists in the checkout.
- Line 71: the link text says "Evidence-to-Conclusion Linkage Score (ECLS)" but the sibling page is titled "Evidence to Conclusion Linkage Score (ECLS)" under the file name Evidence-to-Conclusion-Relevance-Score.md; the two names for the same thing should be reconciled.
- Line 22: "Logical fallacy penalties" names a mechanism the published site refuses by design (render_site.py:1021-1026) and the Next.js app has replaced with scored accusations (fallacy-detection explainer); the term 'penalty' no longer matches any code path that reaches a published number.
- Line 21: "Equivalency scores" as a Belief Score input: the engines' multiplier is called uniqueness (uniq_id / DEFUNIQ / uniquenessScore); equivalence pages exist but weight nothing.
- Lines 33-53: the rank_arguments sample ranks by pro_votes / con_votes; the published site records votes in content/votes.csv and states a vote never moves a score (intake.py:17, render_site.py:1595, method.py limits). The sample contradicts the owner's standing rule and should be kept only if labelled as the 2025 proposal that was rejected.
- Line 47: evidence_strength as one number in [0, 1] has no counterpart; the code splits it into Conf, Link, Imp, Uniq plus the evidence prior p0.
- Line 62: "see sidebar" and "linked across the wiki": the built site's WIKI table (build_pages.py:30-41) links every rule to method.html anchors, not to the GitHub wiki; only src/app/page.tsx:728,735 still links to the wiki.
- Sibling page ReasonRank:-Google's-PageRank-for-Arguments.md (cross-referenced from this page's title) depends on numpy, dask, spaCy en_core_web_lg and scikit-learn; tools/static-site/requirements.txt lists only openpyxl>=3.1 and the published scorer has no model and no network (similarity.py:22-24). Its num_iterations=100 default matches neither reasonrank.py (2000 cap, 1e-12 tolerance) nor scoring-engine.ts (no iteration). Its sentiment multiplier (1 + doc.sentiment) is exactly the model-asserted number the engine refuses (CLAUDE.md on src/core/ai).
- Cross-referenced Truth-Score.md and Quantifying-Reason page link the Logical Fallacy Score and EVS to sites.google.com/d/1f62q1ZSjo3Kcb7Pj5OHCNTEfaacTWuKD/... edit URLs, which are private Google Sites editor links.
- The page has no date or revision note; last wiki commit 13d6a99 on 2025-08-09, while the engines it describes were rewritten through 2026 (evidence prior, confidence, sensitivity, ReasonRank walk, conformance suite, pro-share fix in all-scores.ts).

**What an updated page must say:**

- Keep the title, the Sagan and Hume quotes and the Guiding Principles section as written; they are the owner's framing and nothing in the code contradicts them.
- Belief Score section: state that two engines exist and name them. Engine of record for the published site: tools/static-site/score_reference.py. Row contribution = sign x (2 x Truth - 1) x Conf x Link x Imp x Uniq (line 161); POS = sum of positive contributions, NEG = magnitude of negative ones, a row landing on the side its sign puts it on (lines 176-179); belief score = POS - NEG (open-ended); truth = (POS + w x p0)/(POS + NEG + w) capped by min() over load-bearing components with lb = Y (lines 207-209). Constants: K = 1, UNARG = 0.5, DEFLINK = 1, DEFIMP = 0.5, DEFUNIQ = 1 (line 34); confidence default 1.0 (line 137). Next.js engine (src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts): leaf RR = baseTruth; non-leaf RR = 0.15 x baseTruth + 0.85 x (0.5 + 0.5 x (proSubRank - conSubRank)/count) with DAMPING_FACTOR = 0.85 (lines 58, 495-505); rawImpact = RR x linkage x importance x uniqueness (line 511); TruthScore = ProRank/(ProRank + ConRank), 0.5 when empty, plus evidence term (supporting - weakening)/count x 0.2, clamped [0.01, 0.99] (lines 581-593).
- Belief Score sub-score list: replace the five bullets with the real factors and where each lives. Truth: the claim's own page (UNARG = 0.5 when none). Linkage: a linkage page (DEFLINK = 1) in the static site; (A - D)/(A + D) in [-1, 1] with 0.0 default for a LinkageDebate, A/(A + D) with 0.5 default for simple linkage arguments, and the wizard map proof 1.0 / strong 0.8 / context 0.5 / weak 0.2 / irrelevant 0.0 in Next.js. Importance: an importance page = max over listed interests of Validity x Bears (DEFIMP = 0.5), or deriveImportanceFromBeliefScore = (overallScore + 100)/200 in Next.js. Uniqueness: a uniqueness page (DEFUNIQ = 1) or uniquenessScore default 1.0; this, not equivalency, is the redundancy factor. Confidence: confidence.py structural weights grounding 0.30, two_sided 0.20, scrutiny 0.20, breadth 0.15, depth 0.05, sourcing 0.05, testability 0.05; the wiki's behavioural signals carry weight 0 and are reported as no data. Evidence verification: evidence.py prior p0 = 0.5 + 0.5 x ESIW x (2 x ERP/100 - 1), w = k x 2 x ERQ/(ERQ + 1) capped at 2k, ESIW = the wiki's 16 tiers plus record = 0.90, NOSOURCE = 0.0; Overall EVS = ESIW x ECRS x ERQ x (ERP/100) still computed and shown, read by nothing. Logical fallacies: a filed accusation is a draft counter-argument (changes nothing); confirmed at 60% weighted agreement with quorum 3 it publishes a counter-argument of strength 0.8 (major) / 0.5 (minor) into the linkage sub-debate; the validity ladder 0.95 / 0.75 / 0.45 / 0.25 and the scoreArgument penalty branch exist but are not wired to the live page (confirmedClaimsToDetectedFallacies is unused); the static site deliberately does no fallacy pattern matching (render_site.py:1021-1026). Mark the fallacy-penalty bullet as proposed / not in the scoring path unless the owner wires it.
- State the one rule the owner keeps rediscovering, with its test: a claim nobody has argued sits at 0.5 and contributes 0, so by induction an argument graph with no evidence declared reads 0.50 at every page forever (test_engines.py:43-52 test_argument_alone_can_never_leave_the_neutral_point); only the evidence prior on a claim's page can move a score. Note that scoring-engine.ts does not share this rule (A/(A + D) over stored truth scores lets an unargued listing raise a score) and that CLAUDE.md records the disagreement as an unresolved decision.
- Argument Ranking Algorithm section: keep the owner's rank_arguments sample verbatim but label it as the 2025 vote-based proposal that the published site rejected. Add the real ranking key beside it: a row's signed contribution sign x (2 x Truth - 1) x Conf x Link x Imp x Uniq, and the fact that every per-row table on a belief page sorts by that score descending with nulls last (BELIEF_PAGE_RULES Rule 8; src/features/belief-analysis/lib/ranking.ts). State the vote rule: votes are recorded in content/votes.csv (one per page, on, login; latest wins), shown on the heading line after the score badge, ranked on the home page as the gap between vote share and truth score once three people have voted, and never enter a score (intake.py:17, render_site.py:1595, method.py limits). Mention the one place votes do enter a number in the Next.js engine: aggregateLinkageVotes as priority 2 of resolveLinkageScore when no linkage debate exists. Fix the four-backtick fence.
- Add a ReasonRank-proper section: the name is borrowed for two things. (a) The recursive scoring rule above. (b) reasonrank.py, a true PageRank walk seeded evenly at the beliefs, stepping along rows by capacity = Link x Imp x Uniq (truth and confidence deliberately excluded so the ranking measures dependence, not agreement), DAMPING = 0.85, iteration cap 2000, tolerance 1e-12, dangling mass returned to the seeds; it ranks how much of the corpus rests on a page and changes no truth score; work(page) = rank x (1 - confidence) is the work queue. Every page's Scoring Engine table prints 'Relied on (ReasonRank)' and 'Work value'. The Next.js app's Decision Leverage (src/core/scoring/decision-leverage.ts, /leverage) is the analogous work queue there.
- Transparency section: keep all three bullets; update the third. Every multiplier is a page id or a labelled constant printed in grey; every page exports <key>.json; the Scoring Engine table lists Argument score, Evidence score, Prediction contribution, Belief score, Positive total, Negative total, Starting point, Weight of the starting point, Truth score argued, Truth score; a sensitivity sweep names which single input would change the answer; conformance.py publishes a 24-page corpus, five constants and eighteen tiers so a port in any language can be checked (python3 conformance.py; --write after a deliberate rule change). Replace 'linked across the wiki (see sidebar)' with: the built site links every rule to its own method page (build_pages.py WIKI -> method.html anchors) and the app to /algorithms/*; add the honest limits list from method.py (typed inputs: etype/erq/erp classifications, cost magnitudes, value rankings, criterion labels, load-bearing flag, axis position; a truth score is not a probability).
- Completeness: say what makes a belief complete, since rankings rest on it: publish.CORE_BAR = reasons to agree, reasons to disagree, evidence, objective criteria, testable predictions or a falsifiability test, costs and benefits, interests on either side (publish.py:13-21); every belief is published, drafts carry a draft mark and are listed in data/drafts.json; nothing drafted is deleted.
- See Also: replace the chatgpt.com double-wrapped links with plain wiki links to Evidence-Verification-Score-(EVS), Equivalency-Score:, Evidence-to-Conclusion-Relevance-Score, Confidence-Stability-Scores and ReasonRank:-Google's-PageRank-for-Arguments; drop or create the Logical Fallacy Score page (the nearest living explainer is /algorithms/fallacy-detection and src/lib/fallacy/catalog.ts). Add links to the method page of the published site and to /algorithms/reason-rank.
- Superseded mechanisms to name explicitly with their replacements: vote-share scoring -> signed argued contribution with votes shown not scored; equivalency as a factor -> uniqueness multiplier, with equivalence pages used for merge candidates and w_ces = 0 until a VCA page exists; fallacy penalty -> fallacy accusation as a scored counter-argument confirmed by 60% weighted consensus (ladder and penalty branch exist, unwired); 'see sidebar' wiki links -> the site's own method page and conformance suite; the sibling page's dask/spaCy/sklearn sample and sentiment multiplier -> a model-free lexical similarity (Jaccard + char-4-gram cosine, IDF weighted) that changes no score, with model-asserted numbers refused by policy (CLAUDE.md on src/core/ai).

### Truth-Score

The page defines the Truth Score as a combination of a Logical Fallacy Score (users flag fallacies, discuss each accusation, ML pattern-matches text, results aggregate into a score with a confidence interval) and an Evidence Verification Score, and gives two Python sketches: a stub scorer with -2/0/+2 confidence bands and a spaCy keyword detector. It has not been edited since 2025-08-09 (the docs/wiki copy was committed 2026-03-27 unchanged). Against the code it is roughly one-third implemented, one-third superseded by a different mechanism, and one-third explicitly refused: the Next.js app replaced "fallacy score" with structured fallacy claims that are arguments (a six-field accusation template, a draft counter-argument in the linkage sub-debate, a 60%-weighted community vote, a validity ladder of 0.95/0.75/0.45/0.25), while the published static site (tools/static-site) computes no fallacy term at all and says in integrity.py, method.py and on every belief page why pattern-matching is deliberately not done. The Truth Score formula itself exists in three incompatible forms (app: (LV + V)/2 with LV a pro share; static site: (POS + w*p0)/(POS + NEG + w) capped by load-bearing premises; ALGORITHMS.md: (LV x EQ x VL) +/- CW on -1..+1), none of which is the wiki's.

The static site has no fallacy term anywhere: page truth = (POS + w*p0) / (POS + NEG + w) over signed rows, then min(argued, weakest load-bearing component) (score_reference.py:152-159, 207-209). | Component 2: "Evidence Verification Score measures how strongly a claim is supported by independent, replicable evidence (e.g., blind studies, scenario comparisons)." | Implemented | tools/static-site/evidence.py:79-158; src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:108-136; src/features/belief-analysis/data/fetch-belief.ts:366-379 | Two implementations with different arithmetic. Static site (evidence.py): ESIW is the EVS wiki's 16-tier table verbatim (statistics 0.90, rct 0.85, meta 0.80, observational 0.75, historical 0.70, expert_data 0.65, expert_claim 0.60, anecdote 0.55, logic 0.50, analogy 0.45, norm 0.40, intuition 0.35, news 0.30, survey 0.25, eyewitness 0.20, visual 0.15, artifact 0.10) plus one marked addition 'record' 0.90 (statute, holding, rule, sworn filing); NOSOURCE = 0.0. The score is a prior, not a multiplier: p0 = 0.5 + 0.5 x ESIW x (2 x ERP/100 - 1), weight w = K x replication(ERQ) with replication(n) = 2n/(n+1) (REP_CAP 2.0), defaults erq 1 and erp 100 when absent, so a page declaring nothing starts at (0.5, k) and the strongest source is confined to [0.05, 0.95]. The wiki's Overall EVS = ESIW x ECRS x ERQ x (ERP/100) is computed by evs() and printed in every Evidence Ledger but no score reads it (lines 72-75, 154-158). App (scoring-engine.ts): calculateEVS = ESIW x log2(ERQ + 1) x ECRS x ERP with a five-tier table T0 0.05, T1 1.0, T2 0.75, T3 0.5, T4 0.25 (default 0.5 for an unknown tier), then multiplied on the belief page by verificationWeight(verificationStatus) from src/core/scoring/evidence-exposure.ts (fetch-belief.ts:376). Differences from the wiki's own EVS page: log2(ERQ+1) in the app versus linear ERQ in the wiki versus bounded 2n/(n+1) in the static site; 5 tiers versus 16. "Blind studies" and "scenario comparisons" are not tiers in either table; the nearest thing to scenario comparison is the predictions section, scored by the same row formula (score_reference.py:205-207). |

The static site does not integrate two scores at all: evidence sets where a page's truth STARTS (p0, w) and arguments, evidence rows and prediction rows all pass through the same signed formula into one share (pos, neg = _split(args + evid + preds); raw = _share(pos, neg)). The docstring at evidence.py:6-19 explains why that structure is load-bearing: a row contributes (2 x Truth - 1), so an unsourced graph reads 0.50 at every page forever, and only an evidence prior can move it; test_engines.py::test_argument_alone_can_never_leave_the_neutral_point pins this for chains of 2, 5 and 40 pages. docs/ALGORITHMS.md:316-333 gives a third formula, TS = (LV x EQ x VL) +/- CW on a -1..+1 scale, which nothing implements (method.py:195 lists it as not run). Flagging is deliberately not a button: validateFallacyClaimInput requires fallacyType (a catalog slug), quotedText, explanation (MIN_EXPLANATION_LENGTH 40 characters), missingElements, consequences, and evidenceLinks (non-empty for straw-man, false-dilemma, slippery-slope, cherry-picking, moving-goalposts). The body may not carry severity, weight, consensus, status or score (422, route.ts:131-143). Filing creates a LinkageArgument on side 'disagree' with status 'draft', pattern = slug, targetFactor and fallacyType set, plus the FallacyClaim and an audit log row, and returns "Filed. The accusation itself is a row in the target's linkage sub-debate, but LinkageArgument has no children, so there is nowhere to argue for and against the accusation except the vote. The static site gets the wiki's wording closer: the accusation is a reason to disagree on the linkage page and that reason has its own page with its own reasons (build_pages.py:749), but there is no accusation object or vote. |

Equivalence detection exists separately: agent ingest computes textSimilarity against EQUIVALENCE_CANDIDATE_THRESHOLD and records equivalenceCandidates, resolved by the same 60% vote on /api/equivalence-candidates/[id]/votes; the static site's similarity.py is lexical (IDF-weighted Jaccard over content words averaged with cosine over character 4-grams; FLAG 0.45, MERGE 0.70) and by its own docstring "changes no score" and only points. Note that the /algorithms/fallacy-detection explainer (page.tsx:139-140) says "a fallacy confirmed once stays confirmed for the whole cluster"; nothing in the code does that, so the explainer over-claims. |

What runs is six regular expressions, applied only to agent-submitted claims (statement + rationale, ingest.ts:234), never to human submissions: ad-hominem (a slur list: liar, idiot, stupid, corrupt, dishonest, evil, fraud, shill, crook), appeal-to-popularity (everyone knows / most people agree / it is widely believed), false-cause (correlat... near caus.../proves/proof within a sentence), overgeneralization (always / never / without exception / in every case), cherry-picking (one study / a single study / the only evidence / one survey), anecdotal-evidence (i know a / my uncle / personal experience). Each hit becomes a draft LinkageArgument authored by the agent 'system:fallacy-detector'; the FallacyDetection type deliberately has no numeric field, and a test asserts that. The detector's accuracy is never measured and it has no track record. sentence-transformer code exists in src/core/ai/similarity.py and overlap_engine.py but is for claim overlap, not fallacies, and nothing imports it (CLAUDE.md). |
| Algorithm step 6: "Score flagged arguments based on supporting/opposing sub-arguments." | Partly implemented | src/lib/consensus.ts:35-59; src/lib/fallacy/calibration.ts:22-79; src/lib/fallacy/caller-record.ts:10-33; src/app/api/fallacy-claims/[id]/votes/route.ts:125-214 | Flagged arguments are scored by weighted votes, not sub-arguments. Vote weight = callerCredibility(record) = clamp(accuracyFactor x balanceFactor, CREDIBILITY_FLOOR 0.3, CREDIBILITY_CEILING 1.4), where accuracyFactor = 2 x (upheld / resolved) once resolved >= MIN_RESOLVED_FOR_ACCURACY 3 (else 1), balanceFactor = 0.4 + 0.9 x sideBalance once flagged >= MIN_FLAGGED_FOR_BALANCE 5 (else 1), sideBalance = (min(agreeFlags, disagreeFlags) + 1) / (max + 1); side is read from the accused argument's side in its parent debate (caller-record.ts:25-26). The weight is computed before the vote is stored so voting on your own claim cannot lift it (votes/route.ts:125-127). A new user is exactly 1.0. When the outcome changes, the counter-argument's status is set published/rejected/draft and strength 0.8 (major) or 0.5 (minor), then propagateFromLinkageChange reruns the linkage aggregate. This is the one place in the product where a vote moves a score; the static site's standing rule is that a vote never does (CLAUDE.md; votes are shown as counts beside the score badge). |
| Algorithm step 7: "Aggregate results into a Logical Fallacy Score (confidence interval)." | Not implemented | none found for a fallacy-score confidence interval; nearest: prisma/schema.prisma:679-680 (FallacyClaim.consensus Float?), src/lib/fallacy/claims.ts:100-109 (ladder) | There is no aggregated Logical Fallacy Score and no confidence interval on it. Per claim the code stores consensus (weighted agree share at last resolution, null until quorum) and a three-state status; per argument the only aggregate is the ladder multiplier over confirmed claims. The confidence_interval fields in /api/protocol and /api/scoring are belief-level metrics (belief.metrics.confidenceInterval), unrelated to fallacies. The wiki's five bands (score < -2 very likely fallacious, < 0 possibly fallacious, == 0 no indication, < 2 possibly sound, else very likely sound) appear nowhere in src/, tools/ or tests/. |
| Example Code: Basic Logical Fallacy Scoring (evaluate_sub_argument_score summed over four fallacies; bands at -2, 0, 2; FallacyDetector.detect stub) | Not implemented | none found; the shipped equivalents are src/lib/fallacy/claims.ts:100-127 and src/lib/consensus.ts:30-59 | Both functions in the sketch are TODO stubs returning 0 and the sample loop runs over an empty dict. The shipped arithmetic is different in kind: an accusation does not add a signed per-fallacy score, it is confirmed or not at 0.6 weighted share with quorum 3, and a confirmed set maps to one multiplier (0.95 / 0.75 / 0.45 / 0.25) that scoreArgument applies as truthScore x (1 - penalty). There is no additive score across fallacy types and no symmetric 'sound' side: a rejected accusation leaves the target untouched ("the accusation failed its own debate", votes/route.ts:231) and only lowers the accuser's accuracy rate. |
| Example Code: Enhanced Fallacy Detector (spaCy en_core_web_sm sentence split + regex over fallacy-name keywords such as 'ad hominem', 'red herring', 'post hoc') | Replaced by another mechanism | src/lib/agent-ingest/fallacy-detector.ts:35-78; package.json / pipeline/requirements.txt (no spaCy) | spaCy is not a dependency of the app, the static site, or the pipeline (grep finds spacy only in _archive and in src/core/ai's unwired sentence-transformer modules). The shipped detector matches the behaviour, not the name: the sketch's patterns look for the literal phrases 'ad hominem', 'personal attack', 'red herring', 'post hoc', so none of its own four example sentences ("he's a convicted criminal", "Dr. Smith said it", "what about all the good things", "my lucky socks") would match and the example prints four empty dicts. fallacy-detector.ts instead matches insults, 'everyone knows', correlat.../caus..., universals, 'one study', 'my uncle'. It covers 6 types, not the sketch's 4, and shares none of the sketch's keywords. It also never scores: output is a drafted counter-argument string plus the matched text. |

The only data about fallacy accuracy the system accumulates is per-caller: FallacyClaim.status by submittedById, read by callerRecordFor (caller-record.ts:10-29). The detector agent's own hits are never scored for accuracy. |

The catalog's notFallacyWhen fields are the only domain guard ('ad hominem' is not a fallacy when a relevant conflict of interest is documented; 'appeal to authority' is not when the expert is in-domain and consistent with the evidence), and they are text shown to voters, not code. The static site's refusal is itself a domain argument: on a corpus about officials acting on financial interests, an ad hominem matcher fires on the legitimate central claim. |

Draft rows are displayed on the linkage page but excluded from the aggregate (linkage/page.tsx:412-421). Every filing, vote and resolution writes an auditLog row. |

**Stale on the page:**

- Lines 5, 6, 137, 138: four links to sites.google.com/d/1f62q1ZSjo3Kcb7Pj5OHCNTEfaacTWuKD/... (Logical Fallacy Score and Evidence Verification Score). The Logical Fallacy Score link uses two different page ids on line 5 (1FkEbfeEgEkR9-L2LsaFgUeXIJM1j83Rt) and line 138 (14XmYT4seJJsG8a0v-E4MI3GiGNJk57s2). The wiki has its own Evidence-Verification-Score-(EVS) page and should link it internally; no Logical-Fallacy-Score wiki page exists at all.
- Lines 137-138 (See Also): doubled-link syntax [[text](url)](url) renders as a link inside a link.
- Line 78: the first code block is closed with four backticks (````) instead of three.
- Line 85-90: the Enhanced Fallacy Detector imports spacy and loads en_core_web_sm; spaCy is not a dependency of the Next.js app, tools/static-site or pipeline/ (only _archive and the unwired src/core/ai modules reference transformer models).
- Lines 91-96: the detector's keyword lists are the names of fallacies ('ad hominem', 'red herring', 'post hoc', 'correlation is not causation'), so the four example sentences on lines 113-118 match nothing and the sample prints four empty dicts; the shipped detector (src/lib/agent-ingest/fallacy-detector.ts) matches the behaviour instead.
- The name 'Logical Fallacy Score' is used by nothing in src/ or tools/; the code names are FallacyClaim, FALLACY_CATALOG, logicalValidityMultiplier, VALIDITY_NO_FALLACY/MINOR/MAJOR/MULTIPLE, fallacyPenalty, logicalValidityScore, callerCredibility.
- 'Confidence interval' for the fallacy score (line 34, line 77 'confidence_interval'): no such field exists for fallacies; the confidence_interval in /api/protocol and /api/scoring is a belief metric.
- Line 1-3: 'quantifies the strength of an argument' is the wrong object; in both engines the Truth Score is a page score (belief or claim) and an argument's strength is its row contribution or impactScore.
- Cross-reference: Home.md line 13 links this page as 'Truth Score: Logical Fallacy + Evidence Verification' and ReasonRank:-Google's-PageRank-for-Arguments.md line 116 and ReasonRank.md line 69 link 'Logical Fallacy Score' to a chatgpt.com conversation URL (https://chatgpt.com/g/g-zEqCtLTMb-idea-stock-exchange/c/6897d671-...) with an empty (#) target.
- Quantifying-Reason:-The-Power-of-Truth-Scores.md repeats this page almost verbatim (same two components, same four fallacies, same algorithm steps, same sites.google.com links, plus a link to a #logical-fallacy-score anchor on the wiki home that does not exist); the two pages should be reconciled or one should point at the other.
- Red herring (line 22) and appeal to emotion / circular reasoning (docs/ALGORITHMS.md:433-447) are named as scored fallacies but are not catalog entries; circular reasoning is a structural check (tools/static-site/integrity.py:35) that refuses the build rather than a fallacy type.

**What an updated page must say:**

- Keep the two-component framing but rename and re-anchor it: the two channels that exist are Logical Validity (the argument tree; app: logicalValidityScore in src/core/scoring/all-scores.ts:299-316 = clamp(0.01, 0.99, proWeight / (proWeight + conWeight)), weight = max(0, truthScore x (1 - fallacyPenalty)), 0.5 when nothing argued) and Verification (the evidence ledger; verificationTruthScore lines 318-335, contribution = (evsScore > 0 ? evsScore : 0.1) x linkageScore). Overall = (LV + V) / 2 when both have rows, else the one that has rows, else 0.5, clamped to [0.01, 0.99]. State that on the live belief page fallacyPenalty is never passed (fetch-belief.ts:381-388) so LV is the pro share alone.
- State the published static site's rule in full, since it is what myklob.github.io serves: row = sign x (2 x Truth - 1) x Conf x Link x Imp x Uniq; constants K = 1, UNARG = 0.5, DEFLINK = 1, DEFIMP = 0.5, DEFUNIQ = 1 (tools/static-site/score_reference.py:34); a row for an unargued claim contributes exactly 0; page truth (argued) = (POS + w x p0) / (POS + NEG + w); page truth = min(argued, weakest load-bearing component with its own page) (lines 207-209); belief score = POS - NEG. Arguments, evidence and predictions all go through this one formula (lines 194-207). Add the consequence and its test: an unsourced argument graph reads 0.50 at every page forever (evidence.py:6-19; test_engines.py::test_argument_alone_can_never_leave_the_neutral_point).
- Replace the Google-Sites EVS link with the wiki's own EVS page and give the two implemented forms with their numbers. Static site (evidence.py): p0 = 0.5 + 0.5 x ESIW x (2 x ERP/100 - 1); w = K x 2n/(n + 1) (REP_CAP 2.0); defaults erq 1, erp 100, ESIW 0.0 when no type is named; the 16 wiki tiers 0.90 to 0.10 plus the marked addition 'record' 0.90; p0 confined to [0.05, 0.95]; Overall EVS = ESIW x ECRS x ERQ x (ERP/100) printed in every Evidence Ledger, read by no score. App (scoring-engine.ts:108-136): EVS = ESIW x log2(ERQ + 1) x ECRS x ERP with tiers T0 0.05, T1 1.0, T2 0.75, T3 0.5, T4 0.25, times verificationWeight(status) from evidence-exposure.ts. Name the three differences (log2 vs linear vs bounded replication; 5 vs 16 tiers; prior vs multiplier).
- Rewrite 'Logical Fallacy Score' as 'Fallacy claims' and describe the shipped mechanism by name: FALLACY_CATALOG (14 entries, each with targetFactor relevance \| logical-validity \| evidence-quality, severity minor \| major, detectionCriteria, evidenceRequirement, notFallacyWhen; src/lib/fallacy/catalog.ts); the six-field accusation template (fallacyType, quotedText, explanation >= 40 chars, missingElements, evidenceLinks, consequences; exhibits mandatory for straw-man, false-dilemma, slippery-slope, cherry-picking, moving-goalposts; severity/weight/consensus/status/score rejected with 422); filing creates a draft counter-argument on the target's linkage sub-debate and changes no score; POST /api/arguments/[id]/fallacy-claims and POST /api/fallacy-claims/[id]/votes.
- Give the confirmation rule with its constants: CONSENSUS_THRESHOLD 0.6 weighted share either way, CONSENSUS_QUORUM 3 votes (src/lib/consensus.ts); vote weight = callerCredibility = clamp(accuracyFactor x balanceFactor, 0.3, 1.4), accuracyFactor = 2 x upheld/resolved after 3 resolved, balanceFactor = 0.4 + 0.9 x (min + 1)/(max + 1) over agree-side vs disagree-side flags after 5 flags, new user exactly 1.0, computed before the vote is stored (src/lib/fallacy/calibration.ts, caller-record.ts); on confirmation the counter-argument publishes at strength 0.8 (major) / 0.5 (minor) and propagateFromLinkageChange reruns the linkage aggregate; on rejection the target is untouched and the accuser's accuracy falls; consensus can move back.
- Give the validity ladder as the only aggregate over confirmed claims: none 0.95, one minor 0.75, one major 0.45, two or more 0.25 (VALIDITY_* in src/lib/fallacy/claims.ts:100-109); bridged into scoreArgument as impacts summing to (1 - multiplier) x 100 so baseTruth = truthScore x (1 - sum\|impact\|/100) (scoring-engine.ts:432-436); on the belief page a confirmed claim is an inline note '<type> confirmed, dents Linkage \| Argument Score (validity) \| Evidence Quality' (ArgumentTreesSection.tsx:125-140). Say that relevance fallacies hit linkage, formal fallacies hit validity, evidence fallacies hit evidence quality.
- Describe the automated detector as what it is and mark the ML version as proposed: six regexes in src/lib/agent-ingest/fallacy-detector.ts (ad-hominem slur list; appeal-to-popularity phrases; correlat.../caus...; always/never universals; 'one study'; 'my uncle' / 'personal experience'), run only on agent-ingested claims, each hit a draft counter-argument authored by 'system:fallacy-detector' with no numeric field by construction; no training data, no model, no accuracy tracking. Keep the owner's two Python sketches under a heading such as 'The 2025 sketch', each labelled proposed, and note beside the enhanced detector that its keywords are fallacy names so its own examples would not fire.
- Add the published site's refusal, verbatim in substance, as a section: pattern-matching the prose is deliberately not done because on this corpus an ad hominem matcher fires on the central legitimate argument, and a score moved by a word pattern has no page to appeal to (tools/static-site/integrity.py:3-11; render_site.py:1021-1025; method.py:186, 196). Then list the nine structural checks with their severities (integrity.py:34-44) and that the two serious ones (Circular support; Assumes its own conclusion, including equivalence >= 0.90 to a load-bearing premise) refuse the build via cycles_in_tables (Tarjan SCC), while none of the nine moves a score.
- Mark as proposed, with no implementation: semantic auto-flagging of equivalent arguments for fallacies (step 4; equivalence candidates exist via textSimilarity / EQUIVALENCE_CANDIDATE_THRESHOLD and the lexical similarity.py with FLAG 0.45 / MERGE 0.70, but neither touches fallacy claims); the aggregated Logical Fallacy Score with a confidence interval (step 7 and the -2/0/+2 bands); the training dataset; domain-specific tuning; detector self-improvement. Note the explainer's 'whole cluster' sentence as an over-claim pending the owner's answer.
- Keep the owner's four fallacies as a table but add the catalog mapping: ad hominem -> 'ad-hominem' (relevance, major; not a fallacy when a conflict of interest is documented); appeal to authority -> 'appeal-to-authority' (evidence-quality, minor); false cause -> 'false-cause' (logical-validity, major) and 'post-hoc'; red herring -> no catalog entry, handled as a linkage question or 'whataboutism' (relevance, minor). List the other ten catalog entries. Note the ten-row severity-weight table in docs/ALGORITHMS.md:433-447 as proposed and unused, and that LVS = 1 - sum(sev x weight)/Max is not computed anywhere.
- Add the empirical half's other mechanism, which the page omits: the Falsifiability Test and Testable Predictions. App: FalsifiabilityTestSection shows strengthen/weaken rows ranked by each row's own sub-debate score plus predictions with followsIf, timeframe, verification method, result so far and score (src/features/belief-analysis/components/FalsifiabilityTestSection.tsx). Static site: falsify_for / falsify_against edge rows (ise_tables.py:50) rendered as 'Evidence that would strengthen / weaken' with a Result column and vote counts (render_site.py:793-798); prediction rows score through the same row formula; the stat 'falsif' = share of predictions that are dated and have linkage >= 0.5 (render_site.py:231-232); the structural check 'Nothing stated would show it false' fires on any belief with no prediction; a falsifiability test or predictions is one of publish.CORE_BAR's requirements for a complete belief (publish.py:18).
- Mark the superseded term explicitly: 'Logical Fallacy Score' was replaced by structured fallacy claims plus the validity ladder in the app, and by structural checks plus argued linkage objections on the published site, for the reason given in integrity.py (a deduction nobody can appeal to is worse than none). Mark ALGORITHMS.md's TS = (LV x EQ x VL) +/- CW on -1..+1 as superseded: truth runs 0..1 and the -1..+1 scale survives only inside a row as 2 x Truth - 1 (method.py:195).
- Fix the mechanics: replace the four sites.google.com links with wiki-internal links (Evidence-Verification-Score-(EVS); no Logical-Fallacy-Score page exists, so either create one or drop the link); fix the [[text](url)](url) See Also syntax; close the first code fence with three backticks; reconcile with Quantifying-Reason:-The-Power-of-Truth-Scores.md, which duplicates this page, and with the chatgpt.com links on the two ReasonRank pages.

### Quantifying-Reason:-The-Power-of-Truth-Scores

The page is a second draft of Truth-Score.md: it frames the Truth Score as the integration of a "Logical Fallacy Score" (pro/con sub-arguments on whether an argument is fallacious, eventually ML-detected) with an Evidence Verification Score, lists four tracked fallacies, a six-step algorithm, a "role in the larger system" section and an "alternate explanation" that none of which Truth-Score.md carries, while lacking Truth-Score.md's two Python samples and its See-Also list. Last wiki edit 2025-08-09; the copies at docs/wiki/Quantifying-Reason-The-Power-of-Truth-Scores.md and _archive/wiki-updates/ are byte-identical to it (git 2026-03-27), so no rewrite has landed. Against the code the page is roughly half right in spirit and wrong in nearly every specific: there is no identifier called a Logical Fallacy Score anywhere; fallacies are handled as structured accusations (src/lib/fallacy/catalog.ts, claims.ts, calibration.ts; /api/arguments/[id]/fallacy-claims; 60% weighted consensus in src/lib/consensus.ts) whose only wired effect on a score is a published counter-argument of strength 0.8 or 0.5 in the linkage sub-debate; the validity ladder 0.95/0.75/0.45/0.25 is defined and tested but called by nothing in src; the regex detector drafts arguments and by construction returns no numbers; the published static site refuses fallacy pattern-matching outright and pins that refusal in a test; and ML detection, a detection confidence interval, a tracked red herring, a three-level severity and a stock-price chart of confidence do not exist.

| Promise on the page | Status | Where | What the code does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Intro: the approach 'directly integrat[es] two key metrics', a Logical Fallacy Score and an Evidence Verification Score, into one truth evaluation. | Partly implemented | src/core/scoring/all-scores.ts:268-357 (calculateTruthScoreBreakdown); src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:532-607 (scoreProtocolBelief); tools/static-site/score_reference.py:1-30 and tools/static-site/evidence.py:1-60; tools/static-site/method.py:196 | all-scores.ts is the only place with a two-channel composition: overallTruthScore = (logicalValidityScore + verificationTruthScore) / 2 when both arguments and evidence exist, else whichever exists, else 0.5, clamped to [0.01, 0.99] (lines 338-351). But the 'logical' half is not a fallacy score: it is the pro share of fallacy-penalised argument truth weights, weight = max(0, truthScore × (1 − fallacyPenalty)), LV = proWeight / (pro + con) clamped [0.01, 0.99] (lines 296-313). The engine the belief page actually runs, scoring-engine.ts, has no two-metric split at all: TruthScore = ProRank / (ProRank + ConRank) plus an evidence term (evidenceNet / count) × 0.2, clamped [0.01, 0.99] (lines 556-574). The published static site scores truth = (POS + w × p0) / (POS + NEG + w) with the evidence prior p0 and has no fallacy term; its method page lists 'Logical validity from weighted fallacy deductions ... wiki: Truth Score' under 'Not computed' (method.py:196). |
| Intro item 1: the Logical Fallacy Score 'measures the relative performance of pro/con sub-arguments on whether an argument uses logical fallacies'. | Replaced by another mechanism | src/lib/consensus.ts:31-59; src/app/api/fallacy-claims/[id]/votes/route.ts:78-234; prisma/schema.prisma (model FallacyClaim: status, consensus Float?; model FallacyClaimVote: agree, weight, reasoning); src/lib/fallacy/claims.ts:100-109 | grep for logicalFallacyScore / fallacyScore / 'Logical Fallacy Score' across src, tools and docs/ALGORITHMS.md returns nothing: no such number exists. What replaced it is a per-accusation weighted consensus: resolveConsensus sums vote weights, agreeShare = agreeWeight / totalWeight, outcome 'upheld' when votes.length >= CONSENSUS_QUORUM (3) and agreeShare >= CONSENSUS_THRESHOLD (0.6), 'rejected' when 1 − agreeShare >= 0.6, else 'open'. The share is stored on FallacyClaim.consensus (0-1) and mapped to status open/confirmed/rejected. A separate ladder, logicalValidityMultiplier: none confirmed 0.95, one minor 0.75, one major 0.45, two or more 0.25 (claims.ts:100-109), is the nearest thing to a 'fallacy score' but is exported and called only by tests (grep: no callers in src outside claims.ts). |
| Intro item 2: the Evidence Verification Score 'measures the degree of independent corroboration for a belief (e.g., blind studies, replication rates, scenario similarity)'. | Partly implemented | src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:111-137 (EVIDENCE_TYPE_WEIGHTS, getEvidenceTypeWeight, calculateEVS); src/core/reasonrank/types.ts:179-184 (VERIFICATION_SCORES); tools/static-site/evidence.py:60-140 | Next.js engine: EVS = ESIW × log2(ERQ + 1) × ECRS × ERP (calculateEVS, lines 124-137), with tier weights T0 0.05 (retracted), T1 1.0, T2 0.75, T3 0.5, T4 0.25 and an unknown-tier default of 0.5 (lines 111-119); lifecycle weights VERIFIED 1.0, DISPUTED 0.5, UNVERIFIED 0.5, FALSIFIED 0.0. Static site: prior p0 = 0.5 + 0.5 × ESIW × (2 × ERP/100 − 1), weight w = k × 2n/(n+1) (REP_CAP 2.0), over 16 wiki tiers from statistics 0.90 down to artifact 0.10 plus an added 'record' 0.90 and NOSOURCE 0.0; the wiki's own Overall EVS = ESIW × ECRS × ERQ × ERP/100 is computed and printed but 'read by no score' (evidence.py docstring). Note the log2 in the Next.js formula versus the linear ERQ in the wiki EVS page and the bounded 2n/(n+1) in the static site: three different replication curves. 'Scenario similarity' is an input nowhere; 'blind studies' is not a tier in either tier table. |
| Why This Matters: 'Logical integrity: Detect and score fallacious reasoning.' | Partly implemented | src/lib/agent-ingest/fallacy-detector.ts:35-103; src/lib/fallacy/claims.ts:100-127, 141-144; src/app/api/fallacy-claims/[id]/votes/route.ts:186-214; src/lib/propagate-belief-scores.ts:481-510; src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:269-287 | Detection exists as six regular-expression rules (ad-hominem slur list, appeal-to-popularity phrases, correlation/causation, universals, isolated-study, anecdote) that return only strings, pinned by tests/unit/lib/agent-ingest/fallacy-detector.test.ts:33-41 ('never returns numeric fields'). Scoring exists in two disconnected pieces: (a) the ladder 0.95/0.75/0.45/0.25 and confirmedClaimsToDetectedFallacies, which sizes DetectedFallacy.impact so Σ\|impact\|/100 = 1 − multiplier, is tested but called by nothing in src; (b) the wired path: confirmation publishes the counter-argument with strength 0.8 (major) or 0.5 (minor) into the linkage sub-debate, and propagateFromLinkageChange recomputes linkage = A / (A + D) over published rows (0.5 when none) and ripples upward. So a confirmed fallacy of any targetFactor, including 'logical-validity' and 'evidence-quality', reaches the score only through linkage. |
| Why This Matters: 'Empirical validation: Quantify verification from independent evidence.' | Implemented | src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:124-148; src/core/reasonrank/types.ts:179-184; src/core/scoring/evidence-exposure.ts:1-130; src/app/algorithms/evidence-scores/page.tsx:36-90 | calculateEVS and computeEvidenceImpactScore = clamp(EVS × linkage × 10, 0, 100); verification lifecycle VERIFIED 1.0 / DISPUTED 0.5 / UNVERIFIED 0.5 / FALSIFIED 0.0; evidence-exposure turns the lifecycle into a Standing column and an exposure readout (unrecorded rows count at full weight and carry exposure 1.0 × \|impact\|, deliberately). The static site does the same job with the evidence prior (evidence.py). |
| Why This Matters: 'Better decisions: Connect belief strength directly to evidence strength.' | Implemented | src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:544-574; tools/static-site/evidence.py:1-45; tools/static-site/test_engines.py (test_argument_alone_can_never_leave_the_neutral_point, per CLAUDE.md) | Next.js: each evidence row adds tierWeight × linkageScore to supporting or weakening; evidenceContribution = (supporting − weakening) / count × 0.2 is added to ProRank/(ProRank+ConRank). Static site goes further: the evidence prior is the only thing that can move any page off 0.50; argument alone never does, and a test pins it. |
| Why This Matters: 'Scalable system: Machine learning + human input + transparent scoring.' | Partly implemented | src/core/ai/similarity.py:1-20 and overlap_engine.py:1-25 (sentence-transformers, unwired); pipeline/requirements.txt:10; src/app/api/contributors/[userId]/track-record/route.ts:1-40; src/lib/fallacy/calibration.ts:22-79 | Human input: FallacyClaimVote with weight = callerCredibility = clamp(accuracyFactor × balanceFactor, 0.3, 1.4), accuracyFactor = 2 × upheld/resolved once resolved >= 3, balanceFactor = 0.4 + 0.9 × (min+1)/(max+1) once flagged >= 5, new user exactly 1.0. Transparent: GET /api/contributors/[userId]/track-record returns the raw inputs, both factors, formula and clamps. Machine learning: nothing in the fallacy path; the only ML in the repo is sentence-transformer similarity in src/core/ai (imported by no route) and pipeline/. CLAUDE.md records that a model-asserted number is the one thing the published engine refuses to carry. |
| Common Logical Fallacies We Track: 'Ad hominem – Attacking the person instead of the argument.' | Implemented | src/lib/fallacy/catalog.ts:46-59; src/lib/agent-ingest/fallacy-detector.ts:36-42 | Catalog slug 'ad-hominem', targetFactor 'relevance', severity 'major', evidenceRequirement null, notFallacyWhen 'A relevant conflict of interest is documented with evidence.' Detector rule matches /\b(liar\|liars\|idiot\|idiots\|stupid\|corrupt\|dishonest\|evil\|fraud\|frauds\|shill\|shills\|crook\|crooks)\b/i and drafts a linkage counter-argument. |

No automated detector rule for it; it can only be filed by a human with the full template. |

The detector fires 'false-cause' on correlat*…caus*/prove*/proof in either order within a sentence. |
| 'By identifying these, we help prevent non-sequiturs and flawed reasoning from distorting conclusions.' | Implemented | src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:373-396 (detectNonSequitur), 355-368 (classifyLinkageScore) | Non sequitur is handled as a linkage property, not a fallacy flag: isNonSequitur when classification is NON_SEQUITUR or \|linkage\| < 0.05; isTrueButIrrelevant when \|linkage\| < 0.1 and truthScore > 0.8, with the warning 'True but Irrelevant: ...'. Classification bands: <= −0.5 CONTRADICTION, \|s\| < 0.05 IRRELEVANT, < 0.35 ANECDOTAL, < 0.65 CONTEXTUAL, < 0.95 STRONG_CAUSAL, else DEDUCTIVE_PROOF. |

It is the single source for the detector, the accusation template, the ladder and the explainer; tests/unit/lib/fallacy/claims.test.ts:109-132 pins coherence. Red herring is absent. |
| Algorithm Outline 2: 'Allow users to flag arguments containing them.' | Implemented | src/app/api/arguments/[id]/fallacy-claims/route.ts:110-227; src/lib/fallacy/claims.ts:20-91; prisma/schema.prisma model FallacyClaim | POST /api/arguments/[id]/fallacy-claims requires all six template fields (fallacyType from the catalog, quotedText, explanation >= MIN_EXPLANATION_LENGTH 40 chars, missingElements, evidenceLinks each with a label and non-empty for types with an evidenceRequirement, consequences) and returns 422 with one issue per missing field; the body may not carry severity, weight, consensus, status or score (422 'Fields never accepted from the accuser'); writes are refused with 423 inside the monthly graph-freeze window. This is deliberately stricter than 'flag': the explainer says 'you can't just click a fallacy button'. |
| Algorithm Outline 3: 'Enable pro/con reasoning on whether a fallacy is truly present.' | Partly implemented | src/app/api/arguments/[id]/fallacy-claims/route.ts:168-200; src/app/api/fallacy-claims/[id]/votes/route.ts:78-150; prisma/schema.prisma model FallacyClaimVote (reasoning String?) | Filing creates one draft LinkageArgument (side 'disagree', status 'draft', pattern and fallacyType = the slug, statement from counterStatementFor) in the target's linkage sub-debate, and others vote agree/disagree on the claim with an optional free-text reasoning. There is no nested pro/con argument tree under the accusation itself: the 'reasoning' is a string on the vote row, and the accusation's own linkage/importance/uniqueness are never argued. The wiki's 'relative performance of pro/con sub-arguments' is therefore a weighted yes/no tally, not sub-argument scoring. |
| Algorithm Outline 4: 'Use semantic equivalency scores to auto-flag similar arguments.' | Partly implemented | src/lib/agent-ingest/similarity.ts:43-88; src/lib/agent-ingest/ingest.ts:205-230; src/app/api/equivalence-candidates/[id]/votes/route.ts:1-20; src/core/scoring/all-scores.ts:700-737; tools/static-site/similarity.py:1-50 | Equivalence auto-flagging exists but is lexical, not semantic, and it flags duplicates, not fallacies: textSimilarity = 0.7 × token Jaccard + 0.3 × bigram Jaccard; >= 0.5 persists an EquivalenceCandidate row, >= 0.8 is a restatement speed bump, >= 0.95 goes straight to the grouping vote; grouping needs the same 60%/quorum-3 consensus. calculateBeliefEquivalencyScore blends 0.4 × mechanical + 0.6 × semantic when a backend embedding is passed (none is wired), mechanical threshold 0.85, bands identical >= 0.90, near-identical >= 0.70, overlapping >= 0.45, related >= 0.20. The static site's similarity.py uses IDF-weighted Jaccard plus character-4-gram cosine, FLAG 0.45, MERGE 0.70, and carries weight 0 in every score. No code copies a confirmed FallacyClaim onto an equivalent argument; the explainer's 'a fallacy confirmed once stays confirmed for the whole cluster' rests on pages being merged to one per claim, not on propagation. |
| Algorithm Outline 5: 'Develop ML algorithms to detect fallacy patterns in text.' | Refused by design | tools/static-site/integrity.py:1-20; tools/static-site/method.py:186; tools/static-site/render_site.py:1017-1025; tools/static-site/test_render.py:649-653; src/lib/agent-ingest/fallacy-detector.ts:1-14; _archive/backend/utils/fallacyDetector.js:195-240 | The published site refuses it: integrity.py's docstring says a pattern matcher for 'attacking the person instead of the argument' would fire on the central legitimate argument of a corpus about officials acting on their interests, and 'a score moved by a regular expression breaks the one rule this site keeps everywhere else'; method.py prints 'Fallacy detection is not done' and test_render.py fails the build if that sentence disappears. The Next.js app has a six-rule regex detector, not ML, and its header states the invariant 'fallacy detection changes zero score fields', enforced by returning only strings. The only thing that ever scored a pattern match is the retired _archive/backend/utils/fallacyDetector.js (confidence = min(0.3 × patterns + 0.1 × keywords, 1); LC −= {high 0.15, medium 0.10, low 0.05} × confidence/100), which nothing imports. |
| Algorithm Outline 6a: 'Score flagged arguments based on performance of pro/con sub-arguments.' | Implemented | src/lib/consensus.ts:35-59; src/app/api/fallacy-claims/[id]/votes/route.ts:127-214; src/lib/fallacy/claims.ts:141-144; src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:269-287 | After each vote the claim is re-resolved: agreeShare >= 0.6 with >= 3 votes confirms, 1 − agreeShare >= 0.6 rejects, otherwise open; consensus can move back. Confirmation sets the counter-argument to 'published' with strength counterArgumentStrength(severity) = 0.8 major / 0.5 minor; rejection sets it 'rejected'; open returns it to 'draft'. Published rows then feed linkage = A/(A+D) and propagateBeliefScores. Vote weight is frozen at vote time and computed before the vote is stored so voting on your own claim never lifts your weight (votes route 125-127). |
| Algorithm Outline 6b: 'Confidence intervals for detection accuracy.' | Not implemented | none found for detection; related: src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:577-582 (belief-level confidenceInterval); src/lib/fallacy/calibration.ts:47-51 (accuracyRate of a caller) | No detection accuracy is measured anywhere, and the detector carries no confidence (by construction it returns only strings). The two things with similar names are different: scoreProtocolBelief's confidenceInterval = clamp(0.15 × max(0.3, 1 − 0.03 × args) × (1 − 0.5 × \|pro − con\|/(pro + con)), 0.02, 0.2) is an interval on a belief's truth, and accuracyRate = upheld / (upheld + rejected) is how often a human caller's past claims were upheld, used to weight their votes. Truth-Score.md's sample 'confidence_interval' is five text labels at thresholds −2/0/2, also unimplemented. |

The explainer at /algorithms/fallacy-detection documents the template, the 60% bar, the ladder and the [0.3, 1.4] multiplier. The name is Fallacy Detection, not Logical Fallacy Score. |
| Role in the Larger System: 'Works alongside Equivalency Scores to group similar arguments.' | Implemented | src/app/algorithms/fallacy-detection/page.tsx:132-146; src/lib/agent-ingest/similarity.ts:56-88; src/app/api/equivalence-candidates/[id]/votes/route.ts:1-14; src/lib/propagate-belief-scores.ts:140-175 | The redundancy scan proposes pairs, the community groups them at 60% weighted consensus, and the uniqueness discount (uniqueness = 1 − max similarity to earlier same-side siblings, oldest first, in siblingUniquenessFor) prices residual overlap. The static site's computed similarity carries weight 0 until a VCA page argues it (similarity.py docstring). |

But in the database-backed product nothing fills fallaciesDetected from confirmed claims: confirmedClaimsToDetectedFallacies has no caller in src, so the weight reduction a confirmed fallacy actually causes is the published counter-argument (strength 0.8/0.5) lowering linkage = A/(A+D) and thus impactScore, whatever its targetFactor says it should damage. The static site reduces nothing for fallacies: 'Not computed. The structural checks above name faults and move no score.' |

The static site diffs scores between git revisions (changes.py) and lists 'score variance over time' among BEHAVIOURAL confidence signals at weight 0 with 'no data'. Importance is not combined with the fallacy term: in scoreArgument the fallacy penalty multiplies the argument's own truth (baseTruth), while importanceWeight (default 1.0) multiplies separately in rawImpact = reasonRank × linkage × importance × uniqueness. Side balance uses the side of the parent debate the accused argument sat on (caller-record.ts).

### Evidence-Verification-Score-(EVS)

The page defines the Evidence Verification Score: a 16-row source-type weight table (ESIW, 0.90 down to 0.10), replication count (ERQ), replication consistency (ERP), a relevance factor (ECRS), the product EVS = ESIW x ECRS x ERQ x (ERP/100) summed over a belief's evidence, a sample Python script, and a promise that category assignments are debated with votes. It was last edited 2025-08-09 and the repo's docs/wiki copy is byte-identical. The published engine (tools/static-site/evidence.py, 2026-09-20 onward) carries the wiki's table row for row (plus an 18th 'record' tier and a 0.0 no-source default) but has turned the three inputs into a starting point for a claim's truth, p0 = 0.5 + 0.5 x ESIW x (2 x ERP/100 - 1) with weight w = k x 2 x ERQ/(ERQ+1), and prints the wiki's own unbounded EVS sum on every Evidence Ledger while letting no score read it. The TypeScript product (src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts) implements a different EVS, ESIW x log2(ERQ+1) x ECRS x ERP over five T0-T4 tiers (0.05 to 1.0, default 0.5) that the rules doc says are retired, so the wiki now matches neither engine exactly and the two engines do not match each other.

| Promise on the page | Status | Where | What the code does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Intro / 2.1: EVS weights evidence by 'independence and reliability' via a 16-row ESIW table: Statistics & Data 0.90; RCTs/Meta-analyses 0.85-0.80; Observational 0.75; Historical Trends 0.70; Expert Testimony with data 0.65; Expert & Social Media Claims 0.60; Anecdotes 0.55; Common Sense/Logic 0.50; Analogies 0.45; Cultural Norms 0.40; Intuition 0.35; News 0.30; Surveys & Polls 0.25; Eyewitness 0.20; Visual 0.15; Historical Artifacts 0.10. | Implemented | tools/static-site/evidence.py:79-98 (ESIW dict), :61-70 (notes on the split and the addition), :185-188 (tiers()); tools/static-site/test_engines.py:82-86 (order pinned); tools/static-site/conformance.py:18, :159, :220-225 (tiers shipped in corpus.json and checked); tools/static-site/export_db.py:50-54 (evidence_tier table); tools/static-site/method.py:82-94; docs/BELIEF_PAGE_RULES.md:92-115 | Row by row the static engine matches the wiki exactly: statistics 0.90 (rank 1), rct 0.85 and meta 0.80 (both rank 2, the wiki's single band '0.85-0.80' split in listing order, evidence.py:63-65 says reading it the other way would give 0.80/0.85), observational 0.75, historical 0.70, expert_data 0.65, expert_claim 0.60, anecdote 0.55, logic 0.50, analogy 0.45, norm 0.40, intuition 0.35, news 0.30, survey 0.25, eyewitness 0.20, visual 0.15, artifact 0.10. Two things the wiki does not have: an 18th tier `record` (primary official record: statute, holding, agency rule, sworn filing) at 0.90 with rank None (evidence.py:81, :66-70), and NOSOURCE = 0.0 for a page naming no type or an unrecognised one (evidence.py:99-100, :128). The engine therefore has eighteen keys, which is the number the rules doc and conformance contract use. Keys are matched on a normalised name (trim, lowercase, spaces and hyphens to underscores: evidence.py:123; conformance.py:29-30, pages 17-18). The TypeScript engine does NOT carry this table at all; see the next claim. |
| 2.1 (as it bears on the product): the ESIW weights above are the weights evidence carries. | Partly implemented | src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:108-118 (EVIDENCE_TYPE_WEIGHTS, getEvidenceTypeWeight); prisma/schema.prisma:732 (evidenceType default 'T3'), :746 (sourceIndependenceWeight default 0.5); src/core/types/schlicht.ts:7; src/features/belief-analysis/components/EvidenceSection.tsx:46-54; src/app/algorithms/evidence-scores/page.tsx:48-52; src/core/scoring/grounding.ts:36, :66; src/app/api/beliefs/[id]/evidence/route.ts:159-160; src/app/api/evidence/[id]/route.ts:77, :191; docs/BELIEF_PAGE_RULES.md:92 | The product engine uses five tiers, not sixteen: T0 0.05 (retracted/fraudulent), T1 1.0 (peer-reviewed/official), T2 0.75 (expert/institutional), T3 0.5 (journalism/surveys), T4 0.25 (opinion/anecdote), and an unknown tier reads 0.5 (scoring-engine.ts:117). Row-by-row against the wiki: the wiki's top weight is 0.90, the code's is 1.0; the wiki's bottom is 0.10, the code's is 0.25 (T4) or 0.05 (T0, which has no wiki row); the wiki's 'Surveys & Polls' 0.25 and 'News' 0.30 both fall in T3 0.50; 'Anecdotes' 0.55 falls in T4 0.25, so the two tables rank anecdote above news/surveys in opposite directions. The eighteen-key table exists only in Python. |
| 2.1: 'The category choice for each piece of evidence is debated in pro/con form with voting to ensure accuracy.' | Not implemented | tools/static-site/method.py:232 (states classification is typed and 'a wrong call is not visible as a disagreement'); tools/static-site/ise_tables.py:292-299 (etype/erq/erp are typed columns); tools/static-site/intake.py:17, :90 and tools/static-site/render_site.py:1595 (a vote never moves a score); docs/BELIEF_PAGE_RULES.md:157 (source type listed among typed inputs); src/core/scoring/evidence-exposure.ts:36-41, :141-146 (tierClaim/tierVerified gap reported); prisma/schema.prisma (tierClaim/tierVerified fields); src/app/algorithms/evidence-scores/page.tsx:50-52, :182-184 | Nowhere is the source type of a finding the subject of its own pro/con page. In the static site etype, erq and erp are typed by the author on the pages sheet and the method page lists this as a known weakness, quoting the wiki: 'The wiki says the category should itself be argued in pro and con form. Here it is typed'. The voting half is contradicted by a standing rule: votes are recorded (content/votes.csv) and shown on the heading line, and 'A vote never moves a score' (intake.py:90, render_site.py:1595, export_db.py:25). In the TypeScript product an agent-submitted row carries a tierClaim that a provenance job or a human confirms as tierVerified; evidence-exposure.ts counts rows weighted by an unconfirmed claim and reports the count (tierUnconfirmedCount) without inventing a number for it. That is review, not debate, and there is no vote on it either. Linkage, importance and uniqueness each have an argued page in the static engine; source type does not. |
| 2.2: ERQ = number of independent replications of a study or finding. | Implemented | tools/static-site/ise_tables.py:75, :81, :236-239, :297, :403; tools/static-site/evidence.py:130-132; tools/static-site/conformance.py:31-32, :108; prisma/schema.prisma:747; src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:126 | Static site: the pages sheet has an `erq` column; blank or below 1 reads as 1 ('the finding itself'), with the absence recorded in `na` so the label can say 'no independent replication recorded' (evidence.py:131-132, :169). Conformance page 20 pins erq=0 reading as 1. TypeScript: Evidence.replicationQuantity Int default 1. Both agree with the wiki on meaning; they differ on how the count enters the formula (see the formula claim). |
| 2.3: ERP = percentage of replications producing consistent results. | Implemented | tools/static-site/ise_tables.py:298-299; tools/static-site/evidence.py:133-136; tools/static-site/conformance.py:33-34, :109; prisma/schema.prisma:749; src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:128 | Static site: `erp` is a percentage 0-100; blank reads 100 and the value is clamped into [0, 100] (evidence.py:136), a coercion the conformance contract makes mandatory because a typo of 150 once produced a truth starting point of 1.40 (conformance.py:33-34, :104). A '%' suffix is stripped (evidence.py:114). TypeScript: replicationPercentage is a fraction 0-1 with default 1.0, not a percentage; the formula does not divide by 100. Both defaults mean an unreplicated finding is treated as fully consistent, which the wiki never states. |
| 2.4: ECRS (Evidence-to-Conclusion Relevance Score) is a fourth input measuring how strongly the evidence directly supports or weakens the conclusion. | Replaced by another mechanism | tools/static-site/evidence.py:51-55, :154-158 (evs(row, ecrs) takes the row's linkage); tools/static-site/render_site.py:176-177 (evs=EV.evs(spec, l) where l is the linkage); tools/static-site/method.py:95-96, :204; tools/static-site/test_engines.py:106-112 (source quality is not a row multiplier); prisma/schema.prisma:748 (conclusionRelevance default 0.5), :756-758 (linkageScoreType ECLS, linkageScore default 0.5); src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:127, :147-149; src/core/scoring/all-scores.ts:321-323 | Static site: relevance is the linkage page, 'a separate argument with its own reasons' (evidence.py:53-55); evs() receives the row's linkage score as ECRS so nothing new is argued, and the prior p0/w deliberately excludes relevance because 'the same finding cited on four pages has one truth'. TypeScript keeps ECRS as a separate stored field, Evidence.conclusionRelevance (default 0.5), AND a separate linkageScore (default 0.5), and multiplies relevance in twice: calculateEVS includes conclusionRelevance, then computeEvidenceImpactScore multiplies that EVS by linkageScore again (scoring-engine.ts:147-149), and calculateTruthScoreBreakdown multiplies evsScore by linkageScore a further time (all-scores.ts:321-323). The wiki's own page for this factor is titled 'Evidence to Conclusion Linkage Score (ECLS)', and Prisma's enum value is ECLS, so even the name on this page is the odd one out. |
| 3: EVS(evidence) = ESIW x ECRS x ERQ x (ERP / 100). | Partly implemented | tools/static-site/evidence.py:154-158 (exact), :72-75; tools/static-site/render_site.py:176-177, :544-564 (EVS column), :776-778; tools/static-site/method.py:95-96; src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:120-136 (different formula); tests/unit/core/scoring/scoring-engine.test.ts:518-537; prisma/schema.prisma:751-754 | Static site computes the wiki's product exactly as written, c['esiw'] * ecrs * c['erq'] * (c['erp']/100), prints it in an EVS column on every Evidence Ledger row, and no score reads it (render_site.py:433 'read by nothing'). TypeScript computes EVS = ESIW x log2(ERQ + 1) x ECRS x ERP: replication enters as log2(ERQ+1) rather than ERQ (ERQ=1 gives 1.0 in both; ERQ=3 gives 2.0 versus the wiki's 3; ERQ=10 gives 3.46 versus 10), and ERP is a 0-1 fraction with no /100. ESIW in TypeScript is the T-tier weight (1.0 for T1), so the wiki's sample study_2 (0.85 x 0.9 x 10 x 0.95 = 7.27) would read 1.0 x log2(11) x 0.9 x 0.95 = 2.96. The Prisma schema comment at :751 states the log2 form as the EVS definition. |
| 3: the inputs that determine a claim's evidentiary standing are source type, replications and consistency (implicit in the formula and the three components). | Replaced by another mechanism | tools/static-site/evidence.py:21-49 (design), :140-151 (prior()), :99-101 (NOSOURCE, REP_CAP), :105-109 (replication()); tools/static-site/score_reference.py:15-17, :32, :153-159; tools/static-site/conformance.py:23-34; tools/static-site/test_engines.py:43-104; tools/static-site/method.py:54-56, :75; docs/BELIEF_PAGE_RULES.md:92, :346-350; tools/static-site/export_db.py:184-208 (SQL twin) | The published engine turned the three wiki inputs into a Bayesian-style starting point for the finding's own page instead of a score: p0 = 0.5 + 0.5 x ESIW x (2 x ERP/100 - 1); w = k x replication(ERQ) where replication(n) = REP_CAP x n/(n+1) with REP_CAP = 2.0 (1 -> 1.00, 2 -> 1.33, 3 -> 1.50, 10 -> 1.82, limit 2.00); argued truth = (POS + w x p0)/(POS + NEG + w), k = 1. ERP decides direction, ESIW how far (a confirmed statistic starts at 0.95, a contradicted one at 0.05, so p0 is confined to [0.05, 0.95] and evidence alone never reaches certainty), ERQ how much arguing it takes to move. A page with no etype reads p0 = 0.5, w = k exactly as before. The reason is stated in evidence.py:6-19 and pinned by test_argument_alone_can_never_leave_the_neutral_point: without a prior, every page of an unsourced argument graph reads 0.50 forever. The wiki's unbounded product survives only as the displayed EVS column. The TypeScript engine has no prior; its evidence enters as impact points (EVS x linkage x 10, clamped 0-100, scoring-engine.ts:138-149) summed beside argument impact in fetch-belief.ts:340-362. |
| 3: 'The Overall EVS for a belief is the sum of EVS scores across all its evidence.' and 1: EVS is 'essential for ranking beliefs based on their evidentiary strength'. | Refused by design | tools/static-site/render_site.py:215-217 (s['evs'] = evs_for + evs_against), :776-778 (printed total), :433; tools/static-site/method.py:95-96 (why it is not used); tools/static-site/evidence.py:72-75, :106-107; src/core/scoring/all-scores.ts:842-850; src/app/algorithms/evidence-scores/page.tsx:74-78 | The static site computes the sum exactly, as 'Evidence Verification Score N . supporting A . weakening B' under every Evidence Ledger, and refuses to let it rank anything: 'an unbounded sum grows with every finding listed, and a score that grows with listing is the padding this site exists to stop' (method.py:96). It is shown because it distinguishes a page resting on replicated work (EVS 30) from one resting on a single claim (0.4). Rankings on the site use truth, confidence, ReasonRank and leverage, never EVS. The TypeScript product does not sum either: aggregateEvidenceScore = supportingEVS/(supportingEVS + weakeningEVS), 0.5 when nothing (all-scores.ts:843-850), a bounded share that the /algorithms/evidence-scores page describes as 'supporting divided by (supporting + weakening)'. Note that the wiki's sum, read literally, is signless: supporting and weakening evidence add together, which neither engine does. |
| 4: the example Python implementation (three categories 'statistics_and_data' 0.9, 'formal_scientific_studies_randomized_controlled_trials' 0.85, 'formal_scientific_studies_meta_analysis' 0.8; studies with erq 5/10/3, erp 90/95/85, ecrs 0.8/0.9/0.7; per-study EVS and summed belief EVS). | Partly implemented | tools/static-site/evidence.py:79-84 (keys statistics, rct, meta), :154-158; tools/static-site/ise_tables.py:292-294 (the key list authors must use), :332 (LISTS validation); tools/static-site/conformance.py:85-109 (the corpus that plays the role of a worked example) | The script runs stand-alone with no library and its arithmetic is the static site's evs(): study_1 = 0.9 x 0.8 x 5 x 0.9 = 3.24, study_2 = 0.85 x 0.9 x 10 x 0.95 = 7.2675, study_3 = 0.8 x 0.7 x 3 x 0.85 = 1.428, total 11.9355. But its category keys are not the engine's: the code's keys are `statistics`, `rct`, `meta`, validated against evidence.ESIW (ise_tables.py:332), so the sample's keys would classify as no tier (ESIW 0.0, conformance page 19). Its ecrs values are typed numbers, where the engine takes the row's linkage score. And it sums, which nothing reads. Under the engine the three findings would instead start at p0 = 0.86 (w 1.67), 0.8825 (w 1.82) and 0.78 (w 1.50). The TypeScript engine would give 1.0 x log2(6) x 0.8 x 0.9 = 1.86 for study_1 if typed T1. |
| 5: 'All EVS calculations will be publicly visible.' | Implemented | tools/static-site/render_site.py:540-564 (Starts at and EVS columns, hover shows the working), :776-779 (both formulas with k under the table), :1140, :1151-1152 (Scoring Engine section 'Starting point' line); tools/static-site/evidence.py:161-182 (label() and working() print p0 and w with the page's own numbers); tools/static-site/method.py:54-96 (method page with tier table and both formulas); tools/static-site/export_db.py:50-54, :184-208, :235-246 (evidence_tier table and a SQL view that recomputes p0); tools/static-site/conformance.py (corpus.json carries the tiers); src/app/algorithms/evidence-scores/page.tsx; prisma/schema.prisma:754 (evsScore stored per row) | The static site prints every finding's Starts at figure with a hover that spells out 'tier weight 0.90; starting point 0.5 + 0.5 x 0.90 x (2 x 100/100 - 1) = 0.95; weight 1 x 2 x 1/(1 + 1) = 1.00', an EVS column per row, totals under the ledger, the tier table on the method page, and ships the tiers and expected numbers in conformance_corpus.json / conformance_expected.json so a port can be checked without reading the source. The React page stores evsScore per evidence row and has an explainer at /algorithms/evidence-scores, though that explainer describes the T-tier inputs in prose and prints no formula. |
| 5: 'All category assignments and scoring decisions will be open to community review' and 'Users can challenge evidence classification via the debate system.' | Not implemented | tools/static-site/method.py:232, :235 (typed inputs acknowledged); tools/static-site/intake.py:361 (intake notes a finding with no type starts at a coin flip); tools/static-site/contribute.js, intake.py (issue forms add rows and record votes, not reclassifications); src/core/scoring/evidence-exposure.ts:36-41 (tierClaim/tierVerified reported, not debated) | Classifications are visible (the ledger prints the tier and the method page lists them as typed inputs with 'an author's error'), so review by reading is possible, but there is no mechanism to challenge one: no classification page, no form field that proposes a different etype, and the GitHub issue forms create rows or votes. The TypeScript side has a provenance job confirming an agent's tierClaim; a human can edit a row through /api/evidence/[id] but that is an edit, not a debate.
