| 6: 'Rewards replication and consistency.' | Implemented | tools/static-site/evidence.py:42-45, :101, :105-109; tools/static-site/test_engines.py:88-93 (bounded), :95-104 (strongest prior within reach of argument), :65-69 (symmetric about 0.5), :71-74 (half agreement is 0.5 for every tier); src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:132 | Replication is rewarded in both engines but never linearly as the wiki's ERQ factor implies. Static: weight w = k x 2n/(n+1), capped at 2k, so 'no pile of replications can put a claim beyond argument', and the test at test_engines.py:95-104 shows six sourced objections pull a 50-times-replicated statistic back under 0.5. Consistency: ERP sets the direction; 50 percent agreement starts every tier at exactly 0.50. TypeScript: log2(ERQ+1), unbounded but concave. The wiki's linear ERQ would let ten replications weigh ten times one. |
| 6: 'Reduces bias from anecdotal or low-quality sources' and 'Encourages higher-quality evidence.' | Implemented | tools/static-site/evidence.py:33-40, :87-97; docs/BELIEF_PAGE_RULES.md:96-115 (Starts at column); src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:108-114 | Static: an anecdote (0.55) confirmed starts at 0.775 and contradicted at 0.225; an eyewitness account (0.20) at 0.60/0.40; an artifact (0.10) at 0.55/0.45; a statistic at 0.95/0.05. Lower tiers move a claim less, not nothing, and test_a_weaker_source_moves_a_claim_less_than_a_stronger_one pins the ordering. TypeScript: T4 opinion/anecdote weighs 0.25 against T1's 1.0, and T0 retracted 0.05. Both achieve the stated effect; the magnitudes differ from each other and from the wiki's table. |
| Intro: EVS 'measures the degree to which a belief is supported by reliable, independent, and high-quality evidence' (a property of the belief). | Replaced by another mechanism | tools/static-site/evidence.py:51-55 ('Source quality is a property of the claim, not of the place the claim is cited'); tools/static-site/score_reference.py:166-169; docs/BELIEF_PAGE_RULES.md:346-350; src/core/scoring/grounding.ts:14-28; src/core/scoring/evidence-exposure.ts:1-41 | In the published engine the three inputs belong to the finding's own page and set where that page's truth starts; a belief reads evidence only through its evidence rows, each counted by the same row rule as an argument, sign x (2 x Truth - 1) x Confidence x Link x Imp x Uniq, so a finding at 0.50 contributes nothing however often it is listed. 'How supported a belief is by evidence' is answered on the product side by two other engines the wiki does not mention: grounding.ts (direct = sum tierWeight x \|linkage\|, inherited through argument edges, saturating raw/(raw+1), so assertion volume cannot move it) and evidence-exposure.ts (how many of the points drawn from evidence rest on unestablished standing). Neither is the wiki's EVS. |
| Not on the page but load-bearing in the code the page concerns: a verification lifecycle (Standing) decides how much of an evidence row counts. | Implemented | src/core/reasonrank/types.ts:179-184 (VERIFICATION_SCORES: VERIFIED 1.0, DISPUTED 0.5, UNVERIFIED 0.5, FALSIFIED 0.0); src/core/reasonrank/propagation.ts:110-122 (default UNVERIFIED); src/features/belief-analysis/data/fetch-belief.ts:329-341 (null status counts 1.0), :353-358, :376; src/core/scoring/evidence-exposure.ts:108-181 (counted/exposed weights, NEGLIGIBLE_EXPOSURE 0.5, HIGH_EXPOSURE_SHARE 0.5), :185-209, :229-253 (readout); docs/BELIEF_PAGE_RULES.md:352-369; src/features/belief-analysis/components/EvidenceSection.tsx:82-90, :232, :244 | The wiki says nothing about retraction or verification. The product multiplies each evidence row's impact (fetch-belief.ts:340-343) and its EVS (fetch-belief.ts:376) by VERIFICATION_SCORES[status]: Verified 1.0, Unverified and Disputed 0.5, Falsified 0.0, and a row with no status (legacy) counts at 1.0. evidence-exposure.ts then reports exposure = \|impact\| x (0.5 for unverified/disputed, 1.0 for unrecorded, 0 for verified/falsified), with the 'Unrecorded counts in full' choice deliberate (CLAUDE.md and evidence-exposure.ts:22-27). The static site has no lifecycle: every row is the Unrecorded case and its ledger prints no Standing column (BELIEF_PAGE_RULES.md:363-369). A rewrite of the EVS page has to say which engine's notion of 'verification' the V in EVS now means. |

**Stale on the page:**

- Formatting: the formula block at lines 68-72 opens with three backticks and closes with four (````); the Python block at lines 86-113 opens with ```python and closes with four backticks. Both are stray fences and render as a literal backtick in GitHub's wiki.
- Formatting: the formula block has a blank line immediately after the opening fence (line 69) and the band '0.85–0.80' uses an en dash where every other number on the page is a single value.
- Name: section 2.4 calls the fourth factor 'Evidence-to-Conclusion Relevance Score (ECRS)'. The wiki's own page for it (Evidence-to-Conclusion-Relevance-Score.md) is titled 'Evidence to Conclusion Linkage Score (ECLS)', Prisma's enum is LinkageScoreType.ECLS (prisma/schema.prisma:757), and the static site calls it the linkage page. 'ECRS' appears in the code only as a comment alias.
- Names: the sample code's category keys 'statistics_and_data', 'formal_scientific_studies_randomized_controlled_trials', 'formal_scientific_studies_meta_analysis' match no key the engine accepts; the keys are statistics, record, rct, meta, observational, historical, expert_data, expert_claim, anecdote, logic, analogy, norm, intuition, news, survey, eyewitness, visual, artifact (tools/static-site/ise_tables.py:292-294). Under the engine's normalisation these sample keys classify as 'no tier' and weigh 0.0.
- Code sample: it uses no external library, which is fine, but it computes and prints a summed 'Overall EVS for belief' that no engine uses as a score; the static site prints the same sum labelled 'read by nothing'.
- Missing name: the page never mentions the T0-T4 tiers that the TypeScript product (src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:108-114), its Prisma default 'T3', its seeds and its /algorithms/evidence-scores explainer all still use, nor that docs/BELIEF_PAGE_RULES.md:92 declares them retired.
- Missing name: the page never mentions the `record` tier (0.90) the engine added (tools/static-site/evidence.py:81), nor the no-source default of 0.0 (evidence.py:99), nor the ERQ/ERP defaults of 1 and 100.
- Inbound links: Home.md:13-16 describes the Evidence Verification Score under the Truth Score heading and links Truth-Score, not this page. Truth-Score.md:6 and :137 and Quantifying-Reason:-The-Power-of-Truth-Scores.md:6 link 'Evidence Verification Score' to a sites.google.com edit URL (https://sites.google.com/d/1f62q1ZSjo3Kcb7Pj5OHCNTEfaacTWuKD/p/1Q6VmxCxMqRCIUhp4ErrI4txKeacq88AK/edit) instead of this wiki page; Truth-Score.md:137 is a doubly nested link `[[text](url)](url)`. This page itself contains no links at all, inbound or outbound.
- Cross-reference drift: tools/static-site/evidence.py:15-17 and method.py:194 attribute the start values 'meta-analysis 1.0, peer-reviewed study 0.8, anecdote 0.1, refuted evidence 0.0' to 'the wiki's ReasonRank page'. Those lines are in the repo's docs/wiki/ReasonRank-Google's-PageRank-for-Arguments.md:65-71 but not in the live wiki copy at scratchpad/wiki/ReasonRank:-Google's-PageRank-for-Arguments.md, so the live wiki and the code's citation of it have diverged.
- Third and fourth tables nobody reconciled: docs/ALGORITHMS.md:181-190 gives 'Evidence Type Defaults' as EQ ranges 1-10 (meta-analysis of RCTs 9-10, single RCT 8-9, observational 6-7, expert opinion 5-6, anecdote 1-2), and docs/EVIDENCE_QUALITY_SCORING.md scores quality 0-100 from four methodology patterns with a 40 percent weight on the first. Neither matches the wiki's 16 rows, the engine's 18, or the TypeScript five; method.py:193 lists the four-ratings scheme among the wiki ideas the engine does not implement.
- Formatting: the page's section numbering (1 Purpose, 2 Core Components, 3 Formula, 4 Example, 5 Transparency, 6 Benefits) puts the definition before any statement of what the number is for on a belief page; the engine's own documentation leads with the arithmetic reason the prior exists (tools/static-site/evidence.py:6-19), which the page lacks entirely.

### Evidence-to-Conclusion-Relevance-Score

The page defines the Evidence-to-Conclusion Linkage Score: a per-edge measure of how much an argument would support a conclusion if assumed true, computed as the agree share of argued strength on the linkage, ranging 0.0 (no reasons to agree) to 1.0, multiplied into each argument's contribution. It was last edited 2025-08-09 and is byte-identical to the repo copy at docs/wiki/Evidence-to-Conclusion-Relevance-Score.md (last touched 2026-03-27). The core idea is implemented in every engine the repo carries: a linkage edge with its own two-sided sub-debate whose outcome multiplies the row (tools/static-site/score_reference.py, src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts, src/lib/conclusion-score.ts, src/lib/ise-pages/score.ts). The specifics have drifted in four ways the page does not record: the published engine shrinks the share toward a 0.50 starting point (POS + w*p0)/(POS + NEG + w) rather than taking the raw ratio; three engines use a signed (A-D)/(A+D) form on -1..+1 that the page never mentions; no engine defaults an undebated linkage to the page's 0.0 (the published site presumes 1, the Next.js debate path 0.5, Prisma Argument rows 0.1, the founding workbook 1); and the contribution the page states as Truth x ECLS is now Truth x Link x Imp x Uniq with confidence and sign, so the "Apply linkage weight" step is partial.

| Promise on the page | Status | Where | What the code does |
|---|---|---|---|
| ECLS measures how strongly a piece of evidence (argument) supports or weakens a conclusion if assumed true (intro) | Implemented | tools/static-site/build_subpages.py:36-38 and :39; tools/static-site/render_site.py:157-160 (Corpus.question); src/lib/ise-pages/questions.ts:48-57; src/app/arguments/[id]/linkage/page.tsx:1-18; src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:154-160; docs/ALGORITHMS.md:196 | Every engine frames linkage as a conditional: the static site builds the linkage page's claim by formula as 'If it were true that: X, it would significantly strengthen the conclusion that: Y' (variants: 'observed' for a Prediction type, 'weaken' when Direction is Weakens, 'The work: X supports the conclusion that: Y' for Media, 'it would matter to the interest: Y' for Interest). Note the formula says 'significantly', not 'necessarily'; 'necessarily' appears only in the build_pages.py:2-4 header comment and in docs/ALGORITHMS.md:196. The Next.js linkage page asks the question 'at full strength (would it necessarily support Y?)' and treats the score as the degree (page.tsx:7-8). scoring-engine.ts generateLinkageSubClaim (line 235-247) builds '"X" strengthens the case for "Y"' (or 'is a direct cause supporting' / 'is a necessary condition for' / 'is sufficient to prove'), but nothing calls it from a page. |

Instead a fallacy accusation is a reason to disagree on the row's linkage page, scored like any other claim (build_subpages.py:29, build_pages.py:749). The published static site does NOT take the raw share: a linkage page is a SPECIAL kind whose truth is _share(pro, con) = (POS + w x p0) / (POS + NEG + w), the argued-truth rule, where a linkage page that cites nothing has p0 = 0.5 and w = k = 1, and POS/NEG are signed row contributions sign x (2 x Truth - 1) x Conf x Link x Imp x Uniq partitioned by sign (score_reference.py:176-179, 186-188). build_subpages.py:28 documents the relationship: 'The wiki's (A - D)/(A + D) form is 2 x this score - 1, once enough is argued here that the starting point no longer matters.' src/lib/ise-pages/score.ts:108-112 uses the same share with k but with unsigned Truth x Link x Imp x Uniq rows. Three other engines use a signed form the wiki never mentions: scoreLinkageDebate (A - D)/(A + D) on [-1, 1] over rawImpact of ReasonRank-scored sub-arguments; conclusion-score.ts linkageRatio (agree - disagree)/(agree + disagree) over vote counts; the SQL view the same over COUNT(*). The 'Strength' of a reason differs per engine: the published site reads each reason's own page truth (never a typed number); Prisma LinkageArgument.strength is a typed Float default 0.5 (schema.prisma:484) that the schema comment calls 'the legacy manual weight feeding the (A − D)/(A + D) aggregate until every row carries a computed score' (schema.prisma:491-494). |
| Strength is determined by performance metrics from user-generated pro/con reasons (Formula, 'Where') | Partly implemented | tools/static-site/score_reference.py:160-174; prisma/schema.prisma:478-494; src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:177-184 | Static site: a linkage page's reasons are claims with their own pages; their strength is their own page truth times confidence, link, importance and uniqueness, all read from pages, none typed. scoreLinkageDebate: strength is each sub-argument's rawImpact from the recursive scoreArgument. Prisma/Next.js path: strength is a hand-typed Float (default 0.5) on LinkageArgument, posted through POST /api/arguments/[id]/linkage body {side, statement, strength}; LinkageArgument.score (Float?) is the intended computed replacement and is only used for row ranking, not for the aggregate. 'Performance metrics' in the wiki's sense (voting, credibility) are not what any engine reads. |
| ECLS ranges 0.0 → no reasons to agree with linkage; 1.0 → all reasons agree (Formula) | Partly implemented | src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:258-262 and :272-287; tools/static-site/score_reference.py:152-155; src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:164-169; src/lib/conclusion-score.ts:9 | calculateLinkageFromArguments matches the range: 0 when none agree, 1 when all agree (tests at tests/unit/core/scoring/scoring-engine.test.ts:544-555). The published site's linkage page cannot reach 0.0 or 1.0 with finite argued weight because of the k = 1 starting weight: one unopposed reason of full weight 1.0 reads (1 + 0.5)/(1 + 1) = 0.75, not 1.0. The signed engines range -1..+1: scoreLinkageDebate, conclusion-score.ts linkageRatio, the SQL view, LinkageVote.score (-1 to 1), calculateLinkageFromDiagnostic (sign from direction), and the explainer at src/app/algorithms/linkage-scores/page.tsx:356-364 says 'from -1.0 (actively contradicts) to +1.0 (deductively proves)'. The explainer and the Next.js linkage page therefore state a range the number they display (A/(A+D), 0..1) cannot take. |

Published site: sign x (2 x Truth - 1) x Confidence x Link x Imp x Uniq, so Truth enters on the -1..+1 scale (a refuted reason counts against its own side and an unargued one at 0.5 contributes exactly 0), confidence (confidence.py) scales it, and importance (DEFIMP 0.5) and uniqueness (DEFUNIQ 1) multiply too. scoring-engine: rawImpact = reasonRank x linkage x importance x uniqueness; computeArgumentImpactScore uses \|linkage\| so the filed side alone sets direction and scales x 100 then rounds to one decimal; calculateArgumentImpact = round(truth x linkage x importance x 100). Workbook: childScore x linkage, then x m = 0.7 sub-argument multiplier. The Next.js linkage page shows 'Contribution to Y: argumentScore x linkageScore' (page.tsx:519-524), the closest to the wiki's two-factor product. The explainer's 'Master Formula' is three-factor Truth x Linkage x Importance (linkage-scores/page.tsx:424-426). |

Static site: belief score = POS - NEG (open-ended) and truth = (POS + w x p0)/(POS + NEG + w), then min with the weakest load-bearing component. scoring-engine: subArgScore = 0.5 + (pro - con)/count x 0.5 blended as 0.15 x baseTruth + 0.85 x subArgScore (DAMPING_FACTOR), and scoreProtocolBelief/all-scores use A/(A+D). Workbook: (nAgree - nDisagree) + 0.7 x (Σ agree childScore x linkage - Σ disagree childScore x linkage), one structural point per listed reason regardless of linkage. CLAUDE.md records the unresolved disagreement about whether listing an unargued reason counts (static site: no; scoring-engine.ts: yes). |

No code measures strength by text length, and the sample's 0-when-empty default is replaced by DEFLINK = 1 (no page) or the 0.50 starting point (empty page). The sample is illustrative Python the project does not ship; the real Python is tools/static-site/score_reference.py. |

**Stale on the page:**

- Title mismatch: the page is filed as Evidence-to-Conclusion-Relevance-Score.md but titled 'Evidence to Conclusion Linkage Score (ECLS)'; the sibling EVS page calls the same factor ECRS (Evidence-Verification-Score-(EVS).md:58, 76) and tools/static-site/evidence.py:154-157 adopts 'ECRS' for the EVS formula while method.py:204 cites it as 'wiki: Evidence to Conclusion Relevance Score'. Three names for one factor.
- ReasonRank.md:71 links this page through a chatgpt.com URL (https://chatgpt.com/g/g-zEqCtLTMb-idea-stock-exchange/c/Evidence-to-Conclusion-Relevance-Score) wrapped in a second [[...]] link; the inner target is not a wiki page.
- Formatting: the Formula fence opens with ``` and closes with ```` (four backticks, line 31); the Python sample closes with ```` (line 113). Both leave stray fence characters when rendered.
- The Formula block (lines 26-31) has a blank line before and after the formula inside the fence and a line break mid-expression after 'Linkage)', so it renders as two fragments.
- The Python sample (lines 83-113) uses a length-of-text placeholder for strength and a dict keyed 'A_B'; the project ships no such code and its real Python is tools/static-site/score_reference.py reading pages.csv/edges.csv. The sample's `else 0` default contradicts every shipped default (1, 0.5, 0.1).
- The term 'strengthener'/'weakener' for linkage reasons survives in code only as a LinkageType value (scoring-engine.ts:244, docs/ALGORITHMS.md:224) meaning a kind of edge, not a side of the linkage debate; the shipped vocabulary is 'reasons to agree/disagree that X bears on Y' (build_subpages.py:42) and 'Bears on this: yes / no' (render_site.py:1733).
- The page says ECLS is 'determined by performance metrics from user-generated pro/con reasons' and the algorithm step 4 says 'voting, credibility'; the published site's standing rule is that a vote never moves a score (tools/static-site/README.md:258-270) and there is no LinkageVote table in prisma/schema.prisma.
- Internal repo inconsistency a rewriter should not copy: src/app/api/arguments/[id]/linkage/route.ts:7-10 and the FormulaBreakdown label at src/app/arguments/[id]/linkage/page.tsx:92-100 print 'LS = (A − D) / (A + D)' while the number both display comes from calculateLinkageFromArguments, which is A / (A + D) (scoring-engine.ts:269-287). The explainer at src/app/algorithms/linkage-scores/page.tsx:356-371 likewise gives the signed form and the -1..+1 range for a product path that computes 0..1.
- src/app/algorithms/linkage-scores/page.tsx:466-470 says the missing-assumption prompt fires at 40-60% while shouldPromptForAssumption (scoring-engine.ts:400-403) fires for \|linkage\| in (0.1, 0.4]; neither is wired to a page.

### Equivalency-Score:

The page defines an Equivalency Score as a blend of a computer-generated similarity (CES: spaCy vectors plus a trained model) and a user-generated score (UES: pro/con reasons and up/down votes), weighted by a "Validity Comparison Argument" (VCA), with a worked Python example that defaults the user weight to 0.8 and an empty vote list to 0. It has not been edited since 2025-08-09 (the repo's `_archive/wiki-updates/` copy, committed 2026-03-27, is byte-identical). The code has since taken a definite position on almost every sentence: `tools/static-site/similarity.py` implements the exact `ES = w_ces x CES + w_ues x UES` blend but with `w_ces = 0` by default and no VCA page kind, so the published Equiv number is the argued equivalence page's truth score alone; CES is a lexical IDF-weighted word-Jaccard/char-4-gram measure, not meaning; votes are recorded and displayed and never enter any score. A parallel TypeScript engine (`src/core/scoring/duplication-scoring.ts`, `all-scores.ts`, `/api/beliefs/equivalency`) uses a different measure (synonym-canonicalized Jaccard, 0.85 short-circuit, 0.4/0.6 blend with an embedding slot nobody fills) and different bands, and a third stored-scorecard formula (`/api/equivalence/[slug]/score`: 0.40/0.40/0.20 plus network minus penalty) exists in Prisma but feeds nothing published. The embedding/ML half the wiki describes exists only as unwired `src/core/ai/similarity.py` and `overlap_engine.py`.

Submission path: contribute.js scores typed text against data/claims_index.json (built from the full tables, drafts included); at >= MERGE the button becomes 'This is already here: open it and vote' or 'Already on the site; add it here as well'; intake.py re-checks and at >= MERGE records an agree vote and adds no page (test_intake.py:197-213). Next.js: ingestion persists `EquivalenceCandidate` rows at textSimilarity >= 0.5, speed-bumps restatements at >= 0.8, proposes grouping at >= 0.95; `calculateTopicOverlapScore` counts pairs >= 0.85 as duplicates. |
| Why it matters: "Links reasoning so evidence supporting one claim automatically supports others." | Not implemented | tools/static-site/render_site.py:138-143 (brief), :1068-1085; CLAUDE.md 'a score still rests on page and edge alone'; src/app/algorithms/belief-equivalency/page.tsx:294-302 (prose only) | No evidence or score propagates across an equivalence link. The only effect of an equivalence page scoring >= EQUIV_MERGE (0.9) is that the shorter wording may stand in for the other where space is short; both pages keep their own rows and scores. The Next.js explainer promises a 'sub-argument score cascade' that merges trees, but no code reads `SimilarBelief.equivalencyScore` or `EquivalenceAnalysis` when scoring a belief. |

'Focusing on unique reasoning' diverges between engines: the static site presumes every row distinct (DEFUNIQ = 1) until a uniqueness page is argued and refuses to compute uniqueness as 1 - similarity ('a number that silently discounts an argument and has no page behind it cannot be audited'); the TS engine computes `uniqueness = 1 - maxSimilarity` and `scoreArgument` multiplies `arg.uniquenessScore ?? 1.0`, but the belief page uses `calculateTopicOverlapScore(...).averageUniqueness` only as a readout and the stored `uniquenessScore` comes from the DB. |

No equivalence score is tracked over time and no market mechanism exists outside docs/MARKET_LAYER_SPEC.md. |

The dormant Prisma scorecard has a `networkAdjustment` additive term and `NetworkPosition` rows (upstream/downstream/evidence overlap, `sameCluster`), but nothing computes or publishes them. |

**Stale on the page:**

- Formatting: the formula block at lines 42-46 opens with ``` and closes with ```` (four backticks), and the Python SSM block at lines 57-71 also closes with ````; the formula line is written `ES(A,B) = w\_ces \* CES(A,B) + w\_ues \* UES(A,B)` with backslash escapes that render literally inside a code fence.
- Library: spaCy and `en_core_web_md` (lines 55-71) appear nowhere in the repository or any requirements file; tools/static-site/requirements.txt has no NLP dependency and src/core/ai (sentence-transformers, scikit-learn, numpy) is unwired and partly unparseable.
- Name: 'Validity Comparison Argument (VCA)' is used by no code; the only mentions are tools/static-site/similarity.py:10,39 and method.py:130 recording that none exists.
- Names: 'Semantic Similarity Metric (SSM)' and 'Computer-Generated Equivalency Score (CES)' survive only as the lowercase key `ces` in similarity.py and contribute.js; the Next.js engine calls the same idea `mechanicalSimilarity`/`semanticSimilarity`/`equivalencyScore`, and the static site's user-facing word is 'Equiv' / 'equivalence page'.
- Title: the wiki page is 'Equivalency-Score:' with a trailing colon (docs/wiki/_Sidebar.md:9 links [[Equivalency Score:]]), while the in-repo copy is docs/wiki/Equivalency-Score.md and similarity.py:3 cites `docs/wiki/Equivalency-Score.md`; one of the two names has to win.
- Sample code: `equivalency_score(statement1, statement2, user_scores, vca_multiplier=0.8)` depends on a vote list that no store provides (votes.csv holds agree/disagree per login, not ratings) and on a default the code refuses.
- Cross-reference drift the rewrite will inherit: docs/wiki/Home.md:28,57 points at `backend/utils/redundancyDetector.js` and docs/wiki/Redundancy-Problem.md:53,99 and src/core/scoring/duplication-scoring.ts:8 point at `backend/algorithms/duplication_scoring.py`; neither file exists outside _archive.
- Missing links: the page links nothing internal, although Redundancy-Problem, Template:-Belief-Equivalence-Scorecard, Truth-Score and the method page anchor `method.html#equivalency` all exist and are the pages that now carry the mechanism.
- Inconsistent bands inside the code the page would cite: src/app/algorithms/belief-equivalency/page.tsx:51-75 prints bands ~100 / ~70-90 / ~40-70 / below ~40, while the route it calls (src/app/api/beliefs/equivalency/route.ts:80-86) uses >=0.90 / 0.70-0.89 / 0.45-0.69 / 0.20-0.44 / <0.20, and the static site uses 0.45 / 0.70 (flag/merge) for the computed half and 0.5 / 0.9 for the argued page.
- The 'stock prices' framing (line 9, line 107) has no mechanism behind it; the site's own rule is that votes never move a score and a vote-share gap is ranked, not traded.

**What an updated page must say:**

- Keep the owner's split into CES, UES and a blend, and keep the formula `ES(A,B) = w_ces x CES(A,B) + w_ues x UES(A,B)` with `w_ces + w_ues = 1`, but state its real implementation: `Similarity.blend(equivalence_page, w_ces=0.0)` in tools/static-site/similarity.py:274-282 returns `{ces, ues, w_ces, es}` with `es = ues` whenever `w_ces <= 0` or either page has no text; `w_ces` defaults to 0 and nothing but the test (test_similarity.py:98-107) calls it, so the published Equiv is UES alone.
- Define UES as it is: the truth score of an equivalence page (kind 'equivalence', question 'The belief: X makes the same claim as: Y'), computed by the argued-truth rule (POS + w x p0)/(POS + NEG + w) with p0 = 0.50 and k = 1, each reason contributing sign x (2 x Truth - 1) x Conf x Link x Imp x Uniq; reasons are about truth conditions, never about whether X or Y is true. Cite render_site.py:73-80 (`equivalents`), :163, :1266-1268, and docs/wiki/Template:-Belief-Equivalence-Scorecard.md. Verdict bands printed on the page: 'Merge candidate' >= 0.9 (EQUIV_MERGE, render_site.py:38), 'Overlapping: keep both, name the difference on each page' 0.5-0.9, 'Distinct claims: keep both pages, cross-link' < 0.5.
- Define CES as it is: lexical, transparent, no model. `ces = 0.5 x jaccard(content words) + 0.5 x cosine(character 4-grams)`, both IDF-weighted in `Similarity.between` with idf = max(0, log((N+1)/(df+1))); constants FLAG = 0.45 (flag for a human), MERGE = 0.70 ('probably one page'), IDF_FLOOR = 0.05, JMIN = 0.05, PAIRCAP = 1,500,000, NGRAM = 4, a 70-word STOP list (similarity.py:46-57). State plainly what it misses (paraphrase with no shared words) and what it over-flags (shared subject phrase), quoting similarity.py:34-37. The same measure is ported to contribute.js:10-87 and intake.py:466-498 (Matcher) and the three must agree to the hair, which is why they share one constant set.
- Record where the computed half appears and what it is for: `pairs()` lists every pair above FLAG most-alike first with the equivalence page, uniqueness page and shared parent if any (similarity.py:153-218); `unguarded()` = flagged pairs on one page with no uniqueness page, the 'live padding risk'; the belief page prints 'Wording overlap, unargued' for up to four such pairs (render_site.py:758-763); integrity.py:166-170 files 'Rows that may be the same point twice' under 'worth checking'; method.html#equivalency prints the top 12 pairs with totals, hidden count, candidates compared, and claims left unexamined at PAIRCAP (method.py:126-150). It changes no score: `test_it_changes_no_score` (test_similarity.py:91-96).
- Keep the owner's 'Carbon emissions cause climate change' / 'Climate change is driven by greenhouse gases' example and report what the shipped measures return: static `ces` = 0.2857 (below FLAG), TS `calculateBeliefEquivalencyScore` = 0.25 ('related'); label it as the paraphrase case the lexical measure is known to miss, not as a high CES.
- Replace the spaCy sample with the real code paths, keeping the sample under a 'proposed, not shipped' label: spaCy is not a dependency; the nearest embedding analogue is src/core/ai/similarity.py (`StatementSimilarityEngine`, all-MiniLM-L6-v2, SIMILARITY_THRESHOLD default 0.75, sklearn cosine, simple clustering at the same threshold) and overlap_engine.py (semantic 0.85 + keyword boost up to 0.15; combined weights semantic 0.40 / taxonomy 0.25 / citation 0.15 / navigation 0.10 / graph 0.10; TopicRank 0.50/0.30/0.10/0.10 with 30-day recency half-life), both imported only by services.py, which does not parse.
- Keep the owner's `equivalency_score(...)` sample but mark every default against the code: `vca_multiplier=0.8` versus `w_ces = 0.0` (static) and `layer1 0.4 / layer2 0.6 / layer3 0.0` (TS); `user_score = average of votes, else 0` versus 'votes never enter a score' and an unargued equivalence reading UNARG = 0.50 on the static site (render_site.py:976) or a seed-typed `equivalencyScore` defaulting 0.0 and rendering blank on the Next.js belief page (SimilarBeliefsSection.tsx:17-20).
- Describe the Next.js engine as a second, product-side implementation with its own numbers: `mechanicalSimilarity` = Jaccard over tokens after lowercasing, stopword removal, 19 synonym groups canonicalized to the alphabetically smallest member (decrease/lower/reduce -> decrease; hike/increase/raise -> hike; ban/forbid/prohibit -> ban; tax/taxation/taxes -> tax; etc.) and negated-antonym folding (duplication-scoring.ts:99-121, 199-270); MECHANICAL_EQUIVALENCE_THRESHOLD = 0.85 short-circuits to 1.0; blend 0.4 x L1 + 0.6 x L2 with an unfilled `semanticScore` slot; bands identical >= 0.90, near-identical >= 0.70, overlapping >= 0.45, related >= 0.20, distinct below (all-scores.ts:699-724; route.ts:80-86); live at POST /api/beliefs/equivalency and in the calculator on /algorithms/belief-equivalency. Note that the explainer's own band table (~100 / ~70-90 / ~40-70 / <40) does not match the route.
- Describe the ingestion and community-vote path as the place where user judgement does act, and exactly how far: `textSimilarity = 0.7 x token Jaccard + 0.3 x bigram Jaccard` (src/lib/agent-ingest/similarity.ts:48-54); EQUIVALENCE_CANDIDATE_THRESHOLD 0.5 persists an `EquivalenceCandidate`, RESTATEMENT_SPEEDBUMP_THRESHOLD 0.8 requires acknowledging the existing row, SAME_CLAIM_THRESHOLD 0.95 proposes grouping; `GroupingVote` (weight default 1.0, never voter-supplied) resolves via `resolveConsensus` at CONSENSUS_THRESHOLD 0.6 and CONSENSUS_QUORUM 3 to 'grouped' or 'kept-separate'; the vote moves `status` and `consensus`, never `similarity` or a truth score.
- Mark the stored 17-step scorecard as a design the site does not run: `Equivalence = 0.40 x synonymConvergence + 0.40 x overlap + 0.20 x argumentBalance + networkAdjustment - totalPenalty`, `overlap = 0.25 subject + 0.35 predicate + 0.20 context + 0.20 mechanism`, verdicts merge >= 0.90, merge_with_note >= 0.75, link >= 0.50, else separate (src/app/api/equivalence/[slug]/score/route.ts:7-59; prisma/schema.prisma:1579-1670); every component field defaults 0 and is written by nothing but that route; docs/wiki/Template:-Belief-Equivalence-Scorecard.md:34-36 already says none of these weights feeds a published number.
- State the votes rule once, in the owner's words from the code: votes are one row per (page, on, login) in content/votes.csv, latest wins; shown on the heading line as 'N agree, M disagree; votes, not a score'; a duplicate submission at >= MERGE becomes the submitter's agree vote and adds no page (intake.py:657-672, test_intake.py:197-213); 'Votes are shown as what people think, next to what the analysis says, and never move a score.' (render_site.py:1595).
- Replace 'Links reasoning so evidence supporting one claim automatically supports others' with what happens: at >= EQUIV_MERGE (0.9) the shorter wording may stand in for the page in breadcrumbs and index columns (`brief()`, render_site.py:138-143) and the 'Ways of Saying the Same Thing' table ranks wordings by length with the verdict per row (render_site.py:1068-1085); no row, score or evidence crosses between the pages. Keep the original sentence labelled as a proposal pending the owner's answer.
- Add the uniqueness relationship the wiki implies but never states, because both engines disagree about it: static site DEFUNIQ = 1 'presumed distinct until a uniqueness page shows the overlap' (build_pages.py:55) and the method page's refusal to compute uniqueness as 1 - similarity (method.py:130, :200); TS `uniquenessFromSimilarities = 1 - max(similarity)` and `contributionFactor = 1 - similarity` (duplication-scoring.ts:355-376), consumed by `scoreArgument` only through the DB's `uniquenessScore ?? 1.0`; docs/ALGORITHMS.md section 10 (Jaccard > 0.80, TF-IDF cosine > 0.85, redundancy penalty 0.5 soft / 0.9 hard) is a third, unimplemented statement.
- Keep the 'Future Improvements' list but label each: ML refinement (nothing exists), VCA tracking (no VCA exists; the page kind would have to be added first), graph propagation (today the graph only prioritises flagged pairs by shared parent, similarity.py:241-255; the Prisma `NetworkPosition`/`networkAdjustment` design is dormant).
- Fix the formatting faults (four-backtick fences at lines 46 and 71, escaped underscores inside the formula fence), link the page to Redundancy-Problem, Template:-Belief-Equivalence-Scorecard, Truth-Score and method.html#equivalency, and settle the page name (with or without the trailing colon) so similarity.py:3 and the sidebar point at the same file.

### Confidence-Stability-Scores

The page proposes a Belief Stability Score (BSS), an Evidence Stability Score (ESS) and a Confidence Stability Score built from behavioural signals (votes and their ratios, duplicate submissions, nine-question evaluations, weekly visitors, Google PageRank, time spent, edit frequency) and the standard deviation of argument scores over time, with a Python sample that averages vote ratios and a scraper that reads upvote counts off HTML. It has not been edited since 2025-08-09; the repository copy at docs/wiki/Confidence-Stability-Scores.md is byte-identical and is the file tools/static-site/confidence.py cites as its source. Since then the code has split the idea in two: the published static site (tools/static-site/confidence.py, added 2026-09-20) implements "confidence" as a seven-component structural measure of how much work stands behind a page (weights 0.30/0.20/0.20/0.15/0.05/0.05/0.05) that multiplies every row's contribution to its parent, while the Next.js engine (src/core/scoring/all-scores.ts) carries a differently defined "Confidence Stability Score" = min(1, args/10) x (0.4 + 0.6 x \|pro - con\|/(pro + con)) that is stored and displayed but multiplies nothing. Every behavioural input the wiki names is deliberately weighted zero (confidence.py BEHAVIOURAL, the votes-never-scored rule, tests/unit/core/engagement-firewall.test.ts), "over time" is reported by changes.py and the BeliefScoreEvent ledger but never turned into a dispersion, and the Home page additionally links an "Impact Algorithm" (Argument Importance, Belief Impact Score) to this page that the page never discusses.

|---|---|---|---|

The static site replaces it with `Confidence` (confidence.py), a snapshot measure in [0,1] of how much work stands behind a page, computed from seven structural components and used as the `Conf` factor in `contribution = sign x (2 x Truth - 1) x Conf x Link x Imp x Uniq` (score_reference.py:172-174; Model.conf defaults to `lambda pid: 1.0` at line 137 and render_site.py:56-57 wires the real one). The only over-time readouts are changes.py `score_moves`, which rebuilds the previous git revision (`HEAD~1`) and reports `truth_before/after`, `conf_before/after`, `delta`, `conf_delta` and `crossed` for pages that moved more than 1e-9, and the Prisma `BeliefScoreEvent` ledger (scoreBefore/After, stabilityBefore/After per propagation). Neither computes consistency or dispersion. The Next.js engine's `calculateConfidenceStabilityScore` (all-scores.ts:509) also has no time dimension: it reads only pro strength, con strength and argument count. |

TS: `argFactor = min(1, argumentCount / 10)` with STABILITY_ARG_THRESHOLD = 10, over `args.length` (both sides together); the side split enters only through `dominanceRatio = \|proStrength - conStrength\| / (proStrength + conStrength)` where the strengths are sums of \|impactScore\| per side. The wiki's sample instead caps the pro/con reason-count ratio at 1; no engine uses a count ratio. |

The lists page says explicitly: 'Nobody's votes or views are counted here, so this says how much rests on a claim, not how many people like it.' |

The scope/magnitude/reversibility/urgency rubric (30/30/20/20) is 'guidance for humans, not an automated calculation'. |

Consequences are priced per row: the static site reads a typed `magnitude` (with optional `mag_low`/`mag_high`) and multiplies it by the truth score of the cost or benefit's own page (`ev = m x t`), sums benefits and costs, reports net, a low/high band (every benefit low, every cost high) and benefit-to-cost ratio, and withholds a net when more than one unit is priced ('mixed units'). The TS CBA computes `expectedValue = predictedImpact x activeLikelihood`, with likelihood from competing estimates and `depthAttenuation = 0.5^(d-1)`. Neither multiplies argument strength into impact beyond the likelihood/truth factor. The acronym BIS elsewhere in the wiki means Book Importance Score; the books feature's 'Belief Impact Weight (R0)' is a reach metric and unrelated. |

Unresolved sub-arguments: implemented through grounding (mean confidence of the pages argued beneath) and sensitivity's 'latent' list (inputs that could flip the conclusion once their work is done). Stability across repeated evaluations: not implemented, weight 0. 'Over time' is not measured. |

Hours are not the measure, structure is: 'Hours spent arguing are not recorded anywhere.' |

The static site's confidence multiplies every row and has seven weighted components; the TS stabilityScore is `argFactor x (0.4 + 0.6 x dominance)`, multiplies nothing, and no vitest pins its constants (all-scores.test.ts never mentions stability). The Next.js belief page also prints a per-row 'Confidence' column defined as 'how much of the work behind the argument's own page has been done' and a scorecard sentence 'sign x (2 x Truth - 1) x Confidence x Linkage x Importance x Uniqueness', but no TS code computes or multiplies a per-row confidence: `confidenceScore` is declared optional, never populated by fetch-belief.ts, and `ratioCell` renders it blank (Rule 6). There is no `/algorithms/confidence-stability` explainer; reason-rank lists 'Confidence Stability Scores' with no link and decision-leverage links the phrase to /algorithms/reason-rank. |

**Stale on the page:**

- Title mismatch: the page is titled 'Belief and Argument Stability Scores', its wiki slug is 'Confidence-Stability-Scores', Home.md links it twice under two different names ('Impact Algorithm: Argument Importance & Belief Impact Scores' at Home.md:30 and 'Belief Stability Confidence Score' at Home.md:53), the static site calls the quantity 'Confidence' (method.html#confidence) and the TS engine 'Confidence Stability Score'. tools/wiki_templates.py:107 lists the page as 'Confidence Stability Scores'.
- Three acronyms, none used by the code: BSS, ESS ('Evidence Stability Score' is introduced in Path Forward before it is defined and the intro's 'related measures' never says what they are), and 'Confidence Stability Score'. The code's names are `Confidence` / `conf` / `Conf` (static) and `stabilityScore` / `ConfidenceStabilityResult` (TS).
- Formatting fault: the first code block is closed with four backticks (line 95, '````') instead of three.
- The Python sample depends on nothing the project uses (`statistics`), divides by zero when a belief has no con sub-arguments (`sum(len(a['reasons']) for a in con_sub_args)` in the denominator of bss_weight), and prints 0.0 on its own example data because both stdevs are guarded to 0 with one sub-argument per side; its result grows with dispersion, the opposite of 'stability'.
- The scraper sample depends on `requests` and `bs4` (the project's only bs4 user is the unwired src/core/ai/scraper.py, whose sibling main.py does not parse), on CSS classes `upvote-count`, `downvote-count` and `reason` that exist in no template, and on `https://example.com/argument`. The project's data is two CSV tables in git, not HTML to be scraped.
- 'Google PageRank for related searches' refers to a public metric Google retired; the only PageRank in the project is the internal walk in tools/static-site/reasonrank.py (DAMPING 0.85).
- The page uses em dashes throughout (lines 11, 135 and others); the project's template checker (tools/check_templates.py) and the ISE page skill refuse em dashes, and the rewritten docs/wiki pages should follow the same convention.
- Vote vocabulary ('up/down vote counts and ratios', `up_votes`, `down_votes`, `upvotes`) is banned from src/core/scoring by tests/unit/core/engagement-firewall.test.ts; a rewrite that keeps the sample must label it as the wiki's proposal, not as the engine.
- The repository copy docs/wiki/Confidence-Stability-Scores.md (commit e85970b 'Media Skills') is byte-identical to the unedited wiki page, yet tools/static-site/confidence.py:3 cites it as the source of the implemented rule; readers following that citation find the behavioural proposal, not the structural rule.
- No outbound links on the page at all: it never links Truth Score, Evidence Verification Score, ReasonRank or Evidence to Conclusion Relevance Score, although the code's confidence reads all four (truth pages, ESIW tiers, the ReasonRank work queue, linkage pages in scrutiny and testability).
- The TS side has no explainer route for the score: src/app/algorithms/reason-rank/page.tsx:155 lists 'Confidence Stability Scores' with no href, src/app/algorithms/decision-leverage/page.tsx:290 links the phrase to /algorithms/reason-rank, and ScorecardSection.tsx:92 sets href: null. There is no /algorithms/confidence-stability page to link from a rewrite; use plain text or the static method page anchor.
- 'Percentile rank' (step 5) names a mechanism that exists nowhere; the code ranks ordinally ('rank N of M') and by share.
- The page's 'Path Forward' promises to add 'quality and credibility of sources' as a future factor; it has been the engine's starting-point rule since evidence.py landed, so the tense is wrong.

**What an updated page must say:**

- Lead with the one sentence that is true of the implemented rule: confidence is how much a page's score has earned the right to count, in [0,1], and every row contributes `sign x (2 x Truth - 1) x Confidence x Link x Imp x Uniq` to its parent (tools/static-site/score_reference.py:161-174; render_site.py:174-181; conformance.py item 3). State explicitly that it gates what a page passes upward and never caps the page's own truth score (confidence.py:11-13; method.py:100-101).
- Give the seven structural components with their real names, weights and formulas (confidence.py:30-38, 80-121): grounding 0.30 = max(own ESIW tier of the page's declared etype, mean confidence of the pages argued beneath it), recursive; two_sided 0.20 = 1.0 if both sides have argument or evidence rows, 0.35 if one side, 0.0 if none, not applicable to importance pages; scrutiny 0.20 = share of link/imp/uniq slots on argued rows and predictions (and bearing slots on interest listings) that name a page; breadth 0.15 = n/(n+6) over rows plus predictions; depth 0.05 = d/(d+3) over the longest argument chain below; sourcing 0.05 = mean over evidence rows of 0.5 x has-source-text + 0.5 x cited-page-classified, not applicable with no evidence rows; testability 0.05 = share of predictions that are dated and have a linkage page, not applicable with no predictions. `saturate(n, half) = n/(n+half)`. Components that do not apply are dropped and the rest renormalised: `confidence = sum(w x v) / sum(w)`.
- Give the bands: static `BANDS = {'established': 0.75, 'developing': 0.45, 'early': 0.15}`, below 0.15 'unstarted' (confidence.py:48, 152-155); TS status robust >= 0.75, established >= 0.50, developing >= 0.25, fragile below (all-scores.ts:527-531). Print both and name which engine each belongs to until the owner answers the harmonisation question.
- List the behavioural half exactly as the code does (confidence.py:39-40): 'up and down votes', 'weekly visitors', 'dwell time', 'edit frequency', 'duplicate submission attempts', 'evaluation responses', 'score variance over time', all at weight 0 and reported as 'no data' rather than scored 0, with the sentence 'Wire them up and they take weight from the structural components, which is the migration the wiki describes.' Mark each of the wiki's eight Factors as PROPOSED with its weight-zero status, and keep the nine evaluation questions as the owner's list, noting that 'true' and 'relevant' became argued pages (truth, linkage).
- State the votes rule in full: votes live in content/votes.csv, one row per (page key, on, GitHub login), latest wins; a relevance vote ('on' = the page a row sits on) is counted apart from a truth vote; counts are shown on the heading line after the score badge and on a home card that ranks the gap between vote share and truth score once three or more people have voted; a duplicate submission at or above MERGE becomes a vote for the existing claim (intake.py:10-19); no vote ever moves a score (intake.py:90; render_site.py:1595; test_render.py:2272 TestVotesAreShownAndNeverCounted; tests/unit/core/engagement-firewall.test.ts).
- Describe the second implemented quantity under its own heading, 'Confidence Stability Score (Next.js engine)': `argFactor = min(1, argumentCount / 10)` (STABILITY_ARG_THRESHOLD 10); `dominanceRatio = \|proStrength - conStrength\| / (proStrength + conStrength)`, 0 with no arguments; `stabilityScore = clamp01(argFactor x (0.4 + 0.6 x dominanceRatio))`, the 0.4 floor so a heavily argued balanced belief does not read 0 (all-scores.ts:486-540); inputs are sums of \|impactScore\| per side and args.length (all-scores.ts:870-879; fetch-belief.ts:437-439); stored as `Belief.stabilityScore Float @default(0.5)` (schema.prisma:193), recomputed by src/lib/propagate-belief-scores.ts and logged in `BeliefScoreEvent.stabilityBefore/After`; shown as the 'Confidence stability' scorecard row (ScorecardSection.tsx:92); it multiplies nothing and no test pins its constants. Say plainly that it is not the same quantity as the static site's confidence.
- Note the Next.js belief page's unfulfilled promise so a reader is not misled: types.ts:240-243 declares an optional per-row `confidenceScore` 'how much of the work behind the argument's own page has been done', ArgumentTreesSection.tsx:38 and 205-207 render it blank under Rule 6, and ScorecardSection.tsx:150-165 prints the static site's row formula with a Confidence factor that no TypeScript function computes or multiplies.
- Record the Protocol confidence interval and volatility as a third, separate mechanism that shares the word 'stability' (scoring-engine.ts:582-587, 611-623; ConfidenceMeter.tsx:12-20): `CI = clamp(0.02, 0.2, 0.15 x max(0.3, 1 - totalArgs x 0.03) x (1 - argBalance x 0.5))`; volatility high > 0.12, medium > 0.06, else low; status calibrated (>= 4 args and CI < 0.08), contested (>= 2 args and CI >= 0.08), else emerging; the meter labels these 'High/Moderate/Low Stability'. Also the likelihood CI (scoring-engine.ts:711-728): `min(0.3, max(0.02, stdDev(estimate probabilities) x max(0.5, 1 - totalArgs x 0.03)))`, 0.15 with one estimate; docs/ALGORITHMS.md:572 documents it. This is the only standard deviation in the codebase and it is across competing estimates, not over time.
- Replace 'over time' with what exists: changes.py `since(content_dir, corpus, rev='HEAD~1')` returns rows added/removed/changed column by column and `score_moves` (truth_before/after, delta, conf_before/after, conf_delta, crossed = sign change around 0.5; threshold 1e-9; sorted by \|delta\| then \|conf_delta\|), and returns None rather than a wrong page when git or the previous revision is unavailable or the previous revision contained a cycle the engine refuses. Mark the wiki's 'standard deviation of argument scores over time' as PROPOSED, NOT IMPLEMENTED in either engine.
- Describe the two sensitivity sweeps and name the gap as the value of doing the work (sensitivity.py:13-21, 170-225): every input to `DEPTH = 6` levels is pinned to 0 and 1 with confidence as is ('Moves it now') and again with `pinned_conf = 1.0` ('Moves it if settled'); flip point by bisection (FLIP 0.5, STEPS 16, EPS 1e-9); INERT 0.005; verdicts 'decides it alone', 'breaks it alone', 'carries it alone', 'moves it', 'worth settling', 'inert' (and 'moves it either way/down/up' on the neutral line); the joint move pushes the three widest to their worst extreme at once; test_settling_an_input_can_only_widen_what_it_can_do pins that confidence gates down, never up.
- Describe the work queue (reasonrank.py:39-44, 162-175; render_site.py:1161, 2520-2526): `work(page) = ReasonRank x (1 - confidence)`; ReasonRank is a damped walk (DAMPING 0.85, TOLERANCE 1e-12, ITERATIONS cap 2000) from the beliefs along `capacity = Link x Imp x Uniq` with truth and confidence deliberately absent from capacity (test_rank_ignores_confidence_entirely); beliefs are excluded from the queue by default. This is the implemented form of 'focus attention on unresolved issues', and the only PageRank in the project; mark 'Google PageRank for related searches' as SUPERSEDED by it.
- Describe Decision Leverage as the Next.js counterpart (decision-leverage.ts:12-55, 68-101): `weight = \|linkage\| x importance x uniqueness x 0.5^depth` (= d impact / d truth of the engine's `impact = sign x truth x \|linkage\| x importance x uniqueness x 100`), `openRange = 0.40 x (1 - grounding) + 0.25 x 1/sqrt(1 + linkageVotes) + 0.15 x [unscored] + 0.20 x examination`, `leverage = weight x openRange x 100`; classes crux / load-bearing / open-minor / settled-minor with LOAD_BEARING_WEIGHT 0.35, OPEN_RANGE_THRESHOLD 0.40, NEGLIGIBLE_LEVERAGE 0.5, FRAGILE_CONCENTRATION 0.5; `/leverage` and `/api/leverage`. Its own header distinguishes it from 'Confidence Stability (all-scores.ts)'.
- Keep the Home page's three bullets and label them: 'Argument Importance Score' SUPERSEDED on this page by the importance explainer (static: importance page = max over listed interests of Validity x Bears, DEFIMP 0.5, conformance constant; TS: `importance = clamp01((netScore + 100)/200)`, manual default 1.0 in scoring-engine.ts:439 and cba-scoring.ts:93, rubric 30/30/20/20 guidance only) and by sensitivity/leverage for 'how much a belief depends on an argument'; 'Belief Impact Score (BIS)' PARTIAL as the cost-benefit expected value (static: `ev = magnitude x truth of the row's page`, net, low/high band, benefit-to-cost ratio, 'mixed units' guard, render_site.py:249-299; TS: `predictedImpact x activeLikelihood`, `0.5^(d-1)` depth attenuation, cba-scoring.ts) with no score named BIS; 'depth of analysis / unresolved sub-arguments / repeated evaluations' as implemented / implemented / not implemented respectively.
- Label the Path Forward factors: 'quality and credibility of sources' IMPLEMENTED as the 18-row ESIW table (evidence.py:79-98, with `record` marked as an addition and the RCT 0.85 / meta 0.80 split noted) setting `p0 = 0.5 + 0.5 x ESIW x (2 x ERP/100 - 1)` and `w = k x 2n/(n+1)`, and as the own half of grounding; 'level of expert agreement' PARTIAL as ERP; 'degree of evidence scrutiny' PARTIAL as the scrutiny component and the leverage examination gap; 'number of citations' PARTIAL as ERQ/breadth, 'recency' NOT IMPLEMENTED.
- Keep the two Python samples verbatim, each under a heading that says PROPOSED (2025), NOT IMPLEMENTED, with a one-line note on what replaced each line: vote ratio -> page truth; stdev over sub-argument scores -> none; `bss_weight` ratio -> `two_sided` presence (1.0 / 0.35 / 0.0) and TS `dominanceRatio`; `ess_weight` -> none; the sum -> a bounded [0,1] confidence. Beside the scraper, say the engine reads content/pages.csv and content/edges.csv (plus topics.csv and votes.csv) and that src/core/ai/scraper.py is an unwired statement collector. Fix the four-backtick fence.
- Add a short 'Rules in this page the site does not run' list mirroring method.py:207 ('Behavioural confidence signals (votes, visitors, dwell time and four more)': weight zero; votes are shown and never scored), so the wiki and the method page say the same thing in the same words.
- Rename consistently: use 'Confidence' for the static quantity and 'Confidence Stability Score' only for the TS one, drop BSS and ESS as acronyms (keep them once, in a 'what this page used to call them' line), and reconcile the title with the slug. Avoid em dashes. Link only to targets that exist: the static site's method.html#confidence, #sensitivity and #reasonrank anchors and the Next.js /algorithms/importance-score, /algorithms/decision-leverage and /leverage routes; there is no /algorithms/confidence-stability.
- Cite the pins so a future rewriter can check drift: conformance.py item 2 (seven components, renormalised), test_engines.py test_confidence_does_not_depend_on_which_page_is_asked_first (cycle-truncated results are never cached), test_an_importance_page_can_reach_full_scrutiny, test_settling_an_input_can_only_widen_what_it_can_do, test_rank_ignores_confidence_entirely; and note that no vitest pins the TS stabilityScore constants (0.4, 0.6, 10, 0.75/0.50/0.25).

### Media

The page argues for linking the most influential books, films, podcasts and studies to the beliefs they support or oppose, then specifies how to score media: an eight-factor product (Sales x Engagement x UGC x Citation x Viewership x Validity x Linkage x Unique) for sorting, a Book Accuracy Score BAS = sum(BLS x BTS x BUS x BIS) with sample Python, and a Book Aesthetic Quality Score (BAQS). The wiki copy was last edited 2025-08-09; the identical text sits at docs/wiki/Media.md and _archive/wiki-updates/Media.md (last repo touch 2026-03-27, e85970b). The code has moved far past it: the static site (tools/static-site, last media change 2026-10-04 6d0df52) gives every work its own page with argued Quality and Influence scores, reads a work's truth from the belief pages that cite it as sum(carried truth x Bears)/sum(Bears), scores a work's row on a belief page as (2 x Quality - 1) x Bears x Influence x Importance and shows it without counting it; the Next app (src/core/scoring/media-truth.ts, 2026-08-03 49f76f2) computes a signed Media Truth Score = sum(signed truth x centrality)/sum(centrality) from an extracted-claim ledger, falls back to a genre prior, and ranks works by Epistemic Impact = truth x reach. Neither engine lets sales, engagement, user ratings, citation counts or viewership touch a number; no BAS, BLS, BTS, BUS, BIS or BAQS exists under any name, and the one module that computes log10(sales + citations + shares) (src/features/books) is @ts-nocheck, has no Prisma model behind it, and would throw if its route were hit.

**Stale on the page:**

- Fenced code blocks are malformed: the fences at lines 37-41 and 68-72 wrap the formula in blank padding lines, and the closing fences at line 83 and line 116 are four backticks (````) against three-backtick openers, so the BTS block and the calculate_BAS block render with a stray fence.
- Em dashes throughout (lines 4, 31-32, 44-51, 73-76, 152); the project's template check (tools/check_templates.py) refuses em dashes in reader-facing templates and the rewrite should drop them.
- No external links on the page (none to chatgpt.com or sites.google.com), and no links at all to the mechanisms it describes: the live explainers are /algorithms/media-truth-score, /media, /media/why-pro-con-media (Next) and method.html#media, lists.html#works, media.html (static site), and the template is templates/media-analysis-template.html (wiki twin docs/wiki/Template:-Media-Analysis.md).
- Names the code never uses: 'Impact Score' (for sorting media), 'Sales Data Score', 'Engagement Score', 'UGC Score', 'Citation Score', 'Viewership Score', 'Validity Score', 'Unique Score', 'Book Accuracy Score (BAS)', 'BLS', 'BTS', 'BUS', 'BIS', 'Book Aesthetic Quality Score (BAQS)', 'Logical Soundness Score', 'Verifiability Score'. The code's names are Quality, Influence (static 'impact' section), Bears / Centrality / linkageScore, Importance (imp), Reach, carried truth, Accuracy of what it backs (static), Media Truth Score (signed, Next), Epistemic Impact, genre prior, Media Genre Score.
- 'our forum' and 'users' with credibility weights: there are no accounts or forum. The static site takes rows through prefilled GitHub issue forms (contribute.js, intake.py); the Next app has no media submission at all.
- 'Laws' listed as a media type: the code files laws under a separate Legal Framework section (ise_tables.py:41 law_for/law_against; prisma LegalEntry 1487-1500), never as media.
- Examples Freakonomics and An Inconvenient Truth exist in no corpus; the static site's 177 media pages are studies, reports and books on government-ethics and related beliefs, and the Next seed is the UBI reading list. An Inconvenient Truth survives only as an illustrative +0.70 row in the explainer.
- The Python samples depend on a per-argument dict with pro_score/con_score keys that nothing produces, implement unbounded sums the engine refuses (method.py:90), and ignore the five constants (K = 1, UNARG = 0.5, DEFLINK = 1, DEFIMP = 0.5, DEFUNIQ = 1) and evidence tiers the reference scorer reads (score_reference.py:34; conformance.py:16).
- Code-side remnant of this page: src/features/books (book-scoring.ts, book-service.ts, logic-battlegrounds.ts, types.ts) is the only implementation of a popularity-weighted book score (log10(sales + citations + shares), fixed quality weights 0.2/0.3/0.2/0.15/0.15, AI 0.3 + Crowd 0.5 + Expert 0.2 validity) and is dead: book-service.ts is @ts-nocheck ('Book models depend on a schema that is not active'), prisma/schema.prisma has no Book or Author model, and /books/[id] (src/app/books/[id]/page.tsx) and /api/books would throw on prisma.book; /books/[id] also links 'Back to all books' to /books, a route that does not exist (Rule 5).
- Two meanings of 'Media Truth Score' in the Next app: MediaResource.truthScore is a stored 0-1 genre-derived number (schema 1377-1380) averaged onto the belief scorecard (ScorecardSection.tsx:93), while src/core/scoring/media-truth.ts is the signed -1..+1 claim-based score shown on /media; the explainer and template declare the latter canonical.
- Template line 190 points reasoning quality at 'Media Logical Validity Score, on the wiki; it has no route here yet', but no such wiki page exists among the thirteen and nothing computes it.
- Static-site MediaResource section on a belief page says 'Each work has its own page where its quality and its influence are argued separately' and the Next MediaResource.howItArgues / directnessOfAdvocacy (default 50) fields have no static-site counterpart; a rewrite listing fields should say which engine has which.

### Automated-Conflict-Resolution-Platform

The page is the platform overview: a Wikipedia-inspired dispute-resolution site that applies four Getting to Yes principles, "collective intelligence" and PageRank to arguments, with an eight-step NLP pipeline for separating people from the problem and a seven-step interest-analysis process ending in IACI and IVS scores. It was last edited 2025-08-09; the copies at docs/wiki/Automated-Conflict-Resolution-Platform.md and _archive/wiki-updates/Automated-Conflict-Resolution-Platform.md are byte-identical to it, so nothing in the repo has rewritten it yet. Against the code the four conflict-resolution principles are genuinely implemented as scored page sections (Objective Criteria with a criterion page per yardstick; Interests, Not Positions with Score = Validity x Drives and a computed primary conflict pair; Shared interests / Best compromise / topic Common Ground; People on the Record carrying "history, not weight"), and ReasonRank exists twice (a damped walk with d = 0.85 in tools/static-site/reasonrank.py and ProRank/(ProRank+ConRank) in src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts). The eight-step NLP pipeline is almost entirely absent, and two of its steps (sentiment/word-pattern flagging, hiding author identity) are refused or reversed by design: tools/static-site/integrity.py says why pattern matching is not done, and intake.py stamps every contributed row with the contributor's GitHub login, which the page then renders. IACI does not exist under any name; IVS survives as the interest page's own truth score (Validity) in the static site and a typed 0-100 validityScore with a Resolution Floor of 70 in the Next app.

| Promise on the page | Status | Where | What the code does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Intro: "Lets anyone participate in conflict resolution." | Implemented | tools/static-site/render_site.py:1613-1664 (FORMS: 'interest', 'shared_interest', 'compromise', 'person', 'engagement', 'common', 'criterion'); tools/static-site/render_site.py:906,912,915,918,931,934 (add_form after every Interests sub-table); tools/static-site/intake.py:15-24, 218-227; tools/static-site/contribute.js; .github/workflows/intake.yml | There is no server. Every table on a belief or topic page ends in a form that is a plain GET to a prefilled GitHub issue form; intake.py reads the issue, checks it against data/claims_index.json, and either records a vote in content/votes.csv or opens a pull request adding the row. Participation therefore requires a GitHub account, and a maintainer merges the PR; the wiki's "anyone" is anyone with a GitHub login. |
| Intro: "Ranks solutions based on evidence, not popularity." | Implemented | tools/static-site/score_reference.py:10-26, 33; tools/static-site/evidence.py; tools/static-site/intake.py:17-19; tools/static-site/test_render.py:2272-2370 (TestVotesAreShownAndNeverCounted); tools/static-site/render_site.py:2185-2192 | A row contributes sign x (2 x Truth - 1) x Confidence x Link x Imp x Uniq; page truth = (POS + w x p0) / (POS + NEG + w), where p0 and w come from the evidence a page declares (etype, erq, erp) and default to p0 = 0.5, w = K = 1. Constants: CONSTS = {'K': 1, 'UNARG': 0.5, 'DEFLINK': 1, 'DEFIMP': 0.5, 'DEFUNIQ': 1}. Votes exist (content/votes.csv, currently 2 rows) but "A vote never moves a score"; the heading line prints "N agree, M disagree; votes, not a score" and the test suite asserts every truth score is identical with and without the votes file. test_engines.py::test_argument_alone_can_never_leave_the_neutral_point pins that only evidence can move a score off 0.50. |
| Intro: "Updates conclusions dynamically when new evidence appears." / How It Works: "When a belief changes, all dependent conclusions are automatically recalculated based on the latest and strongest evidence." | Implemented | tools/static-site/score_reference.py:10-26 (Model computes every score from pages and edges alone, no stored scores); tools/static-site/render_site.py:1128-1170 (engine_table: "Every value here is computed from the tables above at build time"); .github/workflows/pages.yml:3-4 (on: push); tools/static-site/changes.py; src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:608-611 (recalculateProtocolBelief) | Nothing is stored: truth is recursive, min(argued truth, weakest load-bearing component that has its own page), so changing any leaf changes every page above it on the next build. "Dynamically" in practice means on every push to master (pages.yml) and after intake lands a vote; changes.py records what moved since the last revision. The Next app recomputes on mutation via recalculateProtocolBelief. No live, in-browser recalculation exists. |
| Intro: "inspired by Wikipedia's collaborative editing model" | Replaced by another mechanism | tools/static-site/intake.py:1-30; tools/static-site/README.md:61, 240; tools/static-site/render_site.py:1905-1925 (claims index for duplicate check) | There is no wiki-style edit button. Contributions are GitHub issues turned into votes or pull requests by intake.py, with a duplicate check (contribute.js against data/claims_index.json) that converts a reworded claim into a vote for the one already there. The content is two flat CSV tables (content/pages.csv 4,263 pages, content/edges.csv 9,472 rows, content/topics.csv 95 topics) under git review, not a live-edited wiki. |
| How It Works: applies "Conflict resolution principles from Getting to Yes (Harvard Negotiation Project)" | Implemented | tools/static-site/render_site.py:807-822 (Objective Criteria), 882-940 (Interests, Not Positions), 985-988 (People on the Record), 2245-2259 (Common Ground and Compromise); src/core/scoring/conflict-resolution.ts:21-23 ("Follows Fisher & Ury"); src/app/algorithms/objective-criteria/page.tsx:458 ("It Enables 'Getting to Yes'") | All four principles have a named section on the built belief page and the topic page, each with computed numbers (see the per-principle claims below). The Next app's conflict-resolution.ts cites Fisher & Ury in its header and derives shared interests, primary conflict pair, value conflicts and compromise candidates from scored rows. |
| How It Works: "Collective intelligence methods (not AI hype)." | Implemented | tools/static-site/similarity.py:20-30 ("this has no model and no network"); tools/static-site/integrity.py:1-27; CLAUDE.md (src/core/ai section: "A number a model asserted is the one thing the published engine refuses to carry"); src/core/ai/scorer.py, overlap_engine.py:14-24, similarity.py:3,20-21 (unwired) | The published engine computes nothing with a model: every number is argued on a page or is a labelled constant. The repo does carry src/core/ai/, an LLM page-generation framework whose scorer.py asks a model for an importance score out of 100 and whose overlap_engine.py and similarity.py use sentence-transformers (all-MiniLM-L6-v2, SIMILARITY_THRESHOLD 0.75); nothing imports the scoring half, two of its files (main.py, services.py) do not parse, and CLAUDE.md records that wiring any of it to a page would need a page arguing it like every other factor. |
| How It Works: "Google's PageRank algorithm, adapted to evaluate arguments and solutions." | Implemented | tools/static-site/reasonrank.py:1-49 (DAMPING = 0.85, capacity(row) = Link x Imp x Uniq, walk restarts at the beliefs); tools/static-site/test_engines.py:298-301; src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:4-53 (d = 0.85), 557 (ProRank / (ProRank + ConRank)); tools/static-site/render_site.py:1163-1164 ("Relied on (ReasonRank)" and "Work value" = rank x (1 - confidence)) | Two different things carry the name. In the static site ReasonRank is a PageRank walk: start at the beliefs, step along rows in proportion to Link x Imp x Uniq (truth and confidence deliberately excluded), damping 0.85, mass at a page with no rows returns to the beliefs; it ranks how much of the corpus depends on a page and never changes a truth score. In src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts ReasonRank is the recursive score itself, ProRank/(ProRank+ConRank) with d = 0.85, and there a listed-but-unargued reason raises the score; the static engine's signed rule means it does not. CLAUDE.md lists that disagreement as open decision 1. |
| Why It Matters: the platform flips "Emotion over reason / Engagement over accuracy / Echo chambers" into "Structure > chaos / Evidence > emotion / Shared truth > tribal loyalty" | Implemented | docs/BELIEF_PAGE_RULES.md:161-167 (Rule 7 symmetry); tools/static-site/render_site.py:2231-2234 (engagement "never feeds the three-part address"); tools/static-site/test_render.py (TestThePagesSpeakPlainly) | Rhetorical, but the mechanisms it points at exist: every two-sided section mirrors Supporters and Opponents (Rule 7), engagement level is "a fact about the person, not the belief" and feeds no score, and votes are shown but not counted. No engagement metric, like count or view count feeds any number. |
| Conflict Resolution Methods Implemented, item 1: "Separate the people from the problem" | Partly implemented | tools/static-site/ise_tables.py:8-11 (pages are claims; no author column on pages or edges); tools/static-site/render_site.py:985-988 (People on the Record: "Who holds a belief never changes its score. These names carry history, not weight."); prisma/schema.prisma:343-362 (PersonOnRecord: "author identity is orthogonal to the final score"); docs/BELIEF_PAGE_RULES.md:466-474; tools/static-site/render_site.py:337-350 (provenance_line renders "added by @login"); src/features/belief-analysis/components/ArgumentTreesSection.tsx:142-170 (~quoteAuthor), 44-60 (AgentWorkTrace) | Structurally achieved: a page is a claim, a row is a claim-to-claim edge, and no scoring formula has an author term; the `who` column on an edge is "who gains or pays: an interest", not an author. People appear only in the explicit People on the Record section (ise_tables.py SECTIONS 'person' -> people_for/people_against), typed and unscored. But identity is not stripped: intake.py:218-227 stamps every contributed row with `added_by` = GitHub login and `added` = date, provenance_line renders "Added by @login on date" linking to github.com/login under the row, and the Next app argument cell prints ~quoteAuthor and a "Show the work" agent trace. The code's rule is "shown, never scored", not "hidden". |
| Conflict Resolution Methods Implemented, item 2: "Insist on using objective criteria" (link to sites.google.com) | Implemented | tools/static-site/render_site.py:807-822 (belief page Objective Criteria), 2281-2296 (topic page Best Objective Criteria); tools/static-site/ise_tables.py:48 ('criterion'), 69 ('criteria' topic section), 88 (EXTRA criterion: method, validity, reliability, linkage, importance, strengthen, weaken, latest); src/core/scoring/all-scores.ts:419-451, 459; prisma/schema.prisma:792-822; src/features/belief-analysis/components/ObjectiveCriteriaSection.tsx:473-477, 510-518 | Static site: each criterion is a claim with its own page; Score = that page's truth; columns are Proposed criterion / Score / Validity / Reliability / Linkage / Importance / Latest reading; the four labels are "typed by the author, not computed, and nothing reads them" (only a Low validity or Low linkage label greys the row, render_site.py:814); Latest reading prints "not yet read" until a sourced number is typed; the How-it-is-measured, strengthen and weaken readings print under the row. Next app: calculateObjectiveCriteriaScore averages FOUR 0-1 scores, validity + reliability + independence + linkage, each Prisma default 0.5, totalScore default 0 (renders blank per Rule 6); labels excellent >= 0.80, good >= 0.60, moderate >= 0.40, weak > 0, invalid = 0; belief aggregate = sum(totalScore x linkageScore) / sum(linkageScore). So the two implementations disagree on the factor set (static: validity, reliability, linkage, importance, typed; Next: validity, reliability, independence, linkage, averaged) and on whether the labels are read at all. The wiki link points at a Google Sites page, not at either. |
| Conflict Resolution Methods Implemented, item 3: "Focus on interests, not positions" | Implemented | tools/static-site/render_site.py:882-909 (section, int_table, primary conflict pair); tools/static-site/ise_tables.py:35 ('interest': int_sup/int_opp), 36 ('interest_listing'); tools/static-site/score_reference.py:22, 191 (importance = max Validity x Bears); tools/static-site/render_site.py:1270-1272 (driver page check table), 1258-1262 (interest page check table); docs/BELIEF_PAGE_RULES.md:414-427 | Section header: "Positions are what people say they want; interests are why." Each interest is a need with a subject ("<who> need <what>"), is its own page, and is argued two ways. Validity = the interest page's truth by the page-truth rule, settled by the mirror test on its own page, "never by the power of who holds it". Drives = the driver page's truth (a page of kind 'driver': "The interest X is what actually drives support for / opposition to the belief Y"), reading UNARG 0.5 when no driver page exists ("No driver page yet, reads 0.5"). Row Score = Validity x Drives. Primary conflict pair is computed, never typed: the highest Validity x Drives on each side, printed with both factors and the share a/(a+b) of paired weight on the supporting side. An interest page is complete only with >= 1 reason each way and both if_true/if_false readings filled (render_site.py:311). |
| Conflict Resolution Methods Implemented, item 4: "Invent options for mutual gain" (link to sites.google.com) | Partly implemented | tools/static-site/render_site.py:910-915 (Shared interests, Best compromise), 245-247 (ease = max(compromise truth) x (1 - mean obstacle truth)), 2245-2259 (topic Common Ground and Compromise: "nothing on this site yet tests which disagreements a small change in likelihood would flip"); tools/static-site/ise_tables.py:37, 68, 87; src/core/scoring/conflict-resolution.ts:31-37, 70-100, 114-117, 165-185, 230-275; src/features/belief-analysis/lib/interests.ts:16-19 | Static site: Shared interests (text + "A direction both could take") and Best compromise (text + "Rests on" premise + "Why difficult") are typed rows with vote links, each a page scored by the page-truth rule; the only derived number is s['ease'] = max compromise truth x (1 - mean obstacle truth), surfaced on lists. The topic page's three columns (Shared Interests / Real Value Conflicts / Proposed Compromises) pool the topic's own `common` rows with the belief pages' shared, dispute-'Values' and compromise rows, and the caption admits no likelihood-flip test runs. Next app (unwired to the static site): conflict-resolution.ts derives shared interests by cross-side mechanicalSimilarity >= SHARED_INTEREST_SIMILARITY = 0.5 with both validities >= RESOLUTION_FLOOR = 70, pairedValidity = 2·sv·ov/(sv+ov); primary conflict pair by driveScore = linkageAccuracy x validity / 100 (validity defaulting to 50 when null); value conflicts where \|supporterRank - opponentRank\| >= minGap 2; and compromise candidates = cost/benefit items whose category net flips with a likelihood shift <= ACHIEVABLE_SHIFT = 0.15 (requiredShift = \|net\| / \|magnitude\|). Nothing is invented by the software; it ranks what people typed. |
| Separate People / Why: "Strips out personalities and focuses only on claims and evidence." | Partly implemented | tools/static-site/ise_tables.py:8-11, 27-52; tools/static-site/render_site.py:337-350, 985-988; tools/static-site/intake.py:218-227 | Claims and evidence are the only scored objects, and no person is a scoring input anywhere. Personalities are not stripped from the page, though: contributed rows carry and display their GitHub author, and People on the Record lists named holders of each side by design ("history, not weight"). |
| Separate People / Why: "Uses argument analysis algorithms (NLP, logic scoring, machine learning) to break debates into components." | Partly implemented | tools/static-site/render_site.py:860-880 (Logical Anatomy; "Which components are load-bearing is typed by the author, not argued; the wiki's Necessity Score is not computed"), 1014-1024 (Structural Checks); tools/static-site/integrity.py:1-45; tools/static-site/ise_tables.py:34 ('component'), 86 (EXTRA component: type, stated, lb, assumes) | Decomposition into components is done by hand: a component row has a typed type, stated flag, load-bearing flag (lb) and assumption. Logic scoring exists as the recursive truth rule and as graph-shape checks only (integrity.py CHECKS: circular support, assumes its own conclusion, same page listed twice, rows that may be the same point, rests only on authority, evidence filed above its premise, and three more), none of which reads the prose or moves a score. No NLP or machine learning runs anywhere in the published engine; the only text processing is similarity.py's lexical duplicate detector. |
| Separate People / Why: "Shows the math behind conclusions so bias and hidden agendas are exposed." | Implemented | tools/static-site/render_site.py:751 ("every column is here so the row can be multiplied out and checked"), 1128-1170 (Scoring Engine table: Quantity / Value / How, plus per-page JSON link and the constants), 1120-1126 (typed_inputs lists what an author typed); tools/static-site/method.py; tools/static-site/build_pages.py:32-40 (every explainer link resolves to method.html#...) | Each belief page ends with a Scoring Engine section listing Argument score, Evidence score, Prediction contribution, Belief score, Positive/Negative totals, Starting point and its weight (k x bounded replication reward, capped at REP_CAP x k), Truth score argued and capped, Relied on (ReasonRank) with rank and damping, Work value = rank x (1 - confidence), Complete, and a citation naming the revision; every number is also exported as <key>.json. Hidden agendas specifically are addressed by the Advertised versus actual motivations table (render_site.py:916-918) and the Drives score, both typed/argued rather than detected. |
| Separate People / How, step 1: "Preprocessing: remove noise (emojis, URLs, etc.)" | Not implemented | none found in the published engine; nearest: src/core/ai/similarity.py:37-43 (normalize_text strips whitespace, URLs and non-word characters; unwired); tools/static-site/similarity.py (content words minus STOP list, IDF-weighted) | The content is typed one-sentence rows in CSV, so there is no raw social-media text to clean.
