| Separate People / How, step 2: "Topic Modeling: group arguments by issue, not person" (sample: LatentDirichletAllocation, n_components=5) | Replaced by another mechanism | tools/static-site/ise_tables.py:12-16, 73 (TOPIC_COLS: key, name, parent, definition, scope, axis); tools/static-site/content/topics.csv (95 topics); tools/static-site/similarity.py:1-30; tools/static-site/render_site.py:1972-1975 (topic page filled from typed rows or the beliefs beneath, "never invented") | Grouping by issue is a hand-filed table: every belief names its topic in the `topic` column and inherits it to every page beneath; topics have parents, so the hierarchy is typed, not discovered. The computed part is the duplicate detector (similarity.py): Jaccard over IDF-weighted content words averaged with cosine over IDF-weighted character 4-grams, used to flag rows that may be the same point and to turn a reworded submission into a vote. No LDA, no CountVectorizer, no fixed topic count anywhere in the repo; the unwired src/core/ai/overlap_engine.py uses sentence-transformer embeddings (all-MiniLM-L6-v2, threshold 0.75) for topic overlap. |
| Separate People / How, step 3: "Sentiment Analysis: flag emotional attacks" (sample: TextBlob polarity, drop comments with sentiment <= -0.5) | Refused by design | tools/static-site/integrity.py:3-12; tools/static-site/render_site.py:1019-1024; docs/BELIEF_PAGE_RULES.md:509-511 ("fallacy pattern-matching is not done"); src/app/algorithms/objective-criteria/page.tsx:70, 428, 516 (Twitter sentiment as the example of an invalid criterion) | integrity.py: "a pattern matcher looking for 'attacking the person instead of the argument' would fire on the central legitimate argument on every page. No TextBlob, no polarity, no filtering of rows by tone exists; the only 'sentiment' in the codebase is the explainer's example of a 0% validity criterion. |

The attribution question ("is this really why the side holds its position") is answered by a point number: Drives (a driver page's truth, 0-1, default UNARG 0.5) in the static site, linkageAccuracy (0-100, typed) in the Next app, plus a typed linkageConfidence string and a typed 0-1 confidence on ranking rows. confidence.py's number is about the page's own completeness and feeds the row formula as Conf. |

Static site: the interest page's truth (0-1), computed by the page-truth rule from its agree/disagree reasons, read identically on every page that lists the interest. Next app: a typed validityScore 0-100 per InterestEntry, gated by RESOLUTION_FLOOR = 70 for shared interests and solutions (a satisfied interest below the floor earns nothing, interests.ts:83-87), combined into pairedValidity = 2·sv·ov/(sv+ov) for shared pairs. A row's importance is the MAX of Validity x Bears over the interests its importance page lists (default 0.5 when none), and the importance then multiplies the row's contribution rather than being averaged across rows. Interests are identified by rows on the importance page (interest_listing), one page per interest, not by an attribute on the argument. The outline itself would not run as written: `a.importance` is attribute access on a dict. |

No party, person or group identity enters any formula. Whether policies are then chosen that way is outside the software; the pages are an input to a decision, not a decision procedure. |

**Stale on the page:**

- Line 35: "Insist on using objective criteria" links to https://sites.google.com/d/1f62q1ZSjo3Kcb7Pj5OHCNTEfaacTWuKD/p/1QKE1_z4d58ZPVSvfHYdpkiPszBPG8Ukz/edit, a Google Sites editor URL; the explanation now lives on the built site's method.html#formula and on every belief page's Objective Criteria section (tools/static-site/render_site.py:807-822), and in the Next app at /algorithms/objective-criteria.
- Line 37: "Invent options for mutual gain" links to https://sites.google.com/d/1f62q1ZSjo3Kcb7Pj5OHCNTEfaacTWuKD/p/1tS20NXnxbYfYHfhoNZSAnStyGW4La__U/edit, same problem; the mechanism is Shared interests / Best compromise on belief pages and Common Ground and Compromise on topic pages (render_site.py:910-915, 2245-2259) and src/core/scoring/conflict-resolution.ts.
- Line 98: the Python code fence opens with three backticks (line 64) and closes with four (````), which renders a stray backtick in some Markdown engines.
- Lines 64-97: the example imports spacy, sklearn (CountVectorizer, LatentDirichletAllocation) and textblob; none of these is a dependency of the project (tools/static-site/requirements.txt, pipeline/requirements.txt, package.json), and the published engine refuses word-pattern scoring (tools/static-site/integrity.py:3-12).
- Lines 130-146: the code outline accesses `a.importance` and `a.interest` on plain dicts, which raises AttributeError; and the mean-of-importance it sketches is not the rule the code uses (importance = max over listed interests of Validity x Bears, score_reference.py:22).
- Line 125: "Interest Attribution Confidence Intervals (IACI)" and "Interest Validity Scores (IVS)" are names no code uses; the code's names are Validity, Drives (static site), Linkage Accuracy / validityScore / Resolution Floor (Next app).
- Lines 52-59: "Preprocessing", "Topic Modeling", "Sentiment Analysis", "Entity Recognition", "Anonymization", "Continuous Learning" and line 45's "NLP, logic scoring, machine learning" name mechanisms with no implementation; two (sentiment flagging, anonymization) are reversed by design.
- Line 13: "Collective intelligence methods (not AI hype)" sits beside an unwired LLM framework in src/core/ai/ whose scorer.py asks a model for a number; the page should say the published engine refuses model-asserted numbers and why.
- Line 1: "inspired by Wikipedia's collaborative editing model": contribution is by GitHub issue form and pull request (intake.py), not live editing; worth restating accurately.
- Line 14: a single "PageRank" claim hides that the repo has two ReasonRanks (a damped walk that never moves a truth score in tools/static-site/reasonrank.py; the recursive ProRank/(ProRank+ConRank) score in src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts) which disagree about whether unargued volume counts (CLAUDE.md open decision 1).
- The in-page anchors #separate-people-from-the-problem and #focus-on-interests-not-positions resolve correctly to the two H2 headings; no fix needed there.

**What an updated page must say:**

- Keep the four Getting to Yes principles as the spine, each with its real section name and location: 'Separate the people from the problem' -> pages are claims (tools/static-site/ise_tables.py:8-11), People on the Record carries 'history, not weight' (render_site.py:985-988; prisma PersonOnRecord 343-362); 'Insist on objective criteria' -> Objective Criteria (render_site.py:807-822) and topic 'Best Objective Criteria for This Topic' (2281-2296); 'Focus on interests, not positions' -> 'Interests, Not Positions' (882-940); 'Invent options for mutual gain' -> Shared interests, Best compromise (910-915) and topic 'Common Ground and Compromise' (2245-2259). Replace the two sites.google.com links with these.
- State the row formula and constants exactly: contribution = sign x (2 x Truth - 1) x Confidence x Link x Imp x Uniq; page truth = (POS + w x p0) / (POS + NEG + w) with p0 = 0.5, w = K = 1 unless the page declares evidence (etype, erq, erp); CONSTS K = 1, UNARG = 0.5, DEFLINK = 1, DEFIMP = 0.5, DEFUNIQ = 1 (score_reference.py:10-33). Say that a vote never moves a score (intake.py:17-19; test_render.py:2272).
- Interests section, as implemented: each interest is a need with a subject ('<who> need <what>'), its own page, argued two ways; Validity = that page's truth via the mirror test, never by power; Drives = the driver page's truth ('is this need really why the side holds its position, settled by the side's record'), reading 0.5 with no driver page; Score = Validity x Drives; the Primary conflict pair is computed as the highest Validity x Drives per side with the share a/(a+b) printed (render_site.py:884-909). The wiki's 'Linkage Accuracy' is Drives; its 'Interest Validity Score' is Validity. Mark IACI as not implemented and name the point scores that stand in for it.
- Importance, replacing the code outline's mean: an argument row's importance page lists up to five interests, each with a bearing page; Importance = max over listed interests of Validity x Bears, Bears = 1 and Importance = 0.5 when nothing is listed (score_reference.py:22; render_site.py:1227-1236, 1254-1257). Keep the owner's outline in a collapsed block labelled 'original sketch; the engine uses max, not mean'.
- Objective criteria, both surfaces: static site, Score = the criterion page's truth; Validity / Reliability / Linkage / Importance are typed labels nothing reads, Latest reading blank until sourced (render_site.py:810-821). Next app, totalScore = mean of validityScore, reliabilityScore, independenceScore, linkageScore (defaults 0.5, total default 0 renders blank), labels at 0.80 / 0.60 / 0.40 / >0 / 0, aggregate = sum(total x linkage) / sum(linkage) (all-scores.ts:419-459). Label the second as the Next app's rule and note the factor-set difference as an open question.
- Mutual gain, both surfaces: static site, typed Shared interests (with 'A direction both could take') and Best compromise ('Rests on' / 'Why difficult'), ease = max compromise truth x (1 - mean obstacle truth) (render_site.py:245-247), and the topic page's three-column pool; Next app conflict-resolution.ts constants RESOLUTION_FLOOR = 70, SHARED_INTEREST_SIMILARITY = 0.5, ACHIEVABLE_SHIFT = 0.15, pairedValidity = 2sv·ov/(sv+ov), driveScore = linkage x validity / 100 (validity default 50), value conflicts at gap >= 2, compromise candidates where \|net\| / \|magnitude\| <= 0.15. Mark the Next pipeline as unwired to the published site and quote the static caption that admits the gap.
- ReasonRank: say there are two. The static site's is a PageRank walk from the beliefs with DAMPING = 0.85 and capacity(row) = Link x Imp x Uniq, excluding truth and confidence on purpose, producing 'Relied on' and 'Work value' = rank x (1 - confidence) (reasonrank.py:1-49; render_site.py:1163-1164); it never changes a truth score. The Next engine's is the recursive ProRank / (ProRank + ConRank) with d = 0.85 (scoring-engine.ts:4-53, 557), where unargued volume counts; cite CLAUDE.md open decision 1.
- Rewrite the eight-step 'How' under Separate People as a status table, keeping every step the owner wrote: Preprocessing (not implemented; content is typed one-sentence rows), Topic Modeling (superseded by the hand-filed topics table content/topics.csv and the lexical duplicate detector similarity.py: IDF-weighted Jaccard over content words averaged with cosine over character 4-grams), Sentiment Analysis (refused by design, quote integrity.py:3-12), Entity Recognition (not implemented; People on the Record is the deliberate home for names), Argument Consistency Tracking (Next app typed Cross-Context Consistency Check table only, ValuesSection.tsx:328-366; nothing computed), Anonymization (reversed: provenance shown, never scored, intake.py:21-24), Ranking (implemented, Rule 8), Continuous Learning (refused: constants and tiers pinned in conformance.py; changes are reviewed diffs). Keep the Python snippet in its details block, fix the four-backtick fence, and label it 'illustrative; no library here is used'.
- Rewrite the seven-step 'How' under Focus on Interests likewise: steps 1, 2, 4, 6 implemented (cite ise_tables.py:35-36, 46; render_site.py:1615-1626, 894-904, 1227-1236, 2885-2937); step 3 partial (argued two ways, completeness ranking via publish.CORE_BAR, no reward system); step 5 partial (typed maslowPrior in the Next app only, InterestValiditySection.tsx:231; none in the static site); step 7 split into IACI (not implemented) and IVS (superseded by Validity; Next app validityScore 0-100 with Resolution Floor 70).
- Add what the page omits that the code has and that serves the same purpose: the computed kind of dispute (Factual / Linkage / Values conflict / Mixed, 0.1 margin, value-rank gap scaled by 3; render_site.py:236-244), the Advertised versus actual motivations and Obstacles and Cognitive biases tables (all two-sided, Rule 7), the Engagement Landscape on topic pages (levels 1 Preference, 2 Active Advocacy, 3 Principled Non-Compliance, 4 Civil Disobedience; 'a fact about the person, not the belief', feeds no score; render_site.py:1959-1960, 2230-2243), the Core Values Conflict topic table (advertised vs critics, 2210-2228), and the Scoring Engine readout that is the page's 'shows the math' (1128-1170).
- Correct the participation model: contributions are GitHub issue forms under every table and a propose-a-belief form, turned by intake.py into a vote (duplicate claim) or a pull request; votes are shown on the heading line as 'N agree, M disagree; votes, not a score'. 'Anyone' means anyone with a GitHub login; a maintainer merges.
- Say plainly that 'collective intelligence, not AI hype' is enforced: the published engine has no model and no network (similarity.py:20-30); src/core/ai/ exists as an unwired LLM page-generation framework whose numbers the engine refuses to carry (CLAUDE.md), and two of its files do not parse (open decision 5).
- Preserve the page's two anchors (#separate-people-from-the-problem, #focus-on-interests-not-positions), its 'Why It Matters' contrast list and 'The Goal' section verbatim, adding to The Goal that every belief in the tables is published however incomplete, drafts marked, and that there is no verdict module: a page prints its numbers and does not say what to conclude.

### Electronic-Democracy

The page is a 2025-08-09 manifesto for a "political party" that would run policy debate on a Wikipedia-style pro/con platform: two columns of reasons, a per-argument score plus linkage and unique scores, media works scored as wholes by direction/validity/influence, and a cost-benefit analysis across seven dimensions (Financial, Time, Freedom, Security, Autonomy, Risk, Health). The in-repo copies (`_archive/wiki-updates/Electronic-Democracy.md` and `docs/wiki/Electronic-Democracy.md`, both from commit e85970b of 2026-03-27) are byte-identical to the wiki, so no rewrite exists yet. The scoring mechanics it sketches are all implemented, mostly in `tools/static-site/` (signed five-factor rows, linkage/importance/uniqueness pages, media quality and influence pages, a priced cost-benefit section with typed units and ranges), with a second, differently-ruled CBA engine in `src/core/scoring/cba-scoring.ts`; the seven dimensions survive nowhere as a list (the static site types free-text units per row, the TypeScript CBA has four canonical categories, and Security, Autonomy and Risk appear as categories in no file), and the political program (party, candidates, pledges, representatives, voting reform) has no code at all, while the one democratic mechanism that does exist, votes, is deliberately barred from moving any score.

| Promise on the page | Status | Where | What the code does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Aims 1-3 and 'How It Works': each policy proposal has two columns, Reasons to Agree and Reasons to Disagree; reasons are ranked by evidence strength and performance; conclusions are scored from the performance of their supporting reasons. | Implemented | tools/static-site/ise_tables.py:28-29 ('argument' agree/disagree); tools/static-site/score_reference.py:7-13, 34, 152-158, 160-173, 181-210; tools/static-site/render_site.py:751 (Argument Trees section, rows ranked by score); src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts:415-510, 649-672; src/core/scoring/all-scores.ts (calculateTruthScoreBreakdown) | Static site: every row contributes sign x (2 x Truth - 1) x Confidence x Link x Imp x Uniq (score_reference.py:172-174) with constants K=1, UNARG=0.5, DEFLINK=1, DEFIMP=0.5, DEFUNIQ=1 (line 34); page truth = (POS + w x p0)/(POS + NEG + w) (line 152-155) where p0 and w come from evidence.py, then capped by the weakest load-bearing component (line 209); belief score = POS - NEG. A row nobody has argued sits at 0.5 and contributes exactly 0, so volume cannot pad a score. TypeScript product engine (scoring-engine.ts:415-510): rawImpact = ReasonRank x linkage x importance x uniqueness with importance and uniqueness defaulting to 1.0 (lines 439-440), DAMPING_FACTOR 0.85 (line 58), and likelihood = pro/(pro+con) clamped to [0.01, 0.99] (lines 649-672), so an unargued listed reason does raise the score there. CLAUDE.md records this volume disagreement as unresolved. Tables are score-ranked with top rows shown (Rule 8, src/features/belief-analysis/lib/ranking.ts). |

### Evidence‐based-policy

The page is a 65-line prospectus: it says the Idea Stock Exchange serves evidence-based policy through five contributions (a structured database linking conclusions to assumptions, a scoring system driven by argument strength, linkage and uniqueness, automatic recalculation, a hub for sharing evidence and challenging assumptions, and public transparency) and then restates them. It carries no formula, table, constant or link, so the audit is of promises rather than numbers; it has not been touched since 2025-08-09, and the repo's own docs/wiki copy under the same filename (committed 2026-03-27) is an entirely different text with a Technical Requirement Specification that the GitHub wiki never had. Against tools/static-site/ the five contributions are substantially built, but each with a shape the page does not describe: the "database" is two flat tables (pages, edges) plus topics and votes, exported as JSON, XML, SQL and SQLite with no stored scores; "automatic updates" are a pure function recomputed at every CI build rather than anything dynamic; uniqueness is a presumed-1 multiplier flagged by a lexical detector and never computed; and the page omits three factors the engine multiplies in (importance, confidence, the evidence prior) while promising one thing the engine deliberately refuses (keeping outdated data out of scores by any mechanism other than arguing it false). Votes exist and are shown but never move a score, by documented design.

| Promise on the page | Status | Where | What the code does |
|---|---|---|---|

'Dynamic' is the one word that does not fit: there is no runtime, no server and no stored score; the site is rebuilt by .github/workflows/pages.yml on every push to master (lines 59-69, 133-141) and changes.html says what moved. The corpus today is 4,263 pages (196 beliefs, 3,385 claims, 407 interests, 177 media, 50 importance, 34 linkage, 9 driver, 3 equivalence, 2 uniqueness) and 9,472 edges. |

'Assumptions' live in two sections: `component` (SECTIONS at line 34, extras `type, stated, lb, assumes` at line 91) and `assumption` (SPLIT at 52-55: assume_accept/assume_reject on a belief page). The relation 'conclusion reads claim' is every edge whose claim/link/imp/uniq/drives/equiv/who/bearing column names a page; render_site.Corpus builds the reverse index `uses` and forward index `reads` at lines 60-67 and the page shows 'Where This Page Is Used' (1097-1112). export_db.SCHEMA declares `page` and `edge` with FOREIGN KEY references and the `page_uses` view (160-170). (1) Completeness first: a belief meeting PUBLISH.CORE_BAR (reasons both ways, evidence, objective criteria, predictions or falsifiability test, costs and benefits, interests; publish.py:13-21) is 'complete' and comes first in every list (render_site.py Corpus.complete 113-116, best_beliefs 2466). (2) Confidence gates what a page passes upward, weighted grounding 0.30, two_sided 0.20, scrutiny 0.20, breadth 0.15, depth 0.05, sourcing 0.05, testability 0.05, with inapplicable components dropped and weights renormalised (confidence.py:218-226, 307-309); bands established >= 0.75, developing >= 0.45, early >= 0.15 (line 236). (3) ReasonRank is a damped walk (DAMPING 0.85, TOLERANCE 1e-12, reasonrank.py:50-52) from the beliefs stepping in proportion to Link x Imp x Uniq, and work value = rank x (1 - confidence). 'Strong assumptions' bite through the load-bearing cap: a weak necessary premise holds the conclusion down (score_reference.py:209), which is what keeps the corpus's beliefs at 0.50 (CLAUDE.md open decision 6). |
| 1.2: 'Each conclusion's score is determined by: Strength of supporting/opposing arguments' | Implemented | tools/static-site/score_reference.py:152-155, 175-179, 194-210; tools/static-site/evidence.py:140-151; tools/static-site/conformance.py:228-240 | Two numbers, not one. Truth (0..1) = (POS + w x p0) / (POS + NEG + w), where POS and NEG are the positive and negative row contributions as magnitudes over the argument, evidence and prediction rows together, and a row lands on the side its sign puts it on (score_reference.py:175-179, 206-207). Belief score = POS - NEG, open-ended (line 210). 'Strength' is signed: a reason argued false counts AGAINST the side it was filed on, and a reason nobody has argued (truth 0.5, confidence 0) contributes exactly 0, so volume cannot pad a score (score_reference.py:163-166; test_engines.py:43 pins that an unsourced chain of 2, 5 or 40 pages reads exactly 0.5 at every page). p0 and w come from evidence.py:146-148: p0 = 0.5 + 0.5 x ESIW x (2 x ERP/100 - 1), w = k x 2 x ERQ/(ERQ+1), k = 1, REP_CAP = 2.0. Note that src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts scores A/(A+D) over argument strengths, where listing an unargued reason does raise the score; the two engines disagree and CLAUDE.md records it as unresolved. |
| 1.2: 'Linkage scores (relevance between evidence and conclusion)' | Implemented | tools/static-site/score_reference.py:34, 172-174; tools/static-site/export_db.py:85-99; tools/static-site/build_pages.py:53; tools/static-site/render_site.py:1733-1745 | Each row may name a linkage page (`link_id`), a page of kind 'linkage' whose question is rendered 'If it were true that X, it would significantly strengthen/weaken the conclusion that Y' (export_db.py:85-92) and whose truth score, argued like any other page, is the Link factor. Default when none exists: DEFLINK = 1, 'a reason placed under a conclusion is presumed relevant and the burden is on the challenger' (build_pages.py:53). The wiki ties linkage to evidence only; the code applies it identically to arguments, evidence and predictions (score_reference.py:166, 'One formula for arguments, evidence and predictions alike'). Readers can also vote yes/no on whether a claim bears on a page (relevance_links, render_site.py:1733-1745; votes.csv `on` column), shown and never scored. 34 linkage pages exist for 2,599 scored rows. |
| 1.2: 'Uniqueness scores (avoiding double-counting similar arguments)' | Partly implemented | tools/static-site/score_reference.py:34, 174; tools/static-site/similarity.py:1-60; tools/static-site/integrity.py:37, 169-173; tools/static-site/method.py:128-130, 200 | The Uniq multiplier exists (`uniq_id`, DEFUNIQ = 1) but is never computed: a row is presumed distinct until someone opens a uniqueness page and argues the overlap, and only 2 uniqueness pages exist in a 4,263-page corpus. What is computed is a lexical similarity, similarity.py: the average of IDF-weighted Jaccard over content words and IDF-weighted cosine over character 4-grams (NGRAM = 4), flagged at FLAG = 0.45 and treated as near-identical at MERGE = 0.70 (lines 46-51). It changes no score: 'w_ces is read from a VCA page when one exists and is 0 when none does' (similarity.py:44-48; method.py:130 'It changes no score and cannot'). It feeds the structural check 'Rows that may be the same point twice' (integrity.py:37, 169-173) and the duplicate check on the contribution forms. method.py:200 lists the wiki's 'Uniqueness computed as one minus the highest similarity' under rules the site does not run. So the double-counting defence the page promises is a reading queue plus a presumption of 1, not a discount. |
| 1.2: the score is determined by those three things (the list is exhaustive as written) | Partly implemented | tools/static-site/score_reference.py:160-174; tools/static-site/evidence.py:140-151; tools/static-site/confidence.py:195-201 | The code multiplies in three factors the page never names. Importance: Imp is the truth of the row's importance page or DEFIMP = 0.5 (score_reference.py:173; an importance page reads max over listed interests of Validity x Bears, lines 190-193). Confidence: each row is gated by the confidence of the claim it points at, 0 when the claim has no page (score_reference.py:172; confidence.py:195-201). The evidence prior: a page's starting point p0 and weight w from etype/erq/erp (evidence.py:146-148), the only thing in the engine that can move a score off 0.50. A rewrite that keeps the page's three-item list as the determinants of a score misdescribes the engine. |
| 1.2: 'Ensures that well-supported policies gain visibility and credibility' | Implemented | tools/static-site/render_site.py:2466-2495, 2664-2760, 1532-1535; tools/static-site/publish.py:13-27 | best.html ranks complete beliefs first then by truth (render_best, 2490-2495, whose caption explains why a well-argued belief can still read 0.50); the home page is cards of ranked lists (render_index 2664-2760) and every page heading carries a score badge (score_badge 1532). 'Credibility' maps to confidence, a separate number printed beside truth and in the citation line. Drafts are published too, marked 'draft' with what they still need (render_site.py:656-657; data/drafts.json), by the owner's standing rule that nothing drafted is deleted. |
| 1.3: 'Scores dynamically recalculate when related assumptions are strengthened or weakened' | Replaced by another mechanism | tools/static-site/render_site.py:3507-3580; tools/static-site/export_db.py:26; .github/workflows/pages.yml:59-69, 86-88, 133-141; tools/static-site/changes.py:144-190; tools/static-site/sensitivity.py:1-45 | Replaced by a pure function of the tables, recomputed in full on every build. No score is stored anywhere ('Scores are never stored: they are computed from these tables', export_db.py:26); Model.evaluate is memoised per build (score_reference.py:184). Recalculation happens when CI runs pages.yml on a push to master (or when intake.py starts it after a vote lands, intake.py:535-553), not when a reader acts. changes.py then rebuilds the previous revision from `git show HEAD~1:content/*.csv` and subtracts (score_moves, lines 144-174), reporting each page's truth_before, truth_after, delta, conf_delta and whether it `crossed` 0.5; CI checks out with fetch-depth 2 so there is something to compare (pages.yml:86-88). sensitivity.py answers the 'what if an assumption moved' question ahead of time: each input beneath a page is pinned at 0 and 1 to DEPTH = 6 levels, the flip point found by bisection in STEPS = 16, inputs moving less than INERT = 0.005 marked inert. |
| 1.3: 'Keeps results aligned with current, most reliable evidence' | Partly implemented | tools/static-site/evidence.py:79-109, 140-151; tools/static-site/confidence.py:283-300; tools/static-site/method.py:82-96 | 'Most reliable' is implemented as the evidence tier: ESIW weights statistics 0.90, record 0.90 (added beyond the wiki's sixteen), rct 0.85, meta 0.80, observational 0.75, historical 0.70, expert_data 0.65, expert_claim 0.60, anecdote 0.55, logic 0.50, analogy 0.45, norm 0.40, intuition 0.35, news 0.30, survey 0.25, eyewitness 0.20, visual 0.15, artifact 0.10, NOSOURCE 0.0 (evidence.py:79-99). Replication ERQ sets the weight 2 x ERQ/(ERQ+1), capped at REP_CAP = 2.0 x k; agreement ERP (clamped 0..100) sets direction. Confidence's grounding component reads the tier too (confidence.py:290). 'Current' has no implementation: nothing in the engine reads a date. The `checked` date intake writes into a row's extra (ise_tables.py:94) and the interest page's `latest` column are displayed only. In the corpus 243 of 4,263 pages declare an etype, 56 an erq and 50 an erp. |
| 1.3: 'Prevents outdated data from influencing decision-making' | Refused by design | tools/static-site/method.py:202; tools/static-site/score_reference.py:160-166; tools/static-site/evidence.py:120-137 | The site runs no recency, decay or lifecycle factor. method.py:202 lists the wiki's lifecycle multipliers ('active 1.0, weakened 0.7, conditional 0.8, outdated 0.3, refuted 0.1') under 'Rules in the wiki this site does not run' with the replacement: 'None. A row argued false scores below zero through 2 x Truth - 1.' The only way an outdated finding stops influencing a conclusion is for its own page to be argued down, or for its erp (share of replications agreeing) to be lowered so p0 falls below 0.5 (evidence.py:146). Also deliberately absent: a model-asserted or scraped staleness number, since every number on the site must link to a page that argues it (integrity.py:9-11). |
| 1.4: 'A hub for fact-driven debate' | Implemented | tools/static-site/render_site.py:1586-1600, 1757-1834; tools/static-site/contribute.js:1-10; .github/workflows/intake.yml:1-56 | There is no server (render_site.py:1586-1590). Every table on every page carries a form (add_form, 1757-1818) that is a plain GET to https://github.com/myklob/ideastockexchange/issues/new prefilled from contribute.yml or belief.yml; take_part (1826-1834) explains the account requirement and the 'Copy as text' path for readers without one. The intake Action (intake.yml, on issues opened/edited with the labels contribution/belief/vote or the matching headings) runs intake.py, which parses the form back, re-checks for duplicates with the Python similarity engine, and records a vote or opens a pull request; check.yml runs the full test suite, integrity and conformance on that PR before merge. |
| 1.4: policymakers, researchers and the public can 'Share evidence' | Partly implemented | .github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/contribute.yml:117-136; tools/static-site/intake.py:60-66, 219-238; tools/static-site/ise_tables.py:94 | The `evidence` section is one of 35 options on the contribution form; a submission carries Source, URL and Date checked, and intake.py stamps the row with url, checked, added_by and added in its extra cell (PROVENANCE, ise_tables.py:94) so the exports carry them and the page shows them. What the form cannot do is set the evidence tier: etype, erq and erp are page columns, and intake.py contains no handling for them (grep of intake.py finds no etype/erq/erp), so a finding submitted through the site starts at p0 = 0.50 with weight k and contributes nothing until an author classifies it in the tables. The wiki's own EVS (ESIW x ECRS x ERQ x ERP/100) is computed by evidence.evs() and printed in every Evidence Ledger, read by no score (evidence.py:154-158; method.py:95-96). |
| 1.4: participants can 'Challenge assumptions' | Partly implemented | .github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/contribute.yml:69-70; tools/static-site/render_site.py:868-881, 1619, 1733-1756; tools/static-site/intake.py:316-345 | Rows can be added to the `component` and `assumption` sections by form (contribute.yml options; forms_grid(... ('component', 'assumption')) at render_site.py:880), and a reader can vote yes/no on whether a claim bears on a page (relevance_links 1733-1745) or agree/disagree with any claim. But the thing that makes an assumption count, the `lb: Y` flag, is typed by the author and not settable by the form (intake.py has no lb handling), and assumption rows carry no score. Challenging an assumption with effect therefore means adding a reason to disagree on the assumption's own page, which moves its truth and, if it is load-bearing, caps the conclusion. |
| 1.4: 'Collaboratively refine policy proposals' | Implemented | tools/static-site/intake.py:596-713; tools/static-site/contribute.js:220-256; .github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/belief.yml; .github/workflows/check.yml | A proposed belief (belief.yml: The belief, Topic, A reason to agree, A reason to disagree, Source) becomes new rows via rows_for and a PR (intake.py:374-420, 672-713). At or above MERGE = 0.70 similarity the submission is counted as the submitter's vote for the existing claim and, if that claim is not yet a row on the target page, a PR adds just that row (intake.py:660-670, place_existing 426-440); between FLAG = 0.45 and MERGE the rows are added and the near matches listed for the maintainer (intake.py:697-699). The browser does the same check as the reader types against data/claims_index.json, drafts included (contribute.js:164-178; render_site.py claims_index 1903-1928). Adding rows that would create a circular argument is refused (intake.py:676-678, cycles_in_tables). |

NO_MOVE in intake.py:90 and VOTE_NOTE in render_site.py:1595 say so on every issue comment and page; test_render.py test_no_vote_moves_any_score (2373) asserts truth and belief score are identical with and without votes.csv; the method page's first limitation (method.py:231) repeats it. Votes are kept in content/votes.csv (one row per key, on, login, latest wins; today 2 rows), shown on the heading line after the score badge, and ranked against the truth score on a home card only once MIN_VOTERS = 3 people have voted (render_site.py:1880-1900). The wiki's behavioural confidence signals (votes, visitors, dwell time, and four more) carry weight 0 (confidence.py:227-228). The stated reason: 'a claim nobody has argued is worth exactly nothing, however many people like it' (render_site.py:1588-1590). |

Each page's computed numbers go out as p/<key>.json (truth, confidence, starts_at, start_weight, rests_on, reasonrank, work_value, checks, sensitivity, cite; page_json 3334-3380) indexed at data/pages_index.json, plus data/claims_index.json and data/drafts.json. The conformance corpus and expected numbers are copied to data/conformance_corpus.json and data/conformance_expected.json (3568-3573). The source tables live in the public repository as content/pages.csv, edges.csv, topics.csv and votes.csv. test_render.py TestEveryExportCarriesTheSameTwoTables (482) compares the shapes row by row. |
| 1.5: 'Transparent methodology allows scrutiny, replication, and validation' | Implemented | tools/static-site/method.py:26-262; tools/static-site/conformance.py:204-254, 392-429; tools/static-site/render_site.py:1033-1051, 1128-1171, 3470-3505; tools/static-site/test_render.py:446 | Scrutiny: method.html is generated from the live modules (method.py:32 'cannot describe a rule the site is not using'), prints the one formula with each factor's default, the evidence tiers with each tier's confirmed/contradicted starting points, the confidence weights, the sensitivity constants, the ReasonRank damping and residual, the structural checks with counts, a table of wiki rules the site does not run (lines 189-210), the five constants, and a limitations section. Every belief page carries a Scoring Engine table (engine_table, render_site.py:1128-1171) with Argument score, Evidence score, Prediction contribution, Belief score, Positive/Negative total, Starting point, Weight of the starting point (k x bounded replication reward, capped at 2.0 x k), Truth argued, Truth, ReasonRank, Work value, each with a 'How' cell, plus the typed inputs it reads but does not compute. Replication: 'Cite this page' prints claim, page key, truth, confidence and 'revision <short sha> of <commit date>' from git (cite 1033-1051; provenance 3470-3482 via rev-parse --short HEAD and log --format=%cs, with a dirty-tree warning in stamp 3491-3505); a build without git says 'revision unidentified' rather than dropping the line. Validation: conformance.py states the contract in eight numbered rules, ships 25 pages and 25 edges exercising each, compares at TOL = 1e-9, and cross-checks the corpus's constants and tiers against score_reference.CONSTS and evidence.ESIW (392-429); CI runs it before publishing (pages.yml:132) and test_render TestThePublishedNumbersAreReproducible (446) holds published precision to the engine's tolerance. |
| 1.5: 'Builds trust in the policy-making process' | Partly implemented | tools/static-site/method.py:229-243 | Not a mechanism, and the code refuses to assert it. The method page instead prints 'What this cannot do' (method.py:229-243): votes never scored; evidence classifications are a judgement call that moves a claim 'from 0.65 to 0.95'; one-at-a-time sensitivity misses correlated failure; the duplicate detector reads words not meaning; some inputs are typed (magnitudes, tiers, value rankings, criterion labels, load-bearing flag, topic-axis position); the bottom line is typed; the sweep stops at DEPTH levels; the revision page compares against the previous revision only; 'A truth score is not a probability'; 'The corpus is small' with live counts. A rewrite should ground 'trust' in that section rather than assert it. |
| 2: 'Decision quality – grounded in measurable facts' | Partly implemented | tools/static-site/evidence.py:6-19, 146-150; tools/static-site/test_engines.py:43-52; tools/static-site/integrity.py:38, 175-195; tools/static-site/render_site.py:182-330 (stats: cba) | Grounding is the only way a score moves: without a declared etype every page in a finite argument graph reads exactly 0.50 (evidence.py:6-11; pinned by test_argument_alone_can_never_leave_the_neutral_point). In this corpus 243 of 4,263 pages declare a tier, and the structural check 'Evidence filed above the premise it bears on' (integrity.py:175-195) names beliefs that cite findings yet read 0.50 because a load-bearing premise beneath them cites nothing (CLAUDE.md open decision 2). Costs and benefits are 'measurable' only as typed point estimates: expected value = magnitude x the row's truth, nets added within one unit only, bands from mag_low/mag_high (render_site.py:252-300); 0 of 218 cba rows state a range today, and the page and the check 'Costs and benefits given as single figures' say so. |

Trust is asked for by showing the disagreement between people and analysis rather than by merging them. |

When git or the previous revision is unavailable the page is omitted rather than published wrong (changes.py:15-17, 177-190). The limit the method page states: it compares against the previous revision only, so 'a number that drifts across twenty revisions drifts invisibly' (method.py:238). 'Less partisan' has one concrete implementation: interests are filed by the group that holds them and the interests page groups stakes by party (stake_groups/party_of, render_site.py:2848-2970, tested by TestTheInterestsAreFiledByParty), and every belief page's interest section applies 'the same suspicion to both sides' (line 884) with symmetric Supporters/Opponents tables (docs/BELIEF_PAGE_RULES.md rule 7). 'More informed' and 'better aligned' are outcomes nothing measures; the method page says explicitly 'Nothing here has been checked against outcomes' (method.py:240). |

**Stale on the page:**

- Formatting: the page ends with a stray closing code fence (``` on the final line) with no opening fence, so the whole page renders as prose followed by an orphan fence on GitHub.
- Two different pages share this filename. The GitHub wiki text (2025-08-09, the one audited, identical to _archive/wiki-updates/Evidence‐based-policy.md) is the five-point summary; docs/wiki/Evidence‐based-policy.md in the repository (committed 2026-03-27, 'Media Skills') is an unrelated 'Evidence Scoring Engine' text with a Technical Requirement Specification promising 'Evidence Impact = Quality Score x Linkage Score', PostgreSQL or Neo4j, UUIDs per belief, an NLP parser that suggests unstated assumptions, ReasonRank as a user-credibility score, and 'real-time recursive updates'. The sidebar (docs/wiki/_Sidebar.md:14) links [[Evidence‐based policy]] and so points at whichever copy is deployed. Several TRS claims contradict the code: ReasonRank is a page's share of a damped walk from the beliefs, not a user score (reasonrank.py:1-15); there is no NLP module and a model-asserted number is refused (CLAUDE.md on src/core/ai/); page ids are integer tab numbers plus text keys, not UUIDs (ise_tables.py:81-83); there is a SQL schema but no running database.
- Terminology the code no longer uses: 'database' (the code is two flat tables, pages and edges, plus topics and votes, exported to SQL/SQLite); 'conclusions and assumptions' (the code says beliefs, claims, components with a load-bearing flag, and assumption rows); 'score' in the singular (the code publishes truth 0..1, belief score POS - NEG, confidence, ReasonRank and work value as separate numbers); 'dynamic recalculation' (the code recomputes at build).
- The page names 'Linkage scores', 'Uniqueness scores' and 'scoring system' without linking the wiki pages that define them (Evidence-to-Conclusion-Relevance-Score, Equivalency-Score, Truth-Score, ReasonRank), and no page in the wiki's Home.md links to this one; it is reachable only from the sidebar.
- The filename uses a Unicode hyphen (U+2010) in 'Evidence‐based' while every other wiki filename uses ASCII hyphens; any link written with an ASCII hyphen misses the page.
- The page omits the factors the code multiplies in since the wiki was last touched: importance (DEFIMP = 0.5), confidence, and the evidence prior (etype/erq/erp), as well as the load-bearing cap; a reader of the page cannot reconstruct why every belief in the corpus reads 0.50.
- No code samples, libraries or external links to check: the page carries none (nothing to chatgpt.com or sites.google.com).

**What an updated page must say:**

- Keep the five-part structure (database, scoring, updates, discussion, transparency) and the section-2 and section-3 lists; under each item state what runs, with the real name and location, and mark proposals as proposals. Remove the orphan closing code fence.
- 1.1 Structured database: say it is two flat tables, `pages` (PAGE_COLS, ise_tables.py:74-76) and `edges` (EDGE_COLS, 78-79), authored in ISE_Data_Entry.xlsx or content/pages.csv and content/edges.csv, with `topics` and `votes` beside them; one claim per page, one row per edge; relations are the claim/link/imp/uniq/drives/equiv/who/bearing columns; assumptions are `component` rows (extras type, stated, lb, assumes) and `assumption` rows (Required to accept / Required to reject). Exported as data/ise.json, ise.xml, schema.sql, ise_data.sql and ise.sqlite with the views page_start, page_coverage, page_one_sided, page_inert, evidence_ledger, page_orphan, page_uses. Scores are never stored.
- 1.1 Quantifying the links: give the one formula, contribution = sign x (2 x Truth - 1) x Confidence x Link x Imp x Uniq (score_reference.py:160-174), with the five labelled constants K = 1, UNARG = 0.5, DEFLINK = 1, DEFIMP = 0.5, DEFUNIQ = 1 and what each presumes (build_pages.py:51-55). State that the link to an assumption is quantified only through the typed load-bearing flag and the cap truth = min(argued, weakest lb component with a page), that assumption lists are unscored, and that the Necessity Score is proposed, not computed.
- 1.1 Prioritising: name the three rankings: the publish bar (publish.CORE_BAR: reasons both ways, evidence, objective criteria, predictions or falsifiability test, costs and benefits, interests; complete beliefs first, drafts published and marked), confidence (grounding 0.30, two_sided 0.20, scrutiny 0.20, breadth 0.15, depth 0.05, sourcing 0.05, testability 0.05; bands 0.75/0.45/0.15; behavioural signals weight 0), and ReasonRank (damped walk from the beliefs, d = 0.85, capacity Link x Imp x Uniq, work value = rank x (1 - confidence)).
- 1.2 Scoring: replace the three-item list with the full set of determinants: POS and NEG as signed row contributions over arguments, evidence and predictions; truth = (POS + w x p0) / (POS + NEG + w); belief score = POS - NEG; p0 = 0.5 + 0.5 x ESIW x (2 x ERP/100 - 1) and w = k x 2 x ERQ/(ERQ + 1), capped at 2k; the 18-row ESIW table (statistics 0.90, record 0.90 added, rct 0.85, meta 0.80, observational 0.75, historical 0.70, expert_data 0.65, expert_claim 0.60, anecdote 0.55, logic 0.50, analogy 0.45, norm 0.40, intuition 0.35, news 0.30, survey 0.25, eyewitness 0.20, visual 0.15, artifact 0.10); the load-bearing cap; importance pages as max over listed interests of Validity x Bears; linkage pages as argued pages with the burden on the challenger. State the fixed point: without a declared evidence type every page of an argument graph reads exactly 0.50 (test_engines.py:43). Keep 'linkage' and 'uniqueness' as named, with uniqueness marked: multiplier argued per page, default 1; computed similarity (FLAG 0.45, MERGE 0.70, IDF-weighted Jaccard + char 4-gram cosine) flags and never scores; the wiki's 1 - max similarity rule is not run. Note that src/core/scoring/scoring-engine.ts uses a different rule and that the disagreement is open.
- 1.3 Automatic updates: rewrite as 'recomputed from the tables on every build': every number is a pure function of pages and edges, CI (pages.yml) rebuilds on each push to master and after a recorded vote, changes.html reports rows edited and pages whose truth or confidence moved (changes.py: truth_before, truth_after, delta, conf_delta, crossed), compared to the previous revision only (fetch-depth 2). Add the sensitivity sweep as the forward-looking half: each input beneath a page pinned at 0 and 1, DEPTH = 6, flip at 0.5 by bisection to 16 steps, INERT = 0.005, a second sweep with confidence held at 1. Mark 'prevents outdated data' as not run: no recency or lifecycle factor; the wiki's lifecycle multipliers (1.0/0.7/0.8/0.3/0.1) are listed on the method page as not run; a finding is retired by argument or by lowering its erp; `checked` and `latest` are display fields.
- 1.4 Discussion: describe the real path: no server; forms under every table are GETs to prefilled GitHub issue forms (contribute.yml with 35 sections, belief.yml, vote.yml); contribute.js checks what is typed against data/claims_index.json (drafts included) so a duplicate becomes a vote; intake.py re-checks with similarity.py, counts a vote at >= 0.70, lists near matches between 0.45 and 0.70, refuses a circular argument, opens a PR; check.yml runs the suite on the PR; 'Copy as text' for readers without an account. State plainly that a vote never moves a score (votes.csv, shown on the heading line, gap card at 3 or more voters) and why. State that the forms carry Source, URL and Date checked but cannot set etype/erq/erp or the load-bearing flag, so a submitted finding starts at 0.50 until an author classifies it.
